# Tolk for Statamic — Implementation Tasks

> **Read this first.** This file is the canonical task list and design reference for building the `reachweb/statamic-tolk` Statamic 6 addon. Work through the phases in order. Each task has a checkbox: flip `[ ]` → `[x]` when it is complete, and append one line to the Done log at the bottom. Every task ends with a **Self-check** that must pass before you move on.
>
> The verified facts behind this plan are in `docs/research/` (six reports, about 5,300 lines, written 2026-10-01). Tasks cite them as `R1`–`R6` plus a section number. When a task says "verify in vendor", read the installed source before writing code: the research was done against Statamic 6.19.0 and `laravel/ai` 1.0.1, and both move quickly. `vendor/statamic/cms` no longer ships `resources/js`, only built assets (T2.2), so for the CP front end (R3's `[SRC]` paths) read core's source on GitHub at the installed tag.
>
> **Decisions confirmed.** The owner answered T0.1 on 2026-10-02. Every row of the Decisions table is now either confirmed or rewritten to match the owner's answer. Where a row says "planner's details", the owner confirmed the decision and the planner designed the mechanics. Change those details only for a reason, and record the reason in the done log.
>
> **Reviewed.** This plan was fact-checked against the research and the Statamic 6.34.1 source, and reviewed for design flaws, on 2026-10-02. Both reviews changed it substantially. What they could not verify is listed under "Risks and open verifications"; Phase 2 settles the points the design depends on.

## Project at a glance

- **Product name:** Tolk ("interpreter" in Dutch and the Scandinavian languages), confirmed by the owner (D12)
- **Package:** `reachweb/statamic-tolk`
- **Namespace:** `Reach\StatamicTolk\` → `src/`; tests `Reach\StatamicTolk\Tests\` → `tests/`
- **Location:** `/Volumes/1TB/Sites/autotranslator/` (repo root; the folder keeps the project's working name; a git repository on `main` since T0.2)
- **Sandbox host site:** `/Volumes/1TB/Sites/tolk-sandbox/` (created in T0.4)
- **Target platform:** Statamic 6 only (`statamic/cms ^6.35`), Laravel `^12.62 || ^13.15`, PHP `^8.3`, Vue 3 + Inertia in the Control Panel (CP)
- **AI layer:** the official Laravel AI SDK, `laravel/ai ^1.0.1`, with Anthropic, OpenAI and Gemini
- **Dev tooling:** Laravel Boost, installed in the sandbox site and used from this repo through `.mcp.json`
- **Tests:** Pest 4 on `Statamic\Testing\AddonTestCase`; Vitest for CP JavaScript; Playwright against the sandbox with a fake engine; opt-in live tests against real provider APIs
- **What it does:** translates multi-site Statamic content from an origin into its localizations with LLMs.
  - In the entry editor: when an editor creates or opens a localization, the addon offers to translate the localizable fields and fills them into the open form, unsaved.
  - Per field: a field action translates one field.
  - In bulk: a listing action and a status overview start translation runs with progress.
  - From the CLI: one command translates a whole site into a new language.
  - Translation files and forms: two CLI commands translate the host's `lang/` files and the texts of its forms (D2, §A16).
  - Over time: it tracks, per field, whether the origin changed since translation and whether a human edited the translated text, and updates only what is outdated.
  - Quality: site profile, global instructions, per-language instructions and a glossary feed every prompt; an optional second model reviews the first model's output and applies corrections.

## Current build state (as of 2026-10-02)

- Nothing is built. The repo contains this file and `docs/research/`.
- The order of the phases is deliberate: Phase 4 delivers a thin end-to-end slice from the CLI (plain text fields of one entry), Phase 5 delivers the owner's first use case in the editor on top of it, and later phases widen content types, add review, runs and the remaining surfaces. Do not build the later layers first.

## Decisions reference (do not re-litigate without owner input)

Status: **Brief** = stated by the owner in the original request. **Confirmed** = the owner accepted the decision as written, or gave the answer recorded in the row (T0.1, 2026-10-02). **Planner's details** = mechanics the planner designed under a confirmed decision.

| ID | Topic | Decision | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D0 | Platform and stack | Statamic 6 only; Laravel AI SDK; Laravel Boost for development; API keys for Gemini, Claude and OpenAI | Brief |
| D1 | Distribution | Commercial Marketplace addon, single edition, users bring their own API keys. `license: proprietary`. No editions code. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D2 | Content scope v1 | Entries (all modes), taxonomy terms, global sets and navigation, in the CP and the CLI. In addition, two CLI-only commands: `tolk:lang` for the host's Laravel translation files (`lang/{lang}.json`, `lang/{lang}/*.php`) and `tolk:forms` for the texts of Statamic forms, which are written as JSON translation strings (§A16). Asset alt text comes in a later version. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 (changed: `lang/` files and forms added, CLI only; alt text moved to a later version) + planner's details (§A16) |
| D3 | Direction | The source is always the target's origin as Statamic defines it. Two entry points: pull (inside a localization) and push (from the origin). No any-to-any. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D4 | Editor behaviour | Translations are filled into the open publish form unsaved; the editor reviews and saves. Bulk action, overview and CLI save server-side. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D5 | Overwrite policy | Per field: untranslated, machine (current or outdated), manual. A field is manual as soon as a human changed its text. Manual fields are never overwritten unless the operation explicitly sets "overwrite manual edits". | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D6 | Origin changes | Outdated translations are flagged and updated on request. Only changed fields are re-sent, and unchanged paragraphs inside a changed field are reused. Automatic update on origin save is an optional extra (T11.1), built 2026-10-09 off by default. | Confirmed 2026-10-02; T11.1 built 2026-10-09 at the owner's request |
| D7 | Context | Whole-entry context (document summary and neighbouring paragraphs), AI-generated editable site profile, global instructions text, per-language instructions, glossary, per-field "translate" toggle and hint. No retrieval-based consistency memory in v1. | Brief (context, default text) + Confirmed 2026-10-02 (details; the owner chose the model-generated site profile) |
| D8 | Review | Reviewer-as-editor: a second model receives source and translation and returns corrected text only for segments it would change, each with a reason; corrections are applied automatically. Off by default; a setting turns it on and picks the reviewer; every operation can override; "Review again" is a manual action that accepts any configured model, including the same one. One automatic round. | Brief (multi-model review, follow-up review) + Confirmed 2026-10-02 (mechanics). The owner asked for corrections to be applied automatically and for the most cost-efficient method. This design is that method: only corrected segments come back, reused paragraphs are not reviewed again, and instructions are cached where the provider allows it. |
| D9 | Model defaults | Quality tier per provider by default, fast tier selectable. Explicit model ids are stored; SDK defaults are never relied on. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D10 | Publish state | New localizations created server-side are **drafts** by default; a setting and a per-operation option (`--publish`, "same as origin") mirror the origin's published state instead. Existing localizations keep their state. Immediately published machine translation is a pitfall competitors are criticised for (R5 §6.1 item 7). | Confirmed 2026-10-02 (changed from "mirror" during the plan review) |
| D11 | Host assumptions | Zero-infrastructure baseline: no database, `sync` queue. A real queue is used when configured. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D12 | Identity | Product name **Tolk**. Package `reachweb/statamic-tolk`, namespace `Reach\StatamicTolk\`, commands `php please tolk:*`, config key `tolk`, env prefix `TOLK_`. No custom addon slug, so Statamic's slug is `statamic-tolk`. The repo folder keeps its working name `autotranslator`; if it is ever renamed, the path repository in T1.9 changes with it. No Statamic addon or Packagist package uses the name (checked 2026-10-02); an old Rails i18n gem is also called Tolk. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D13 | Versions | PHP `^8.3`, `laravel/ai ^1.0.1`, `statamic/cms ^6.35`, `laravel/framework ^12.62 \|\| ^13.15`. | Confirmed 2026-10-02; Statamic floor raised from `^6.30` to `^6.35` by the owner in T2.4 (6.30.0 carries advisory GHSA-jppw-r5j3-xf7x; 6.35.0 fixes a cold multi-site Stache rebuild that drops entries, statamic/cms#15551) |
| D14 | API keys | `.env` only, through the SDK's standard `config/ai.php` keys, with optional addon-specific overrides. The settings page shows provider status and a "Test connection" button. Keys are never stored in CP settings. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D15 | `localizable` flag | Respected strictly. An audit (CLI and CP) lists text fields that are not marked localizable and can switch them on after confirmation; the whole-site command runs it as a preflight. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D16 | Slugs | A new localization keeps its origin's slug, except in the collections of the `translated_slugs` setting (empty by default), where it gets the slug of its translated title, made as the CP's form makes it on save. Server-side flows keep the slug that `makeLocalization()` copies, or replace it with the translated one once the fields are written, adding a numeric suffix only when another entry in the target site already uses it. Term slugs are never localized. In the publish form, core regenerates the slug from the translated title: the addon puts the origin's slug back (T5.9), except in the setting's collections, where it leaves core's slug. An existing slug is never changed. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 (changed from "translate by default" at the owner's request); the per-collection setting added 2026-10-09 at the owner's request, off by default |
| D17 | Fieldtypes | Core text-bearing fieldtypes plus a registration API for custom fieldtypes. No bundled third-party adapters. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D18 | Permissions | `translate content` (editor and single items) and `run bulk translations` (bulk action, overview, runs), on top of Statamic's own site, edit and publish permissions. Settings use the permission core registers. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D19 | Cost visibility | An estimate before a bulk run starts (on the run page and in the CLI), and usage per run and per month. No budget caps. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D20 | Conventions | Pest 4, single root `src/ServiceProvider.php`, `CLAUDE.md` and `TASKS.md` committed, `CHANGELOG.md` with an Unreleased section, built CP assets committed, Pint with the Laravel preset. Laravel Boost is installed in the sandbox site, where the addon really boots, and reached from the addon repo through a committed `.mcp.json`. (Through Testbench, as vouchers does, a root package is not booted as a Statamic addon and Boost finds no package guidelines or skills.) | Brief (mirror Resrv, use Boost) + Confirmed 2026-10-02 (where the owner's addons disagree). The brief's "check my resrv addon to see how I've implemented changes" means these conventions, not a change log (owner, T0.1). |
| D21 | Sandbox and tests | A dedicated flat-file sandbox site with English, Greek and German; PHP tests with SDK fakes; a fake engine for the sandbox; Playwright scenarios from Phase 5 on; opt-in live tests; Vitest. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D22 | Translation state storage | Outside content data, behind a repository. Driver `file` under `content/tolk/` so state travels with content in git. A `database` driver is an optional extra (T11.2). | Confirmed 2026-10-02 (forced by R4 §13.2: non-blueprint keys are dropped when a localization is saved in the CP) |
| D23 | Bulk execution | One run engine with three drivers: queue jobs when a real queue exists, browser-driven requests otherwise, and worker processes for the CLI. Model calls run in parallel; content is saved by **one writer at a time** (§A11), because parallel Stache writers lose each other's index and path updates. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 (follows from D11 and from how Stache caches) |
| D24 | Editor speed | The browser orchestrates: one plan request, then unit requests in parallel (default 4); fields fill as their units return. Every request makes at most one provider call. No token streaming, because the SDK cannot stream structured output (R2 §7.5). | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D25 | Bard handling | Walk the ProseMirror JSON and translate block by block with numbered inline placeholders. Never round-trip through HTML (lossy, R4 §7.5). | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D26 | Revisions | On collections with revisions enabled, updates to a published localization go to its working copy, as the CP does. "Publish changes" publishes them only when the working copy held nothing else. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D27 | Machine versus manual | Decided by text alone: a field counts as machine-translated while its translatable text is exactly what the addon produced. Differences that are not text (a swapped image, a toggled option, ids, normalisation by the CP) are not tracked, and Update rebuilds the field's structure from the origin. An editor can protect a field explicitly ("mark as manual"). | Confirmed 2026-10-02 (the alternative, comparing whole values, misreads CP normalisation as manual edits and silently stops updates) + planner's details (T2.4: defaults that Statamic fills into set fields are not target text, §A3 "Target-side walks") |
| D28 | Untranslatable parts | When one part of a field cannot be translated after the retries (invalid output, refusal), the field is still delivered with that part left in the source language, flagged, and retried on the next Update. A `strict_fields` setting fails the field instead. | Confirmed 2026-10-02 |
| D29 | Optional extras | Automatic update on origin save, database drivers, Eloquent test group, dashboard widget and command palette entries are built only if the owner asks for them (Phase 11). The owner asked for none of them for v1 (T0.1), so the Phase 11 tasks are marked deferred (`- [-]`). On 2026-10-09 the owner asked for T11.1, off by default, as a run rather than a job of its own, and with a second setting that also creates missing localizations. | Confirmed 2026-10-02; T11.1 2026-10-09 |

## Vocabulary

- **Site**: a Statamic site (`Statamic\Sites\Site`), one per language.
- **Origin / localization**: Statamic's terms. A localization inherits every field it does not override from its origin.
- **Source / target**: for one translation, the source is the origin item and the target is the localization (which may not exist yet).
- **Top-level field**: a field directly on the blueprint. Statamic localizes at this level only: a Bard, Replicator or Grid field is inherited whole or stored whole (R4 TL;DR 1).
- **Leaf**: one translatable piece of text inside a field value (a text field's string, one Bard paragraph, one table cell).
- **Segment**: a leaf prepared for the model, with an id.
- **Plan / unit**: a plan lists the segments to translate for one target and groups them into batches. A unit is one provider call: a batch, half of a split batch, a repair of one segment, or a review pass.
- **Run / run item**: a run is a bulk job; each item is one target.
- **State**: what the addon remembers about a translated field (fingerprints, model, time). It is never trusted on its own: status is always derived by comparing state with current content.

## Cross-cutting agent rules

1. **Tests after every task** that touches `src/`, `tests/` or `resources/js/`: `vendor/bin/pest`, and `npm test` when JavaScript changed. From Phase 5 on, also the browser scenarios named in the task (`npm run test:browser`). Stop and diagnose on the first failure.
2. **Code style:** run `vendor/bin/pint` before a task is complete.
3. **Built assets are committed.** Whenever `resources/js` or `resources/css` change, run `npm run cp:build` and commit `resources/dist/build` in the same commit. Tailwind also scans `src/**/*.php` (`resources/css/cp.css`), so a PHP change can change the CSS too: run the build after PHP changes as well and commit what changes.
4. **Service provider:** use `bootAddon()`, never `boot()` (R1 §9 item 1); call `parent::register()` in `register()` (R1 §2.3).
5. **No real API calls in the default test suite.** `Http::preventStrayRequests()` is on in the base TestCase. Live tests live in the Pest group `live` and run only on request.
6. **Driver-agnostic content access.** Use Statamic facades and object methods only. Never read or write content files directly, never use file modification times, query with `where('site', …)` not `locale` (R4 §13.4).
7. **Never write `values()` back into data**, and never call `data([...])` to change single fields; use `set()` / `merge()` / `remove()` (R4 §16.2).
8. **One writer.** Every server-side save of Statamic content goes through `Support\ContentWriter::write()` (§A11). Never save entries, terms, globals or trees from parallel code paths.
9. **Never hardcode `/cp`.** PHP uses `cp_route()`; JavaScript uses the global `cp_url()` (R1 §4.2).
10. **CP JavaScript:** `<script setup>` only; components from `@statamic/cms/ui`; do not bundle Vue, Pinia or Inertia; register everything inside one `Statamic.booting()` block in `resources/js/cp.js`; every UI string through `__()` with the English text as the key; never use `v-html` for content or model output. Mount modals with `createModal()` (`resources/js/modal.js`), keep the focus with `createFocusKeeper()` (`focus.js`), use `Description` rather than `Text variant="subtle"`, and give a `dark:` or `hover:` colour `!` when core's stylesheet has its light class (`tests/Feature/CpStylesTest.php`, T12.4).
11. **PHP style:** PHP 8.3 features (constructor promotion, readonly, enums, return types). No comments unless the reason is non-obvious. Enum cases in TitleCase with lowercase string values.
12. **Cache arrays and scalars only.** Plans, outcomes, runs and scan results are stored as arrays (`toArray()` / `fromArray()`), never as objects: hosts on Laravel 13 may restrict unserialisation (R1 §9.2). Cache-backed tests run against the `file` store, because the `array` store does not serialise.
13. **Secrets:** API keys come from the environment only. Never log them, never put them in agent properties, plans, runs, state or queue payloads. Provider error messages are passed through one sanitiser before they are stored or returned.
14. **Never mutate the host's `ai.*` config.** Build providers with `Ai::build()` (R2 §4.3).
15. **Commands:** signatures have no `statamic:` prefix (`tolk:translate`), the class uses `Statamic\Console\RunsInPlease` directly, so the same name works with `php please`, `php artisan` and `$this->artisan()` in tests.
16. **Update this file** whenever you finish a task: check the box, add a done-log line, and if you learned something that changes a later task, edit that task in the same commit.
17. **Commits:** small and atomic, one task or a few related tasks per commit, imperative sentence-case messages. Do not push, tag or open pull requests unless the owner asks.
18. **Stop and ask the owner** when a finding contradicts a row in the Decisions table, when a dependency has a new major version, when a task's self-check cannot pass as written, or when a self-check needs a browser or an API key that the session does not have.
19. **Do not edit `docs/research/01`–`06`.** Record new findings in `docs/research/07-spike-findings.md` (created in Phase 2, committed on `main`).
20. **Sandbox safety:** the sandbox is the only site you may install the addon into. Never touch the owner's other projects under `/Volumes/1TB/Sites/`.

Haiku 4.5 (64K, $1 / $5) is now legacy, still "Active" with retirement "not sooner than October 15, 2026" and no deprecation notice. Haiku 5.5: `thinking: {type: disabled}`. It rejects `between_tools` with a 400 and accepts `disabled` at its default effort `medium` (T13.4). Neither spent thinking tokens in the T13.4 probe. |

Provider built with `store: false`. Not probed live: no OpenAI key in T13.4, deferred by the owner. Never send `temperature`, `top_p` or `top_k` (R6 §0 trap 1). Provider options are shallow-merged into the request (T2.1), so catalog options must never contain `output_config` (Anthropic), `text` (OpenAI) or `response_format` (Gemini): each would replace the structured-output format. `laravel/ai` 1.2.0 keeps the format for `output_config` and `text` (laravel/ai#1105), but hosts on 1.0.x and 1.1 do not, so the rule stays.

## Architecture reference

Tasks cite these sections as §A1–§A15. If a task and this reference disagree, the reference wins; fix the task.

### §A1 Layout and class map

```
docs/                      user documentation, one Markdown file per topic, and images/ (T13.1; held to the code by tests/Feature/DocsCoverageTest.php); docs/research/ holds the planning reports
config/tolk.php            developer config (additions to and changes of the model catalog, limits, drivers, paths)
resources/models.php       Tolk's model catalog, merged with tolk.models by id (T11.6)
resources/blueprints/settings.yaml   native addon settings (user-facing)
resources/boost/guidelines/core.blade.php
resources/css/cp.css
resources/js/cp.js                   single entry, one Statamic.booting() block
resources/js/api.js                  HTTP client (core axios instance, looked up per call)
resources/js/modal.js                createModal(): the open state of a modal, mounted afresh each time (T12.4)
resources/js/focus.js                createFocusKeeper(): keeps the keyboard focus in the banner or a page when its control disappears (T12.4)
resources/js/editor/                 plain JS modules: registry, store, statuses, banner, fill, unitRunner, translate, saveHooks, offer, options, fieldActions, diff, push (unit-tested)
resources/js/runs/                   ticker (lanes, apply loop, polling), follow (the run page's loops and buttons), describe (what the run pages show)
resources/js/overview/               cells (loads the Overview's cells, three at a time), matrix (what the Overview shows, the runs its buttons create)
resources/js/audit/                  checks (what the Audit page shows: levels, the summary, the fields it can make localizable)
resources/js/components/             EditorBanner, OriginPush, TranslateOffer, TranslateOptions, ChangesModal, TranslateFieldConfirm, ProgressBar, RunsTable
resources/js/fieldtypes/             Vue side of the settings fieldtypes
resources/js/pages/                  Inertia pages: Runs/Index, Runs/Show, Overview, Audit
resources/dist/build/                committed build output
routes/cp.php

```

A root provider in `src/` gets Statamic's auto-discovery for `Actions`, `Console/Commands`, `Fieldtypes`, `Listeners`, `Widgets`, `UpdateScripts`, `routes/cp.php` and the settings blueprint (R1 §2.2). Listener discovery works by reflection on public `handle*` / `__invoke` methods and their first parameter's type. Discovery is not recursive.

### §A2 Which fields are translated

A **top-level** blueprint field is in scope when all of these hold:

1. `$field->isLocalizable()` is true.
2. Its fieldtype handle has a handler in the `FieldtypeRegistry`.
3. `$field->fieldtype()->config('tolk_translate')` is not `false`. Read it through `fieldtype()->config()`, because values equal to the default are stripped from blueprint YAML (R4 §8).
4. `$field->visibility()` is neither `computed` nor `hidden`.
5. Its handle is not in the `excluded_fields` setting.

A **nested** field (inside Replicator, Bard sets, Grid, Group) is in scope when 2 and 3 hold. Nested fields have no `localizable` flag.

Special cases:

- `slug` is never translated as content (a field with segments). It is a property of the entry and follows §A11 (D16).
- `title` is an ordinary text field. Skip it when the collection auto-generates titles (`$collection->autoGeneratesTitles()`), because Statamic regenerates it on save (R4 §16.2 item 8).
- Chains (`en → de → de_CH`): a field of a target whose source is itself a localization is eligible only when that source holds its own value for the field. If the source still inherits it, the text is in another language than the one the prompt names, so the field is skipped with reason `origin_untranslated`.

`Content\TranslatableFields` (T4.5) applies rules 1–5 and the `slug` and `title` cases; the chain rule belongs to the planner. It reads `auto_title` and `excluded_fields` only from the context its caller passes, never from the collection or the settings. `explain()` gives every top-level field the first reason that applies, in this order: `slug` (the handle, whatever its fieldtype), `auto_title`, `unsupported_fieldtype`, `computed`, `hidden`, `disabled`, `excluded`, `not_localizable`. So every reason after `unsupported_fieldtype` means the fieldtype has a handler, and `not_localizable` means the field would be in scope if it were localizable, which is what the audit lists (T9.6).

The source value of a field is read from the source's own data (`has()` / `get()`), never with `value()` or `values()`, which mix in computed values and the collection's injected defaults (R4 §2.1).

Handlers shipped in v1: `text` (only when `input_type` is unset or `text`), `textarea`, `markdown`, `bard`, `replicator`, `grid`, `group`, `table`, `list`, `array`. Everything else is ignored unless a handler is registered (D17).

### §A3 Leaves, segments and placeholders

A handler walks one field value and exposes each translatable string as a **leaf**. The same walk is used to read leaves and to write translations back, so order is identical by construction.

A string is **not** a leaf (it is left untouched and never sent) when, after trimming, it is empty, contains no letter (`/\p{L}/u`), or is only a reference or an identifier: an absolute or protocol-relative URL, a root-relative path with no spaces, `mailto:` / `tel:` / `statamic://`, `entry::…` / `asset::…`, a `#fragment`, an email address, a hex colour, or a single lowercase token joined by `-` or `_` (`text-center`, `icon_arrow`), which in content is almost always a class name or key. The rules look at the string with its Antlers tags removed (T4.4), because those are carried through as atoms: `{{ partial:hero }}` alone is not a leaf, and `{{ site:url }}/contact` is a path.

**Target-side walks** (T2.4). When a set field with a text `default` is added to a blueprint after content was written, the CP's next save and the server-side normalisation (§A11 step 3) put that default, in the source language, into every existing Replicator row, Grid row and Bard set (T2.2 check 4, T2.3). So a walk over a **target** value (status and text fingerprints, the reuse memory, review) leaves untouched every nested field whose value equals that field's configured `default`. Such texts then neither make a machine field read as manual (§A6) nor shift the leaves that reuse and review pair by position (§A7, T7.4). A walk over a **source** value keeps them: a default an editor left in place (a "Read more" label) is visible text and is translated. The rule depends only on the value and the blueprint, and is applied the same way when a target hash is produced and when it is checked. Two consequences: a translation that happens to equal its field's default is left out on both sides and only breaks the positional pairing, which the alignment guard (T4.15) detects; and changing a set field's default later makes stored copies of the old default count as text again, so a machine field holding one reads as manual.

Segment kinds:

| Kind | Used for | What the model sees |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `plain` | text, textarea, table cells, list items, array values, image alt, link title | The string, with protected spans replaced by placeholders |
| `inline` | one Bard block (paragraph, heading, list item paragraph, table cell paragraph) | Text runs with numbered tags for formatting |
| `markdown` | one block of a markdown field | Markdown as written, with code, link destinations and raw HTML replaced by placeholders |
| `html` | one top-level block of a Bard field stored as an HTML string (`save_html`; of a block element only its content, T6.9), HTML found in plain fields | Text with every tag replaced by a placeholder |

Placeholders:

- `<1>…</1>` is a paired tag: a run of formatted text, or a matched HTML open/close pair.
- `<x1/>` is an atom: something carried through unchanged (an Antlers tag, a code span, a link destination in Markdown, an unpaired HTML tag, an inline Bard node, a Bard run carrying the `code` mark).
- `<br/>` is a Bard hard break.

Every leaf carries a `placeholders` map, id → descriptor (a canonical string describing what the placeholder stands for: the marks of a run, the original tag text, the node). The descriptors let `Renumber` express a leaf taken from the **target** in the numbering of the corresponding **source** leaf, which reuse (§A7) and review (T7.4) need: after a translation reorders formatted runs, the target's own numbering no longer matches the source's.

In `plain` and `inline` segments, literal `<`, `>` and `&` in the text are escaped to `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `&amp;` before sending and unescaped after. `markdown` and `html` segments are not escaped.

In `html` segments (T6.2), a matched open/close pair is a paired tag whose descriptor is the opening tag's text; void, self-closing and unmatched tags, comments, `<!…>` / `<?…>` declarations, text shaped like a placeholder, and whole `script`, `style`, `pre` and `code` elements are atoms. Antlers tags are atoms too, and an Antlers tag inside an HTML tag or element is part of that placeholder. What a reader sees of an `html` segment (`Placeholders::visible()`, used for `max_length`, the leaf rules and the alignment guard) has its character references decoded. A Bard field stored as HTML is cut into its top-level blocks (`Tokenizer::splitHtmlBlocks()`), and when a block is exactly one block element (`<p …>…</p>`, `<ul>…</ul>`), its tags stay outside the leaf (`Tokenizer::unwrapBlock()`, T6.9): text between blocks belongs to the next block on the next walk, so a translation that put text there would change the leaves.

In `inline` segments (T6.4), consecutive text nodes with equal marks form a run; a run with marks is a paired tag whose descriptor is the canonical JSON of its marks (`Fingerprint::canonicalJson()`, so the `null` attributes the CP adds change nothing); a run carrying the `code` mark, every inline node other than `text` and `hardBreak`, and Antlers tags (also inside a pair) are atoms; `hardBreak` is `<br/>`. Descriptors leave out a link mark's `attrs.title` and an `image` node's `attrs.alt`: both are leaves of their own, so a source and its translation keep the same descriptors.

In `markdown` segments (T6.3), one per block of the field, the atoms are fences, code spans, link and image destinations (directly after `](`, so rule 7 sees them; link text and titles stay text), whole reference definitions (a title on the next line included), the label of a full or collapsed reference (`[text][label]`, `[text][]`), footnote references, autolinks, attribute lists (`{#id .class}`), hard breaks (two spaces or a backslash before a line break) and Antlers tags; raw HTML is tokenized as in `html`. A collapsed or shortcut reference to a defined label (`[price list][]`, `[our story]`) names its definition by its text, so its atom (the `[]`, or an empty atom after the `]`) carries the label as its descriptor and in `Tokenized::$references`, and `restore()` of a changed text writes that label instead, turning it into a full reference (`[τιμοκατάλογος][price list]`). `visible()` keeps a `markdown` segment as written: it holds its Markdown syntax anyway, and Markdown fields have no character limit.

A translated segment is **valid** when:

1. Paired tags are balanced and properly nested, and every paired id of the source appears exactly once. The one exception is `inline` segments: there a formatted run may be split into several occurrences of the same id, and paired tags are never nested (the source never nests them).
2. Every paired placeholder encloses at least one non-whitespace character (a nested placeholder counts). (`<1></1>` would silently drop a link.) A pair that is already empty in the source may stay empty (an HTML anchor, T4.3).
3. Every atom of the source appears exactly once.
4. The number of `<br/>` equals the source's.
5. No placeholder id appears that the source did not have, and no other tag-like token appears: a `<` before a letter, `/`, `!` or `?`, as a browser would read it, closed by `>` or not (T4.3); nor more Antlers openings (`{{`) than the source has.
6. In `plain` and `inline` segments, the translation contains no more escaped `<` or `>` than the source. (An escaped `&lt;script&gt;` the source did not have would become a literal tag after unescaping.)
7. In `markdown` segments every link and image destination is still a placeholder, so the model can never introduce a link target; nor does the translation hold a web or email address (`https://…`, `www.…`, `name@host`) more often than the source does, which a Markdown field with `automatic_links` would turn into a link (T12.3).
8. The text is not empty when the source was not.
9. Its length is at most `4 × source length + 200` characters.

### §A4 Fingerprints

Three functions in `Content\Fingerprint`:

- `of(mixed $value): string`, used for **source** values. The first 16 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the raw stored value of one top-level field. Canonical form, applied recursively:
  - a map (non-list array) has its keys sorted (compared as strings), and entries whose canonical value is `null`, `''` or `[]` are dropped (this mirrors Statamic's own null removal on save, R4 §2.1 and §7.3);
  - a list keeps its order and all its items;
  - scalars become strings (`true` / `false`, the plain decimal form for numbers: the fewest digits that parse back to the same number, never an exponent, so `1.0` becomes `1` and `1e20` becomes `100000000000000000000`); in strings `\r\n` becomes `\n`;
  - JSON is encoded with `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES`.
- `ofLeaves(array $texts): string`, used for **target** values. The first 16 hex characters of the SHA-256 of the JSON list of the leaf texts (as the target-side walk of §A3 produces them, placeholders included), in walk order. "The target's text fingerprint" everywhere in this file means `ofLeaves` of that walk.
- `ofText(string $text): string`. The first 12 hex characters of the SHA-256 of one leaf text.

Why the two sides differ (D27): the source fingerprint must react to every change of the origin, structural ones included, so the translation is flagged and rebuilt. The target fingerprint must **not** react to anything but text. A load and save in the CP rewrites values without any human edit (row ids, `enabled` flags, defaults of newly added set fields, Bard's own JSON), and a whole-value fingerprint would read each of those as a manual edit and silently stop all future updates of that field. T2.2 confirmed that such a save changes no text, with one exception: the default of a set field added later can be a text, which the target-side walk (§A3) leaves out.

The other side of the same fact: the first CP save of origin content that was written outside the CP (seeded, imported, saved by code) adds row ids and `enabled` flags to it (T2.2 check 4). That changes its source fingerprint, so its machine-translated targets read outdated once although no text changed. Update then rebuilds them without a model call, because every leaf is reused (§A7). This is accepted; the docs mention it (T13.1, `statuses.md`).

### §A5 State

State is stored per target, outside content data (D22), through `StateRepository`. A target is identified by a reference string:

| Type | Reference | File (file driver, under `content/tolk/`) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Entry | `entry::{localization id}` | `entries/{id}.yaml` |
| Term | `term::{taxonomy}::{default slug}::{site}` | `terms/{taxonomy}/{slug}/{site}.yaml` |
| Global set | `globals::{handle}::{site}` | `globals/{handle}/{site}.yaml` |
| Navigation | `nav::{handle}::{site}` | `navs/{handle}/{site}.yaml` |
| Translation file | `lang::{group}::{lang}` | `lang/{group}/{lang}.yaml` |
| Form texts | `forms::{lang}` | `forms/{lang}.yaml` |

The entry, term and globals formats are the ones the CP publish container exposes as `reference` (R3 §4.3), so the browser can send them as they are. The `nav::` format is the addon's own; core has no reference for nav trees. The `lang::` and `forms::` formats (§A16) are the addon's own too, and only the CLI uses them. Storage paths are lower-cased and made filesystem-safe.

```yaml
reference: 'entry::0b5f…'
source_site: en
v: 1                                   # format version of walker and state; records of another version are not reused
fields:
  title:
    source_hash: 9f2c1a7e5b3d4c10      # Fingerprint::of() of the source value this translation was made from
    target_hash: 1b7d02c4a9e6f358      # Fingerprint::ofLeaves() of the translated leaf texts; null for a human baseline
    segments: [a1b2c3d4e5f6, null]     # Fingerprint::ofText() of each source leaf in walk order; null = that leaf was left untranslated
    identical: false                   # true when the translation equals the source text
    incomplete: true                   # true when any leaf was left untranslated (D28)
    translated_at: '2026-10-01T12:00:00Z'
    translator: 'anthropic:claude-sonnet-5-5'
    reviewer: null
    history:                           # up to 3 earlier machine outputs of this field, overtaken deliveries included
      - { source_hash: 77aa…, target_hash: 31c9… }
    pending:                           # up to 3, newest first: produced, not yet known to be saved
      - { plan: 01J…, source_hash: …, target_hash: …, target_before: null, segments: […], identical: false, incomplete: false, translator: …, reviewer: null, created_at: … }
                                       # an entry may also hold `baseline: true` (a reviewed manual field, T7.4)
```

Rules:

- Every key of a field record is optional. A record may consist of `pending` entries only.
- Every flow writes a `pending` entry **before** content is saved and resolves it after: the editor flow when it hands values to the browser, server-side flows right before `save()`. A crash between save and state write therefore leaves a pending entry that the next reconcile promotes.
- `target_before` is the target's text fingerprint when the plan was made (`null` when the field was inherited). It tells "nothing was saved" apart from "something else was saved".
- A **baseline** is a record with `source_hash` set and `target_hash: null`. It says "a human owns this text, and it was checked against this source". It keeps the field manual while making later source changes detectable.
- When a new machine output replaces an earlier one, the earlier `{source_hash, target_hash}` pair moves to `history`. When a pending entry is promoted, the outputs of the other entries move there too (reconcile rule 1).
- When a server-side apply removes an override because the source was emptied (§A7), the field's record keeps only its `history`, which takes in the machine pair and the outputs of the pending entries (not an entry with `baseline: true`), and the pending entries are dropped (`StateWriter::recordRemoved()`, `FieldState::removed()`). A form left open before the removal or a restored revision that saves one of those outputs again then reads **outdated** (§A6 rule 1), and Update removes it again; a forgotten record would read it as manual and untracked, and keep it. The editor's own removal (a delivered `sync`, §A8 step 5) writes no state, so the record keeps its machine pair and the same text reads outdated too.
- Repository writes are atomic read-modify-write (`update(TargetRef, Closure)`), because parallel requests write the same record.
- Reading is lenient (T4.6), because these files live in git and may be edited or merged by hand: a value of the wrong type reads as missing; a `pending` entry without `plan`, either hash or `created_at`, and a `history` pair without `target_hash`, are dropped; `history` and `pending` are capped at 3 on read too; a record without `v` reads as version 0, so it is not reused; a file whose `reference` is not the requested target reads as empty. Times are written as UTC `…Z` strings. A field record is written with only the keys it holds (no `null`, `false` or empty list), and a record without hashes, history or pending entries is not stored.
- Promoting a pending entry sets `translated_at` to its `created_at`. A baseline written from a pending entry (reconcile rule 2) also moves the previous machine pair to `history`, so that output still reads as machine output if it comes back. `history` holds each `target_hash` once and never the confirmed one.
- A pending entry with `baseline: true` is a review's output for a manual field (T7.4). Promoting it writes a **baseline**, not machine keys: its `source_hash`, `translator` and `reviewer`, `target_hash: null`, and its text left out of `history`. So the reviewed text stays manual whether it is saved server-side or from the editor, and a crash between save and state write leaves it manual too.

**Reconcile.** Resolving the pending entries of one field against the current target (`hasOverride`, `T` = `ofLeaves` of the target's own value):

1. Exact match first, over all pending entries: if `hasOverride` and `T == p.target_hash`, or if not `hasOverride` and `p.identical` (the Eloquent driver stores a translation identical to its source as "inherited", R4 §13.3), then `p` is **promoted** to the confirmed keys (to a baseline when `p.baseline`) and the other entries are dropped, their `{source_hash, target_hash}` pairs moved to `history`, newest first and ahead of the previous machine pair (an entry with `baseline: true` holds a human's reviewed text and stays out). Each is a delivery that another save overtook: a form left open while a run, a push, an automatic update or a second tab saved the field. When that form is saved later with the delivery untouched, the field holds an older machine output, which reads **outdated** (§A6) whether or not the origin changed, so Update translates it from the current origin; it is not manual, and it is no edit for rule 2.
2. Otherwise, take the newest entry `p` for which `hasOverride`, `T != p.target_before`, `T` is not the confirmed `target_hash`, not in `history` and not `C`, the source's own text (§A6). The value changed after that plan and is not machine output: the editor edited the delivered text before saving. (A copy of the source's text is no edit: the form saves one when an inherited Bard field desyncs itself and the delivery was undone, T6.11; rule 3 keeps the entries.) Write a **baseline** from it (`source_hash = p.source_hash`, `target_hash = null`, keep `translator`) and keep the pending entries, which expire as in rule 3, with their `target_before` set to `T`. Keeping them follows D27: the saved text need not be an edit of these deliveries (a second tab saved its own text, or the editor later undoes the edit), so one of them saved exactly later is machine output, and rule 1 promotes it. With `target_before = T` the same text makes no baseline again, so a later baseline (Protect, `tolk:baseline`) is not overwritten, and `StateWriter::confirm()` reports a field as baselined only when its record changed.
3. Otherwise nothing was saved yet. Keep entries younger than 30 days (`FieldState::PENDING_DAYS`), drop older ones.

The rules live in `FieldState::reconcile(?string $targetHash, CarbonImmutable $now, ?string $sourceTextHash = null)`, where a `null` hash means "no override" and `$sourceTextHash` is `C`; `savedPending()` and `editedPending()` return the entry rules 1 and 2 pick (T4.8, T6.11).

`StatusResolver` applies these rules in memory and never writes. `StateWriter::confirm()` applies and persists them. It is called by the editor's confirm request after a save, at the start of every server-side operation on a target, and by `tolk:prune`.

### §A6 Field status

Computed by `StatusResolver`, never stored, without side effects. Inputs for field F of target T: `S` = `Fingerprint::of` the source's current value, `hasOverride` = the target's own data has key F, `T` = `Fingerprint::ofLeaves` of the target's own value, walked as a target (§A3), `st` = the state for F after the in-memory reconcile of §A5, `C` = `Fingerprint::ofLeaves` of the source's current value walked as a target (computed only when it can decide something: an override without a known `source_hash`, or pending entries; T6.11).

1. The source value is empty or has no leaves:
   - the target holds untouched machine output (`hasOverride`, `T == st.target_hash`) → **outdated** when `st.source_hash != S`: the text was removed in the origin, and Update removes the override. With an unchanged source (and not `st.incomplete`) it is **current**: that is the copy a clear writes for a source with structure but no text (§A7), which would otherwise read outdated forever (T4.8);
   - the target holds an older machine output (`T` in `st.history`, also the output an apply removed when the source was emptied, §A5) → **outdated**, as in rule 3;
   - the target holds a manual value → **manual** (rule 3);
   - otherwise **not applicable**, excluded from counts.
2. Not `hasOverride`: **current** or **outdated** (by `st.source_hash == S`) when `st.identical` is true, and **outdated** when `st.incomplete` too, so the part left in the source language is retried (D28); otherwise **untranslated**.
3. `hasOverride`:
   - `T == st.target_hash`: machine output, text untouched. **outdated** when `st.incomplete`; otherwise **current** when `st.source_hash == S`, else **outdated**.
   - `T` equals a `target_hash` in `st.history`: **outdated**. An older machine output came back (a stale form was saved, also with a delivery that another save overtook, §A5 rule 1; a revision was restored; content was rolled back).
   - `T == C`, no `source_hash` is known and `C` has text: **untranslated**. The target holds a copy of the source's text that nobody vouched for, so no human changed its text (D5). The CP saves such a copy when an inherited Bard field desyncs itself while the form loads (T6.11); a field translated by hand that happens to equal its source reads the same way. A baseline (Protect, T5.10) makes it manual.
   - otherwise **manual**, with `sourceChanged` = `st.source_hash != S` when a `source_hash` is known, or `null` (untracked) when it is not.

Item status, worst first: **missing** (the target does not exist) → **untranslated** (any field untranslated) → **outdated** (any field outdated) → **attention** (any manual field with `sourceChanged === true`) → **current**. Counts also report **untracked** manual fields (`sourceChanged === null`) separately, so a site translated by hand before the addon was installed does not look fully tracked; `tolk:baseline` fixes that.

For entries on collections with revisions enabled, "the target's own value" is read from `$entry->fromWorkingCopy()` (§A11), which is what the CP shows.

### §A7 Modes and eligibility

| Mode | Fields processed |
| --- | --- |
| `missing` | untranslated |
| `update` | untranslated + outdated |
| `all` | untranslated + outdated + current |
| flag `overwrite_manual` | additionally manual |
| `review` | fields whose text is machine output and current; with `overwrite_manual` also manual fields. The text is the target's own value, or the open form's values reconciled as if saved, so delivered output not yet saved counts (T7.4); a translation the target only inherits is not reviewed (`untranslated`) |

A target that does not exist is created and all its applicable fields count as untranslated. Skip reasons recorded in a plan: `not_requested` (not in the operation's `fields`), the caller's own reason for a field it rules out (`edited_in_form`, editor, T5.8), `origin_untranslated`, then the field's status when the mode does not select it (`current`, `outdated`, `manual`, `not_applicable`), and `structure_differs` (review: the source and target walks differ in length or kinds, or no target leaf renumbers, T7.4). The planner gives each field the first that applies, in that order (T4.14).

**Outdated because the source was emptied** (§A6 rule 1): the field is planned with no segments. Applying it removes the override when the source value is empty, and the field's state keeps only its history (§A5), or writes a copy of the source value when it merely has no text.

**Reuse on update.** For an outdated field whose target is untouched machine output, the target's leaves (walked as a target, §A3) line up one-to-one with `st.segments` (the pipeline verifies this when it produces a value and stores an empty list when it does not hold, T4.15). Build a memory `source leaf hash → target leaf texts by occurrence`, in walk order, leaving out each leaf whose `segments` entry is `null`. The n-th copy of a text in the current source takes the n-th earlier translation of it, else (a copy the origin added, or one that does not renumber) the first that renumbers, each taken through `Renumber::like()` so its placeholders use the numbering of that current source leaf. By occurrence because of D6: an unchanged copy keeps its own translation, which the model may have made differ from another copy's in context, or a reviewer corrected alone. A leaf none of whose earlier translations renumbers is not reused. Every current source leaf with a translation in the memory reuses it; only new, changed or previously untranslated leaves are sent to the model. The new value is always rebuilt from the **current source structure**, so structural changes in the origin propagate and a change with no new text costs no API call. Mode `all` does not reuse. Records with another `v` are not reused.

**Context for partial work.** A segment whose neighbouring leaf is not in the same batch carries that neighbour's source text as `before` / `after` (read-only, truncated to 300 characters), so a single changed paragraph is not translated out of context. Neighbours are the previous and next leaf of the same field's walk, reused leaves included; only segments in a batch get them. The text is what a reader sees (`Placeholders::readable()`: `visible()` with hard breaks as spaces; whitespace squished), so the model is never shown placeholders it could copy; a longer `before` keeps its last 299 characters after `…`, a longer `after` its first 299 before `…` (T6.10).

### §A8 Pipeline: plan, units, assemble

One pipeline serves the editor, runs and the CLI. **Every step makes at most one provider call and never sleeps**, so a web request stays inside a proxy timeout.

1. **Plan** (`Planner::plan(Target, PlanOptions): Plan`): reconcile state in memory, resolve statuses, choose fields by mode, walk each field's source value into leaves, apply reuse, give every remaining leaf a segment id (`s1`, `s2`, …), and group segments into batches by character budget (document order; a field may span batches). Planning has no side effects. The caller puts the plan into the `PlanStore` (cache, TTL `plan_ttl`). A stored plan is never modified.
2. **Units.** The initial units are the batches (`b1`, `b2`, …). `Pipeline::runUnit(Plan, unitId): UnitOutcome` takes a lock on the unit, makes one provider call, validates the result, and stores an **outcome** under the unit's own cache key. An outcome may **spawn** follow-up units with deterministic ids (§A10): the halves of a split, a repair of one segment, a request for missing ids, the review pass. The set of units of a plan is the initial batches plus everything spawned by stored outcomes, so parallel requests never write the same key.
3. **Progress.** `Pipeline::runnable(Plan)` lists units without an outcome; `isFinished(Plan)` is true when there are none. `translation(Plan, segmentId)` resolves the final text of a segment: a review correction, else the valid translation, else the reused text, else none (the segment failed for good).
4. **Assemble** (`Pipeline::assemble(Plan, Target, handle): AssembledField`): once no runnable unit touches the field, walk the source value again and replace each leaf. A leaf whose segment failed for good keeps its source text and the field is marked `incomplete` (D28), unless `strict` is on, in which case the field fails. The result carries the raw target value, `source_hash`, `target_hash`, `segments`, `identical`, `incomplete`, corrections and warnings.
5. **Deliver.** The editor flow converts assembled values to publish-form shape and returns values and meta, writing `pending` state. Server-side flows save through the `Target` inside `ContentWriter::write()`, which checks each field's source again under the lock (§A11).

Transient provider failures (rate limit, overload, timeout of a single segment) do **not** produce an outcome. `runUnit` throws `ProviderPaused` or `ProviderUnavailable` with a wait, and the caller runs the same unit again later: the editor's unit runner, a run lane, or the CLI loop.

Plan shape (stored as an array; what tests assert against):

```
Plan {
  id, kind: translate|review, reference|null, target: { type, source_id, site }, user_id|null,
  source_site, target_site,
  options: { mode, overwrite_manual, fields|null, skip: { handle: reason }, translator, review, reviewer, fallback, profile: web|background, strict },
  document: { type, title, container, url|null, summary },
  fields:   { handle: { source_hash, target_before, leaf_count, status_before, segment_ids[], reused, reused_translator|null, clear, value?, record? } },
  segments: { id: { field, index, kind, text, label, hint|null, max_length|null, plural_form, before|null, after|null, translation|null } },
  batches:  [ { id, segment_ids[], chars } ],
  skipped:  { handle: reason },
}
```

`segments[id].translation` is pre-filled for reused leaves; `fields[*].reused_translator` is the state's `translator` when anything is reused, and `assemble()` counts each reused segment for it (T6.10). In a review plan, `text` is the source text and `translation` the current target text in the source's numbering (`Renumber::like()`), and each field also holds `value` (the raw value under review) and `record` (`segments` and `translator` of the state record its text matched, T7.4); a review plan has no reused segments. `index` is the leaf's position in its field's walk. `label` is the label path of the owning field ("Body › FAQ › Question"). `plural_form` is true for one form of a pluralised lang string (§A16), whose translation must hold no `|`; it stays out of the prompt, where the segment's hint asks for the same.

### §A9 Prompt contract

- **System prompt** (identical for every request with the same source language, target language and settings): role and quality bar, site profile, global instructions, per-language instructions, glossary, then the fixed rules. Static parts first and byte-identical. No timestamps, ids or per-item text.
- **User message**: JSON, `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES` (R6 §4c): `{ "document": {…}, "segments": [ … ] }`. Segment keys always in this order: `id`, `field`, `kind`, then `hint`, `max_length`, `before`, `after` only when set, then `text` (for review: `source` and `translation`), then `note` only on a repair.
- **Translation output schema** (fixed for every request; Anthropic and OpenAI cannot express dynamic keys, R6 §0): `{ "translations": [ { "id": string, "text": string } ] }`, everything required.
- **Review output schema**: `{ "corrections": [ { "id": string, "text": string, "reason": string } ] }`. A segment the reviewer accepts is simply absent.
- No sampling parameters. Reasoning options come from the model catalog (see "Version pins").
- Provider caching is a bonus, not something the design depends on. With this SDK only Anthropic gets an explicit cache breakpoint (`#[CacheInstructions]`), and only when the system prompt reaches the model's minimum size (512 tokens on Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5); OpenAI's models need a breakpoint the SDK does not send (R2 §11.4, R6 §1.4, §2.4). From GPT-5.6 on, OpenAI's default implicit mode writes each whole request to the cache at 1.25 times the input price and never reads it back, because the user message differs per request; so `StructuredAgent` sends `prompt_cache_options: {mode: explicit}` without a breakpoint to those models, which writes nothing (earlier models reject the key with a 400; both checked live on 2026-10-09, `07-spike-findings.md` "SDK"). It matches ids that start with `gpt-` only: a fine-tuned id (`ft:gpt-6-luna:…`) gets nothing, because no live check showed whether fine-tunes accept the key. `docs/configuration.md` describes adding it to a fine-tune's catalog options as an opt-in to try on one entry in the editor first, and leaves it out of its example: an OpenAI 400 is `request_rejected` (§A10), so a refused option does not abort a run but delivers every field in the source language (D28). A catalog option `prompt_cache_options` replaces it. Never warm caches or count on cache reads in estimates.

### §A10 What a unit's result leads to

`runUnit` makes one call. What happens next:

| Result of the call | Handling |
| --- | --- |
| `RateLimitedException` (every HTTP 429) | First read the error body through `getPrevious()->response`, and only when the previous exception is a `RequestException` (Gemini's Interactions endpoint wraps the body in a JSON list, `[{"error": …}]`, which `ErrorClassifier` unwraps for every status). A spend-cap, quota or billing code is not a rate limit and counts as "rejected": Anthropic `enforced_spend_limit_reached`; OpenAI `credit_balance_exhausted`, `organization_spend_limit_exceeded`, `project_spend_limit_exceeded`, `organization_usage_limit_exceeded`, `insufficient_quota`; Gemini `quota_exceeded` (the code Google documents for the Interactions API's daily quota), or a `QuotaFailure` detail in `error.details[]` whose violation's `quotaId` contains `PerDay` (a daily quota, reset at midnight Pacific time) or whose `quotaValue` is `0` (a quota the tier does not include), both rejected with code `quota_exceeded` (R6 §1.5, §2.5, §3.5, §4b). A real rate limit: record a provider-wide pause in `Throttle` (honour `retry-after`, else the delay of Google's `RetryInfo` detail, `retryDelay` such as `"40s"`, else the backoff of `RetryPolicy`) and throw `ProviderPaused`. No outcome is stored. |
| `ProviderOverloadedException` (502, 503, 504, 529 and similar), HTTP 500 and 408 (plain `RequestException`), and `AiException` (an error reported inside a 200 response, such as OpenAI `status: failed`; T2.1) | Same as a real rate limit. |
| `ProviderConnectionException` (connection failures and timeouts, R2 §10.3) | No provider-wide pause: the previous exception has no response, and one slow call must not stall the others. A timeout or an HTTP 413 (which arrives as a plain `RequestException`, T2.1) on a unit with several segments stores an outcome that spawns two halves. With one segment, or on a plain connection failure, throw `ProviderUnavailable`. |
| Transient failures in a row | The caller counts them per unit (in the cache). After `retry.max_attempts`, `runUnit` uses the fallback model for that unit, if one is set, its provider has a key, and it is not the paused provider. Without a usable fallback it keeps throwing; the caller decides when to give up (T5.8, T8.4). |
| Rejected: `InsufficientCreditsException` (402); HTTP 401 / 403; HTTP 404 (unknown or retired model id); the non-retryable 429 codes; for Gemini, any other 4xx whose `error.details[].reason` starts with `API_KEY_` (Google answers a wrong or revoked key with 400 `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, reason `API_KEY_INVALID`; code `invalid_key`) or whose `error.status` is `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (billing, or a region the free tier does not serve), or whose `error.code` is `failed_precondition`, the shape Google's API errors page documents for the Interactions API (`{"error": {"code": "failed_precondition", "message": …}}`; both read without regard to case, code `FAILED_PRECONDITION`) | Throw `ProviderRejected` with a sanitised message naming the provider and, for 404, the model id. Runs abort. Never retried. |
| HTTP 400 and other 4xx (`RequestException`) | Log the request shape without content. The outcome fails the unit's segments with reason `request_rejected`. |
| Finish reason `Length`, or `structured === []` (R2 §6.4) | Several segments: spawn two halves (split depth at most 3). One segment: spawn one retry with the output cap doubled (up to the catalog cap), then fail the segment with reason `too_long`. |
| Finish reason `ContentFilter`, an OpenAI `refusal` content item (OpenAI reports a refusal as `Stop` with `structured === []`; only `$response->raw` shows it, T2.1), or an OpenAI `incomplete_details.reason` of `content_filter` (laravel/ai before 1.2.1 reports it as `Length`, like a truncation; checked before the `Length` row) | Several segments: spawn two halves to isolate the refusal. One segment: fail it with reason `refused`. |
| Ids missing from the output | Spawn one unit with the missing segments. If they are missing again, fail them with reason `missing`. |
| A segment that is not valid (§A3, or a pipe character in the translation of a `plural_form` segment, §A16) | Spawn a repair unit for that segment alone, with a `note` naming the problem; at most 2 repairs, then fail it with reason `invalid`. Exceeding `max_length` gets one repair and is then accepted with a warning. |
| Valid translations, review on | Spawn one review unit for those segments. A review unit that fails for any reason other than `ProviderRejected` adds the warning "review skipped" and never fails a segment. A rejected reviewer is handled as in the rejected row above: its key or model is wrong, so `ProviderRejected` propagates and runs abort (T7.3). |

A segment that failed for good is handled at assembly (§A8 step 4). The SDK has no retries of its own (R2 §10.2).

### §A11 Saving content

**One writer at a time.** Stache keeps its path map and indexes in process memory and writes them back whole on every save, and a request or command that is not a queue worker never refreshes that memory (queue workers get only a partial reset between jobs). Two processes that save entries in parallel therefore lose each other's entries from the index: files exist on disk but Statamic no longer finds them, and the next run would create duplicates (both seen in T2.3). So:

- Every server-side save goes through `Support\ContentWriter::write(Closure $callback)`, which takes one global cache lock (`tolk:write`), refreshes Stache's in-process memory, suspends Statamic's git listeners (`GitListeners::suspended()`, a `try` / `finally` around `Statamic\Git\Subscriber::disable()` / `enable()`; core's own `withoutListeners()` does not re-enable them when the callback throws), runs the callback, and releases the lock.
- The refresh (T2.3, `07-spike-findings.md` "Stache and parallel writers"): core's between-jobs reset (`resetMemoizedState()` on every Stache store and on every index in `app('stache.indexes')`), then each store's and child store's memoized `keys` and `usedIndexes` set to `null` (protected properties of `Statamic\Stache\Stores\Store`, cleared through a closure bound to that class; no public method does it), then all of Blink (`Blink::clearResolvedInstance(\Statamic\Support\Blink::class)`). Without the second step a save skips indexes that another process started using; without the third, `existsIn()` answers from memory and a localization can be created twice. The lock is needed as well: the refresh alone lost entries when saves overlapped.
- The target is resolved **inside** the callback, from fresh queries. Never save an entry instance that was loaded before the lock was taken. Assembly (§A8 step 4) may run before the lock, from a target the caller resolved after the units ran, so `PlanApplier` takes the lock only when a field is left to save: a review without corrections and a strict plan whose every field failed return without it. That target may still hold a source as it was before another process saved it, because `fresh()` does not drop what core keeps in this process's Blink (a localization's origin in `origin-Entry-{id}`, an entry's localizations in `entry-descendants-{id}`, a global set's variables in `global-set-localizations-{id}`, form blueprints in the store `blueprints.found`; `07-spike-findings.md` "Stache and parallel writers"). So `Target::apply()` checks every field's scope and source again on the target it resolves inside the lock, after the refresh (step 1), and no save depends on what `fresh()` sees. Runs claim, resolve and assemble inside their own lock (T8.5). The editor's delivery saves nothing and takes no lock, but before a unit delivers a field it calls `ContentWriter::refreshStache()` and resolves the plan's target again (`EditorTranslation::target()`, the check the unit endpoint makes before the unit runs), because this process may still hold the old source, or a localization, origin or term another request deleted or detached, and that assembly is its only source check; a target that is gone (the origin or the term deleted, or the origin left without a localization in the site, deleted or detached) or a localization that is not the one the plan's reference names (deleted and created again) answers 410 `target_missing` (T5.4). A unit that completes no field reads no content and does not refresh.
- Translation work (planning and units) only reads content and may run in parallel. Applying never does.

**Applying one target** (`Target::apply(array $fields, ApplyOptions $options)`, always inside `ContentWriter::write()`):

1. Resolve the target fresh. Every field must still be in scope and its source value must still have the fingerprint it was assembled from (`AssembledField::$sourceHash`, the plan's `source_hash`), the check assembly makes (`Translation\SourceCheck`); otherwise `SourceChanged` is thrown before anything is written, state included. Then `StateWriter::confirm()` it. Assembly's walk check (the same value with another blueprint walk) is not repeated: a blueprint changed between an assembly before the lock and the save goes unnoticed. Runs assemble under their lock, and `TargetTranslator` serves only `tolk:lang` and `tolk:forms`, whose fields have no blueprint.
2. For each field, compare the target's current text fingerprint with the plan's `target_before`. If it changed since planning, somebody edited the field in the meantime: skip it with reason `target_changed`, unless `overwrite_manual` is set. A reviewed field (`AssembledField::$fromTarget`, set by `Pipeline::reviewed()`) is skipped whatever `overwrite_manual` says: its value is the text the plan read with the corrections, not a translation of the source, so writing it would undo the edit. A target that already holds what the field writes (`AssembledField::$targetHash`, `null` for a removed field, which the target-side walk keeps equal to the normalised value's, step 3) has not changed: a run's attempt at the plan saved it and then threw (an `EntrySaved` listener of the host, the confirm of step 4), so its next attempt writes it again instead of reading its own output as an edit, also when the editor's confirm promoted the pending entries meanwhile.
3. Normalise each value the way a load and save in the CP would, so a later CP save does not rewrite it: `$blueprint->setParent($target)->fields()->addValues([$handle => $value])->preProcess()->process()->values()[$handle]`. Set the parent right before `fields()`, because all entries of a collection share one blueprint object. The round trip is idempotent (T2.3). It gives Replicator and Grid rows and Bard sets an id, Replicator rows `enabled`, and every set field missing from a Replicator row or a Bard set its default, text defaults included. The ids it gives are random, and the assembled value has none where the origin stores none (content written by code), so before the round trip `Content\RowIds::keep()` gives each such row or set the id the target's own row or set at the same position has, when it is of the same set type (Bard sets by their position among the sets), at any depth: the ids the target already has are kept, and a retranslation does not rewrite them all. The editor's delivery does the same before it pre-processes (§A8 step 5). It does not add the attributes the browser's Bard component adds. Compute `target_hash` from the normalised value. Because the target-side walk (§A3) leaves out fields that equal their default and the round trip changes no other text, the normalised value has the same target leaves as the assembled one, and the `segments` of the assembled field still line up with it.
4. Write a `pending` entry for each field, save, then `StateWriter::confirm()` (which promotes them).

**Entries** (`EntryTarget`):

- Source: the origin of the localization. For a missing localization: the root of the family, or its localization in the run's `source_site` when one is given.
- Create: check `existsIn()` first; `makeLocalization()` does not guard (R4 §2.2). Give the result an id right away (`->id(Stache::generateId())`): an unsaved entry reads its origin from the Blink key `origin-Entry-`, which every unsaved entry shares, and its blueprint from a key built from its object id, which PHP reuses once an object is freed (statamic/cms#15509; before 6.35.0 the key was shared outright). Two unsaved localizations can therefore read each other's origin and blueprint, inside `save()` too (T2.3). With an id it still saves as a new entry. A new localization is first saved with no data of its own and as a draft, as the CP's localize action does, so it has a reference before any field is written; the fields are then written as for an existing one. Tree placement happens in `makeLocalization()`'s after-save callback, which looks the localized parent up when `makeLocalization()` is called; run items depend on their parent's item so children land under their localized parent (§A12). Core's callback appends the page to the end of its level, and for an orderable collection (`max_depth: 1`) it saves no tree at all, so the tree lists the new entry wherever its query returns it. Runs apply items in the order their translations finish, so `EntryTarget` registers its own after-save callback right after core's, which moves the new page, in the same save and the same write, to its origin's position among the pages already at that level: right after the nearest preceding origin sibling whose localization is there, else right before the nearest following one, else at the end, and never to index 0 of the top level when the structure expects a root. Each origin sibling, nearest first, is looked up within the collection and its family's page in the target site found with `in()`; the first one at the level decides, so placing a page costs a few queries per sibling it passes, never one `Entry::find()` per page of the level, which in an orderable collection is every entry (review R35). Pages already in the tree are never moved, so a target tree the editors reordered keeps its order. It saves the tree with `tree($branches)->save()` when the page moved, and always for an orderable collection. Localizations made by core's own localize action (the form's Sites panel) stay where core puts them.
- Root page: on a collection whose structure expects a root (`structure.root: true`), the first entry placed in a site's empty tree becomes that site's home page, and a later entry whose localized parent is that page goes to the top level (T2.3). So into a target site whose tree is empty, only the localization of the origin tree's root may be created. Any other entry fails with `RootMissing` ("Translate {root title} into {site} first.", followed by why: the first entry saved into the empty tree would become its home page) before anything is saved; the exception carries the root page's edit URL for a link. Runs order the root's item first (§A12, T8.3), and single-target flows check the same condition before planning, so no tokens are spent. A tree that is not empty is used as it is.
- Write: `set($handle, $value)` per field, `remove($handle)` for a cleared field. Never `data([...])`.
- Slug (D16): a localization created in this operation (or by an earlier attempt at the same plan that threw, `ApplyOptions::$createdEarlier`) keeps the origin's slug, which `makeLocalization()` copies, except in a collection of the `translated_slugs` setting. There, once the fields are written and before they are saved, its slug becomes `Str::slug($title, '-', $site->lang())` (Statamic's `Str`, as `EntriesController::resolveSlug()` does on a CP save) of the written title, or of `autoGeneratedTitle()` on a collection that generates its titles; it stays the origin's when the title was not written in this operation, or was written `incomplete` (with a warning). When another entry of the collection in the target site already uses the slug, append `-2`, `-3`, … and add a warning naming the slug it wanted; with translated slugs, the creation's save makes the origin's slug unique without a warning, and the warning is about the final slug. The second save moves the file (`ExistsAsFile` deletes the initial path). With revisions on, the slug is set on the working-copy view that is saved or published, and `ApplyResult::$slug` reads that view. Existing slugs are never touched. A localization an earlier attempt created gets the translated slug only while its slug is still the origin's or the translated one, either with or without a suffix of `-2`, `-3`, …, as the creation or that attempt's save left it: a slug somebody gave it between the attempts is kept.
- Published (D10): only for a localization created in this operation, or by an earlier attempt at the same plan that threw (`createdEarlier`). `draft`, unless the operation asks for `mirror`, the user may publish (`$user->can('publish', $entry)`; CLI runs have no user and may), and every in-scope field was delivered complete (each in-scope field whose source value has leaves was applied in this operation, and none is `incomplete`; T4.16); then the origin's state. Existing localizations keep theirs. On a localization an earlier attempt created, `mirror` never unpublishes: when it is published and its origin is not, somebody published it between the attempts, and it stays published.
- Revisions (D26, R4 §11), when `$entry->revisionsEnabled()`:
  - a new localization is always created with `store([...])` (a draft with a revision) and, when it is to be published, published through `publish([...])`, never saved directly as published;
  - a published localization is updated in its working copy (`fromWorkingCopy()`, set fields, `makeWorkingCopy()->user($user)->message(…)->save()`) and the live entry is left alone;
  - `publish_changes` calls `publish([...])` afterwards only when no working copy existed before this operation and the user may publish. `publish()` publishes the whole working copy, so an existing one could contain somebody else's unapproved edits; in that case leave the changes unpublished and add a warning.
  - an unpublished localization is saved directly, unless it has a working copy, which then gets the fields, because the target's values are read from it.
  - Built in T9.1: the target's own values are read from `fromWorkingCopy()`, and fields are written into that view; the view is saved directly only for a draft with no working copy (the view is then the live entry), else as a working copy. Core's `EntriesController::update()` decides on the view's `published()` alone, but restoring a revision saved while unpublished (an `unpublish` or creation revision) of a published entry leaves a working copy that says unpublished (`RestoreEntryRevisionController`), and saving that view directly would unpublish the live entry and keep the working copy, which the next run reads stale and translates again (review R30). The addon saves the creation's revision itself, as `store()` does (the draft save, then `makeRevision()->user($user)->save()`), because `store()` returns the revision's result and so hides a save that a listener cancelled. The working copy and the publish revision carry the message "Translated from {source site} with Tolk", the creation's revision none, as with the CP's localize action. The working copy is saved without `updateLastModified()`, as the CP does (revisions leave `updated_at` and `updated_by` out). A revision is stored per second (`{key}/{timestamp}.yaml`), so a localization created and published in the same second keeps only its publish revision. `RevisionSaved` is one of the content events of Statamic's git subscriber, which `ContentWriter` suspends with the others.
- Save: `updateLastModified($user)` when a user is known, then `save()`. A `false` result (a listener vetoed) is an error; so is a `false` from a revision's `save()` or from `publish()`.

**Terms** (`TermTarget`): the source is the term in the taxonomy's default site (the first site of `$taxonomy->sites()`); every other site of the taxonomy is a target and always exists (R4 §3.1). Write with `$term->in($site)->merge($values)` and `save()`. Term slugs are never localized (D16). `updateLastModified()` needs a user on a `LocalizedTerm` (R4 §16.2 item 19). `hasOverride()` is `$localized->data()->has($handle)`.

**Global sets** (`GlobalsTarget`): the source is the origin configured on the set for that site (`$set->origins()`). A site with no origin configured is not translatable; the audit reports it. Write with `$set->in($site)->merge($values)->save()`. Origins are read from the set, never from `Variables::origin()`: the Stache can keep variables with an old copy of the set, whose origins are stale (T9.3).

**Navigation** (`NavTarget`): trees are independent per site with no inheritance (R4 §5.1), so the whole tree is treated as one field named `tree`. The source is the tree of the default site, or of the run's `source_site` when the nav has a tree there. The target tree is rebuilt from the source tree: branch `title` and the text fields of the nav blueprint's `data` are translated; `url` is kept; each `entry` id is mapped to the localization in the target site (a branch must hold the entry of its own site). A branch whose entry has no localization there is dropped when no branches below it remain and becomes a text-only branch (titled with its entry's title, translated with the tree) when some do, with a warning either way. The root page of a nav that `expectsRoot()` (its first branch) is never dropped: without a localization it becomes a text-only root the same way, without children, because dropping it would make the next branch the root, and core's `validateTree()` rejects a root with children on every read. Branch ids are preserved. The source value is the source tree **already mapped this way** (T9.4): its fingerprint changes when a missing localization appears later, so the tree reads outdated, and the saved tree's leaves line up with the plan's segments.
