  - `src/Translation/PlanStore.php`, cache-backed on `config('tolk.cache_store')`, TTL `plan_ttl`, storing **arrays only** (rule 12). Keys: `tolk:plan:{id}` and `tolk:plan:{id}:unit:{unitId}`. `put(Plan)`, `get(string $id): ?Plan`, `putOutcome(string $planId, UnitOutcome $outcome)`, `outcome(string $planId, string $unitId): ?UnitOutcome`, `forget(Plan $plan)` (removes the plan and every outcome reachable from it).
  - `Unit`: `id`, `kind` (`UnitKind`), `segmentIds`, `depth`, `repair` (0–2), `capMultiplier`, `notes` (segment id → note), `useFallback`. `UnitOutcome`: `unit` (id), `translations` (id → text), `corrections` (list of `id`, `before`, `after`, `reason`), `failed` (id → reason), `warnings`, `usage` (usage map), `spawned` (list of `Unit`). Both have `toArray()` / `fromArray()`.
  - `src/Translation/SegmentValidator.php`: `validate(array $segment, string $translation): ?string` using `Placeholders::validate()` plus the `max_length` check, returning a problem description to be used as the repair `note`.
  - `src/Translation/Pipeline.php`:
    - `units(Plan $plan): array`: the initial batch units plus every unit spawned by stored outcomes, found by following `spawned`.
    - `runnable(Plan $plan): array`: units without an outcome. `isFinished(Plan $plan): bool`.
    - `runUnit(Plan $plan, string $unitId): UnitOutcome`: returns the stored outcome if there is one. Otherwise takes `Cache::lock("tolk:unit:{plan}:{unit}", timeout + 10)`; a second caller gets `UnitBusy`. Makes one `Engine` call, applies §A10, stores and returns the outcome. Transient failures store nothing: count them per unit in the cache (`tolk:unit-fails:{plan}:{unit}`), and from `retry.max_attempts` on use the fallback model when `ModelCatalog::resolveFallback()` gave one and its provider is not paused (`Throttle::pausedFor()`). `ProviderRejected` is rethrown.
    - What the engine gives `runUnit` (T4.12): `EngineCall::attempt` = 1 + the unit's counted transient failures, so the backoff grows. `ProviderPaused` has already paused the provider. `ProviderTimeout` extends `ProviderUnavailable`: on a unit with several segments catch it and store an outcome that spawns halves; on one segment let it propagate. `RequestRejected::tooLarge()` (HTTP 413) spawns halves the same way; any other `RequestRejected`, and a 413 on one segment, fails the unit's segments with `request_rejected` (the engine has already logged the request's shape). Both waiting exceptions extend `TransientProviderFailure`, whose `seconds` is the wait.
    - Plan segments are keyed by id and carry no `id` key; a unit's `EngineCall::$segments` adds it, and gives the prompt's `field` the segment's `label` (the label path, "Body › FAQ › Question"), which says what the text is for; the segment's own `field` is the handle. Read the options from `$plan->options` (`PlanOptions`: `translator`, `fallback`, `profile`, `strict`, …).
    - Spawned unit ids are deterministic: `b3.a` / `b3.b` for halves, `b3~s7` and `b3~s7~2` for repairs of segment `s7`, `b3?` for missing ids, `b3^` for the single-segment retry with a doubled cap, `b3.v` for review (T7.3).
    - Every call records usage: return it in the outcome and hand it to the `UsageRecorder` callback the caller passed (the monthly log and run totals are built in T8.2).
    - `translation(Plan $plan, string $segmentId): ?string` and `failure(Plan $plan, string $segmentId): ?string`, resolved over all outcomes as §A8 step 3 says.
    - `readyFields(Plan $plan): array`: handles for which no runnable unit holds one of their segments.
    - `assemble(Plan $plan, Target $target, string $handle): AssembledField`: re-read the source value and compare its fingerprint with `fields[handle].source_hash`; if it changed since planning, throw `SourceChanged`. A `clear` field assembles to "remove the override" (or a copy of the source value when the source has structure but no text). Otherwise inject the translations in leaf order; a leaf whose segment failed keeps its source text, sets `incomplete`, records `null` in `segments` and adds the warning `left_untranslated` with the reason, unless `options.strict`, in which case throw `FieldFailed`. Return `handle`, `value` or `remove`, `sourceHash`, `targetHash` (`ofLeaves` of the produced value's target-side leaves, §A3: `StatusResolver::targetHash()`), `segments`, `identical`, `incomplete`, `corrections`, `warnings`.
    - Alignment guard: after injecting, walk the produced value as a target (§A3) and compare its leaves with the injected texts, one by one and in order. Reuse on a later update (§A7) depends on this one-to-one match. If it does not hold, return `segments: []` so the field is fully retranslated next time instead of being reused out of alignment.
  - Built (planner's details): `Unit` and `UnitOutcome` (final readonly, `src/Translation/`) also carry `Unit::$missingRetry` (a `?` unit, and its halves: ids missing again fail with `missing`) and `UnitOutcome::$model` (the model that was called; `null` for a split after a timeout); outcome `warnings` are segment id → list of messages, so `assemble()` gives each field its own; the failure reasons are constants on `UnitOutcome` (`INVALID`, `MISSING`, `TOO_LONG`, `REFUSED`, `REQUEST_REJECTED`); `UnitOutcome::reasonLabel()` words them, and `unavailable`, through `__()` for the warnings, `FieldFailed`'s message and a skipped review's warning, and the run page's `describe.js` names a field that failed in strict mode with the same English text, so one translation covers both. `Unit` builds its follow-ups (`halves()`, the first taking the odd segment; `repairOf()`, `{id}~{segment}` then `{id}~2`, each repair with its note and `repair` + 1; `withDoubledCap()`; `forMissing()`), each with `useFallback` set when this call ran on the fallback, so a fallback answer's follow-ups stay on the fallback; `canSplit()` is more than one segment and `depth` below `MAX_SPLIT_DEPTH` (3). A unit that cannot split is handled like one segment: `length` / `empty` → `{id}^` once (`capMultiplier` 1 → 2), then `too_long` for all its segments; `refused` → `refused`; a timeout propagates. Splits after a timeout or a 413 count no failure. `PlanStore` owns every plan key: `units(Plan)` (the initial batches, each followed depth first by what its outcome spawned, with the outcome or `null`; `Pipeline::units()` and `runnable()` keep that order), `failures()` / `countFailure()` (`tolk:unit-fails:…`, TTL `plan_ttl`), `lock()` (`tolk:unit:…`), and `forget()` also drops the failure counts. `runUnit(Plan, string $unitId, ?Closure $recordUsage = null)`: the recorder receives `(ModelRef, Usage)` per call; an unknown unit throws `UnknownUnit`; the outcome is checked again once the lock is held; a review unit throws `LogicException` until T7.3. `SegmentValidator` also exposes `placeholders()` (§A3) and `length()` (`max_length` counted on what a reader sees: `Placeholders::strip()`, new, then unescaped in escaping kinds); `validate()` joins both problems into one repair note. A translation over its limit with valid placeholders gets one repair (only when `repair` is 0) and is then accepted with a warning. `AssembledField` (final readonly) also carries `plan` (the plan id), `targetBefore` and `translator` (the model that translated most of the field's segments in this plan, `null` when none was called), which T4.16 needs for the pending entry. `assemble()` throws `SourceChanged` also when the field left the target's scope or its source walk no longer gives the planned segment texts (a changed blueprint), `LogicException` while a runnable unit holds one of its segments and for a review plan (T7.4), and `FieldFailed` (`handle`, `reasons`: segment id → reason) in strict mode. A clear removes the override (`remove: true`, `value` and `targetHash` `null`) when the source value's canonical form (§A4) is empty, and otherwise copies the source value with `identical: true`. `identical` is true when the injected texts equal the source leaves' texts, so a field left wholly in the source language is also identical. The alignment guard compares what a reader sees (placeholders stripped, escapes undone), because the handler re-tokenizes the value it wrote: moved atoms are renumbered and a literal `&` comes back escaped. Warnings are English strings with the field's label path and a short excerpt of the source text.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Translation/PipelineTest.php` with faked agents and `useFileCache()`:
    - a two-batch plan runs to finished and assembles every field; `runUnit` twice makes one model call; plan and outcomes survive the file store;
    - (T4.13 helpers: a `fakeTranslations()` transform that returns `null` leaves an id out of the answer; `modelCalls()` counts model calls)
    - a missing id spawns `b1?` and is then translated; a dropped placeholder spawns a repair with a note, which succeeds; two bad repairs fail the segment, and the field is assembled with that leaf in the source language, `incomplete` true and a `null` in `segments`; with `strict` the same field throws `FieldFailed`;
    - `length` on a three-segment unit spawns two halves; on a single segment it spawns one retry with `capMultiplier` 2 and then fails the segment `too_long`;
    - `refused` on a multi-segment unit spawns halves; on a single segment it fails that segment;
    - a paused provider throws `ProviderPaused` and stores nothing; after `retry.max_attempts` transient failures the fallback model is used; a fallback on the paused provider is not;
    - `ProviderRejected` propagates;
    - an origin edited between plan and assemble raises `SourceChanged`;
    - a translation with no letters (no longer a leaf) produces `segments: []`;
    - two overlapping `runUnit` calls for the same unit make one model call (the second gets `UnitBusy`).

- [x] **T4.16 `Support\ContentWriter` and `EntryTarget::apply()`**
  - `src/Support/ContentWriter.php`: `write(Closure $callback): mixed` as §A11: `Cache::lock('tolk:write', write.lock_seconds)->block(write.wait_seconds)`, then the Stache refresh of §A11, then `GitListeners::suspended($callback)`, releasing the lock in `finally`. Move `refreshStache()` from `tests/Feature/Contracts/StacheRefreshTest.php` into `ContentWriter` as a public static `refreshStache()` and make that test call it, so the contract test guards the production code. A lock that cannot be taken in time throws `WriterBusy`. `ContentWriter::active(): bool` says whether the current process is inside `write()`.
  - `ApplyOptions`: `user` (nullable), `newStatus` (`draft` or `mirror`), `publishChanges`, `overwriteManual`, `createdEarlier` (an earlier attempt at the same plan created the localization and threw, so step 6 finishes it as created in step 2; runs set it, §A11 `ApplyOptions`, §A12 "Applying"). `ApplyResult`: `ref` (`TargetRef`), `created`, `slug` (nullable), `applied` (handles), `skipped` (handle → reason), `warnings`. Both exist since T4.7 (`src/Targets/`, final readonly, because the `Target` contract names them; `ApplyOptions` rejects a `newStatus` other than `draft` or `mirror`).
  - `EntryTarget::apply(array $fields, ApplyOptions $options): ApplyResult`, where `$fields` is a list of `AssembledField` (T4.15: each carries `plan`, `targetBefore` from the plan and `translator`, which the pending entry of step 5 takes along with `sourceHash`, `segments`, `identical` and `incomplete`; `reviewer` stays `null` until T7.3). It must be called inside `ContentWriter::write()`; throw `LogicException` when `ContentWriter::active()` is false. Steps, as §A11:
    1. `fresh()`, then `StateWriter::confirm()`.
    2. Find or make the localization (`existsIn()` first). A new one gets its id right after `makeLocalization()` (§A11 "Create") and is saved once right away, with no data of its own and as a draft, exactly as the CP's localize action does (R4 §2.3). It then has a reference and its place in the tree before any field is written, so the pending entries of step 5 can be recorded for it. Before that first save, apply the slug rule of §A11: keep the origin's slug, and add a suffix only on a collision. Before making it, throw `RootMissing` when `rootMissing()` is true. `EntryTarget::rootMissing(): bool` is true when the localization does not exist, the collection's structure expects a root, the target site's tree is empty, and the source entry is not the root of the source site's tree (§A11 "Root page").
    3. Per field, as §A11 step 2: skip with `target_changed` when the current text fingerprint (`StatusResolver::targetHash()`, `null` without an override) differs from `target_before` and `overwriteManual` is off. A changed reviewed field (`AssembledField::$fromTarget`) is skipped whatever `overwriteManual` says, and a target that already holds the field's output (`AssembledField::$targetHash`, `null` for a removal) has not changed.
    4. Normalise each value as §A11 step 3 (the round trip of `tests/Feature/Contracts/RoundTripTest.php`) and recompute its `targetHash` from the normalised value's target-side leaves (§A3).
    5. `recordPending()` for each field. A removed field (`remove: true`) has `targetHash: null`, and a pending entry needs a target hash, so it gets none; after the save, replace its record with one that keeps only its history (`StateWriter::recordRemoved($ref, [$handle])`, which moves the machine pair and the outputs of the pending entries there, §A5), so it reads not applicable while the source is empty, untranslated once the source has text again, and outdated when a stale form or a restored revision puts a removed output back.
    6. `set()` / `remove()`; published state and slug for a localization created in step 2, or by an earlier attempt at the same plan that threw (`createdEarlier`, where nobody changed them since; §A11 "Slug", "Published"), including the publish-permission rule; `updateLastModified($user)` when there is a user; `save()`. A `false` result throws `ApplyFailed`.
    7. `StateWriter::confirm()` again, which promotes the pending entries.
  - Revisions are handled in T9.1; until then, an entry on a revision-enabled collection throws `NotSupportedYet` (the exception exists since T4.7 with `contentType()`; add a `revisions()` constructor).
  - Slug collision check: query the collection's entries in the target site by slug; on a collision, add a suffix and a warning.
  - `authorize()` as §A14 is already built (T4.7): the target site's `view` ability, then `edit` on the localization, or on the source while the localization is missing.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Targets/EntryApplyTest.php`:
    - creates a localization whose own data holds the given field keys and no other field (with a user, `updated_at` and `updated_by` are added by core); the new localization is a draft by default; with `mirror` it copies the origin's state, but stays a draft for a user without the publish permission and when a field is incomplete;
    - a new localization keeps the origin's slug, even though its title is translated; when another entry in the target site already has that slug, it gets `-2` and a warning; an existing localization keeps its slug and published state;
    - a structured collection places the child under its localized parent;
    - on a collection with `structure.root: true` and an empty target tree, applying a non-root entry throws `RootMissing` and saves nothing, and applying the origin root first, then the others, gives the target the origin's tree;
    - a new localization has its id before its first save: in one process, apply missing targets of two collections with different blueprints alternately; an `EntryCreating` listener sees an id, and a blueprint and origin of the entry's own collection, every time;
    - inside `ContentWriter::write()` the callback sees what another process cached: plant a path and an index usage in the Stache cache as `StacheRefreshTest` does, and both are visible in the callback;
    - a field edited between plan and apply is skipped with `target_changed` and the human value is intact;
    - a `clear` field removes the override;
    - an exception thrown between `save()` and the final `confirm()` (simulate it) leaves a pending entry, and the next `StatusResolver::resolve()` reads the field as machine and current;
    - a rewritten value keeps its status: apply a Replicator-shaped fixture, run the CP round trip over the stored value, and the field is still machine and current;
    - calling `apply()` outside `ContentWriter::write()` throws; the git listener fires zero times during the write and is active again afterwards;
    - a user without access to the target site fails `authorize()`.

- [x] **T4.17 `Translation\TargetTranslator` and `TargetTranslated`**
  - `translate(Target $target, PlanOptions $options, ApplyOptions $apply): TargetResult`: `StateWriter::confirm()`; plan; return a skipped result when the plan is empty; store the plan; run runnable units in order until finished, sleeping through `ProviderPaused` / `ProviderUnavailable` (both extend `TransientProviderFailure`, whose `seconds` is the wait, T4.12; this class is the synchronous, single-target path, so it may wait; give up after 10 waits); assemble the ready fields with `$target->fresh()`, resolved once after the units ran, so a source edited meanwhile raises `SourceChanged` (`assemble()` reads the target it is given); save through `PlanApplier` (T8.5), as `ContentWriter::write(fn () => $target->apply(…))`, whose `apply()` resolves the target fresh inside the lock and checks the sources again (§A11 step 1); forget the plan; fire `Events\TargetTranslated` (reference, handles, models, usage map).
  - On `SourceChanged`, re-plan once and continue; a second one fails the target. `apply()` throws it too, before saving anything, for a field that left the target's scope or whose source changed after assembly (T4.16, §A11 step 1).
  - Built in T4.16: `ContentWriter::write()` and `active()` are static; `apply()` may also throw `RootMissing` (its `rootId` names the root entry), `WriterBusy`, `ApplyFailed` and `NotSupportedYet::revisions()`. `ApplyResult::$warnings` holds only the apply's own warnings (the slug suffix), so `TargetResult::$warnings` adds each `AssembledField`'s warnings itself. `apply()` dispatches no git commit (§A11 suspends the listener); the caller commits (T4.18, `RunProcessor::finalize()`).
  - `TargetResult`: `status` (`done`, `skipped`, `failed`), `ref`, `created`, `fields` (handle → `applied`, `incomplete`, or a skip or failure reason), `corrections`, `warnings`, `usage` (usage map).
  - An `EntryTarget` whose `rootMissing()` is true fails with reason `root_missing` and its message before planning, so no tokens are spent (§A11 "Root page").
  - Built (planner's details): `TargetTranslator` is final readonly (`Planner`, `PlanStore`, `Pipeline`, `StateWriter`). `TargetResult` (final readonly, `src/Translation/`) has `status` (new enum `Enums\TargetResultStatus`: `done`, `skipped`, `failed`), `ref` (null while the target does not exist), `created`, `fields` (in blueprint order: `applied`, `incomplete`, the plan's skip reasons, `apply()`'s `target_changed`, or the first segment reason of a strict field failure such as `refused`), `corrections` (handle → list), `warnings`, `usage` (`UsageMap`), and also `reason` and `error` for a skipped or failed target; the reasons are constants on `TargetResult` (`nothing_to_do`, `target_changed`, `root_missing`, `source_changed`, `provider_unavailable`, `fields_failed`, `writer_busy`, `apply_failed`, `not_supported`); `appliedFields()` lists the saved handles. The root check is the new public `EntryTarget::ensureRootExists()` (throws `RootMissing` with its message; `apply()` uses it too). Waits are counted **in a row**, as §A12: a unit that succeeds resets the count, and the eleventh transient failure in a row fails the target with `provider_unavailable` ("The AI provider [p] was still unavailable after 10 waits."); each wait sleeps `max(1, seconds)` through Laravel's `Sleep`. Status: `done` when any field was saved, else `failed` when a field failed strictly (`fields_failed`), else `skipped` (`target_changed`). Fields that fail strictly are left out of `apply()`, and when every field fails nothing is saved (no empty localization). `RootMissing`, a second `SourceChanged`, `WriterBusy`, `ApplyFailed` and `NotSupportedYet` (revisions) become a `failed` result with the exception's message; `ProviderRejected` propagates, because every other target would meet it too, and so does `UnitBusy`. The plan is forgotten in a `finally`, on every path. Usage is collected through `runUnit()`'s recorder closure, so it includes the calls of a plan dropped after `SourceChanged`. `TargetTranslated` (final readonly, `Dispatchable`) fires only for `done`: `ref`, `handles` (the saved fields), `models` (the model refs called for the target, the keys of `usage`) and `usage`.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Translation/TargetTranslatorTest.php` with faked agents: translate a missing localization to current; run again and nothing is sent; edit the origin and run mode `Update`: only the changed field is sent; a manually edited field is left alone; `overwriteManual` replaces it; the event carries the reference, handles, models and a usage map (faked agents report zero tokens, R2 §13.1, so assert its shape).

- [x] **T4.18 `tolk:translate`, first version**
  - `src/Console/Commands/TranslateCommand.php`, signature `tolk:translate` (rule 15), options for now: `--entry=*`, `--collection=*`, `--site=*` (required), `--mode=missing` (`missing`, `update`, `all`), `--force` (overwrite manual edits), `--publish` (new localizations mirror the origin's state instead of being drafts), `--translator=`, `--dry-run`, `--yes`.
  - Flow: validate sites, handles and the model ref; fail with a clear message when no provider is configured and the fake engine is off. With the fake engine on and no key (the sandbox has none), `PlanOptions::fromSettings()` still throws `NoProviderConfigured` from `ModelCatalog::resolveTranslator()` unless `translator_model` or `--translator` is set (T4.14): make `resolveTranslator()` fall back to the quality model of the first provider when `FakeEngine::enabled()` and none is configured, with a test. Enumerate entry families (root entries of the collections, or the families of the given ids) × target sites. A structured collection's families are enumerated in the source site's tree order, depth first, so the root page and every parent come before their children (§A11 "Root page" and tree placement). For each target print one line and, unless `--dry-run`, call `TargetTranslator::translate()` in-process, one target after the other. `--dry-run` plans each target and prints fields, segments and characters without calling the model.
  - Content saved inside `ContentWriter::write()` has no git commit listener (§A11), so after the loop, when any target was applied, call `GitListeners::commit()` once (T4.16).
  - `TargetTranslator::translate()` returns a `TargetResult` (T4.17: `status` is a `TargetResultStatus`, `fields` holds `incomplete` for fields left partly untranslated, `reason` and `error` say why a target was skipped or failed) and throws `ProviderRejected` (bad key, no credits, unknown model): catch it, print its message, stop the loop and exit 1, since every other target would fail the same way. Until T9.1, a target in a collection with revisions enabled fails with `not_supported` only after its units ran, so tokens are spent on it.
  - End with a summary table (done, skipped, failed, fields left incomplete) and the fields that were out of scope with their reason (`Target::explainFields()`, T4.7), so "nothing was translated because the fields are not localizable" is visible. Exit code 1 when any target failed.
  - Phase 8 replaces the in-process loop with the run engine; keep the enumeration and the output code reusable.
  - Built (planner's details): enumeration is `Targets\EntryFamilies` (`roots(collections, entryIds)`: the roots of the collections' entries (`lazy()`, `isRoot()`) and of the given entries (`root()`), each once, grouped by collection in first-named order, a structured collection's roots in the order of its trees (every site of the collection, depth first; roots in no tree after the others); `targets(roots, sites)`: family by family, each requested site the collection has except the root's own, as `TargetFactory::fromDescription()` arrays). Output is the trait `Console\Concerns\ReportsTranslations` (`startReport()` first in `handle()`, because one command instance serves every `Artisan::call()` in a process; `targetLabel()` "{source title} → {site name}", `startTarget()` writes it before the call, `reportResult()` ends the line with `done` / `skipped` / `failed` and the details (created, field count, `left incomplete`, `manual edits kept`, `edited meanwhile, kept`) and prints the warnings below; `reportPlan()` / `planSize()` for dry runs; `summarize()`: the table Done / Skipped / Failed / Fields left incomplete, a table of the incomplete fields, then `summarizeOutOfScope()`: every out-of-scope field but `slug`, as `container.handle`, fieldtype and a readable reason, plus a hint when one is `not_localizable`). Validation order: sites, mode (`review` refused until T7.6), collections (unknown is an error, `excluded_collections` skipped with a warning, as T8.3), entries (an entry of an excluded collection is skipped the same way), translator, provider key (skipped under the fake engine). A collection that lacks a requested site gets a warning; no target prints "Nothing to translate." and exits 0. Without `--publish` a new localization follows the `new_localization_status` setting. The confirmation is "Translate and save them?" with default yes, so `-n` and a closed stdin proceed; in an agent's shell it waits for input unless `--yes` is given. A dry run plans without `StateWriter::confirm()` (nothing is written) and reports a target as "would fail" with the `RootMissing` message unless the root's target in that site is planned before it. `ProviderRejected` ends the line, prints the message and the summary, exits 1. The commit after the loop sits in a `finally` and uses the new `ContentWriter::COMMIT_MESSAGE` (`Content translated`). `ModelCatalog::resolveTranslator()` falls back to the quality model of `ModelRef::PROVIDERS[0]` (Anthropic) under the fake engine.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Console/TranslateCommandTest.php` with faked agents: `--dry-run` translates nothing; a real run on one collection and one site makes every target current for its text fields; `--mode=update` after editing an origin touches only that entry; `--force` overwrites a manual field and plain `update` does not; `--publish` mirrors the origin's state; an unknown site exits 1. In the sandbox, with `TOLK_FAKE_ENGINE=true`: `php please tolk:translate --collection=pages --site=el` fills `title`, `intro`, `seo_title` and `seo_description` of the Greek pages with `[el] …` text as drafts, reports `content` as an unsupported fieldtype, and a second run reports nothing to do. The Greek pages tree then has the shape of the English one, with `home` as its root. A test with faked agents checks the same order on a collection with `structure.root: true`, with the root entered last in the origin collection. Reset the sandbox content with git afterwards.

## Phase 5 — Slice 2: the entry editor

Goal: the owner's first use case. Click into another language, be offered a translation, see the text fields fill in, save. Only `text` and `textarea` fields at this point; Phase 6 widens the content. Read R3 §1–§5 and §11 and the "Publish form" findings of T2.2 first. Keep logic in plain modules under `resources/js/editor/` and keep Vue components thin, so the logic can be unit-tested without a browser.

The CP stores what it receives, so compute the pending `target_hash` from the value as it will be stored (the delivered value run through `process()`). The target-side walk leaves those defaults out (§A3), so this hash equals the assembled field's `targetHash` and its `segments` still line up (T6.11 tests it on a Replicator).

    - `origin` (null unless the item has localizable sites below it): `localizations`, one per site the user may access: `site`, `name`, `exists`, `status` (`ItemStatus`), `counts`, `edit_url`;
    - `defaults` (`translator`, `providers_configured`, `fake_engine`) and `offer` (the `offer_on_create` setting).
    - An entry in the middle of a chain gets both blocks.
  - Built in T5.2: `Http\Controllers\Cp\EditorController` exists with every editor method calling `notBuiltYet()` (`abort(501)`); replace `status()` here, and T5.4 replaces the others and deletes the helper. `Http\Requests\EditorStatusRequest` validates `reference` (required string); the route group already requires `translate content`, so the controller adds only the per-item check (`Target::authorize()`, §A14).
  - Built (planner's details): the `Target` contract gained `inheritedValue(string $handle)` (the raw value the form shows for a field the target does not override, read as core's edit page reads it: the source's own data, else its origins'; a root's `null`, `''` and `[]` read as unset, because `ExtractsFromEntryFields` runs `Arr::removeNullValues()` over a root's data, so the field shows its default) and `formFields(array $values, bool $source = false): Fields` (the target's blueprint with the values added, parented to the target's `fromWorkingCopy()`, or with `$source` to the source, as core does for `originValues`). `deliver()` assembles every handle first (so `SourceChanged` and `FieldFailed` propagate before any pending entry is written), returns the handles in the requested order, and each translated payload also carries the field's `warnings`; a `sync` payload's value and meta come from `formFields(inheritedValue…, source: true)`, and it writes no state. It throws `LogicException` for a target that does not exist. The status payload is built by `Http\EditorStatus::for(string $reference, User $user)` and always has every key: `translatable`, `reason` (`single_site`, `unknown_reference`, `no_access`; null when translatable), `localization`, `origin`, `defaults`, `offer`. Order of checks: a reference that does not parse, is not `entry::` (terms come in T9.2) or names no entry is `unknown_reference`; then the per-item check (`Target::authorize()` for a localization; site `view` and `edit` for a root) throws `AuthorizationException`, so a user who cannot edit the entry gets 403; then a collection with fewer than two sites is `single_site`; with neither block (no origin, and no site below the item the user may access) it is `no_access`. The localization block also has `status` (the `ItemStatus`); `fields` holds every in-scope field, `not_applicable` included (an empty PHP array, so `[]` in JSON when there are none); `out_of_scope` is a list of `handle`, `label`, `type`, `reason` for every out-of-scope field except `unsupported_fieldtype` and `slug`. The origin block lists, in the collection's site order and only for sites the user may `view`, the item's direct localizations (`directDescendants()`) and, when the item is the root of its family, the sites the family has no localization in (`exists: false`, `status: missing`, `edit_url: null`); a site whose localization descends from another entry of the family is listed under that entry. `origin` is null when it would list nothing. `defaults.translator` is null on `NoProviderConfigured` or `MissingDefaultModel`, as `GET models`.

- [x] **T5.4 `editor/plan`, `editor/unit`, `editor/confirm`, `editor/baseline`**
  - `EditorController@plan`: body `reference`, `mode`, `overwrite_manual`, `fields`, `skip` (handles the editor has unsaved changes in), `translator`. A `translator` that is not in the catalog is refused unless the user may edit the addon's settings (§A14). Builds `PlanOptions` (profile `web`, the current user), plans, stores, and returns `plan` (id), `units` (initial unit ids with their field handles and characters), `fields`, `skipped`, and `ready`: delivered values for fields that need no model call.
  - `EditorController@unit`: body `plan`, `unit`. Loads the plan (410 with `code: plan_expired` when it is gone), checks it belongs to the user and that the user may still edit the target, raises the PHP time limit to the web timeout plus 15 seconds, runs the unit, delivers every field that became ready, and returns `fields` (handle → value, meta, flags), `spawned` (new unit ids), `failed` (handle → reason, only in strict mode), `warnings`, `usage` and `remaining` (runnable units left).
    - Error mapping: `ProviderPaused` → 429 with `retry_after`; `ProviderUnavailable` → 503 with `retry_after`; `ProviderRejected` → 502 with the sanitised message; `UnitBusy` → 409 with `code: busy`; `SourceChanged` → 409 with `code: source_changed`; `UnknownUnit` (a unit id the plan does not have, T4.15) → 404.
    - The editor endpoints never compute a slug: in the publish form, the browser puts the origin's slug back on a new localization (T5.9).
  - `EditorController@confirm`: body `reference`, `delivered` (the handles the browser filled since the last save and the save was meant to store: still localized, or left out by `leaveOutHidden()`; not a field put back to inheriting, which holds no override either). Calls `StateWriter::confirm($target, commit: true)` and returns the fresh status payload plus `not_saved`: delivered handles that still have an unresolved pending entry (`ConfirmReport::$unresolved`) and no override, which happens when a field condition hid the field and the form did not post it. Core posts a hidden field's handle in `_localized` while the field is localized, without its value, which stores an empty override; the editor's `*.saving` hook takes a field it filled out of `_localized` then (`leaveOutHidden()`, T5.8), so it is listed. A field that held an override before the fill is stored empty by core and not listed (`07-spike-findings.md` "Fields a condition hides").
  - `EditorController@baseline`: body `reference`, optional `fields`, `protect` (bool, also turn machine fields manual, D27). Calls `StateWriter::baseline(..., commit: true)` and returns the fresh status payload.
  - Built in T5.3: `EditorDelivery::deliver($plan, $target, $handles)` returns handle → `{ value, meta, incomplete, warnings }` or `{ value, meta, sync: true }`; it assembles every handle before writing anything, so one field's `FieldFailed` (strict) or `SourceChanged` aborts the whole call: in strict mode deliver the fields one at a time (or catch `FieldFailed` per handle) so a failed field does not hold back the others, and report it in `failed`. The fresh status payload for `confirm` and `baseline` is `Http\EditorStatus::for($reference, User::current())`, which throws `AuthorizationException` (403) for a user who may not edit the item.
  - Built in T5.2: the Form Requests `EditorPlanRequest` (`reference` and `mode` required; `mode` is `missing`, `update` or `all`, since review has its own endpoint (`editor/review`, T7.5); `overwrite_manual` boolean; `fields` nullable list and `skip` list of strings; `translator` nullable, `provider:model` format checked by `Http\Rules\ModelReference`, the catalog check is this task's), `EditorUnitRequest` (`plan`, `unit`), `EditorConfirmRequest` (`reference`, `delivered`) and `EditorBaselineRequest` (`reference`, `fields`, `protect`). `tests/Feature/Cp/EditorRequestsTest.php` covers their validation.
  - Built (planner's details): `Http\EditorTranslation` (final readonly) builds the `plan` and `unit` payloads; `EditorController` resolves the target, authorizes and maps errors (`notBuiltYet()` is gone). Error bodies are `{ message, code }`, the message through `Sanitizer`, plus `retry_after` and a `Retry-After` header on 429 and 503.
    - `plan`: the reference must name an existing localization: 404 `unknown_reference` when it does not parse or names no entry, 422 `not_translatable` for an origin or another content type; 403 without `Target::authorize()`; 422 `no_translator` on `NoProviderConfigured` / `MissingDefaultModel`. `skip` handles get the reason `EditorTranslation::EDITED_IN_FORM`. The catalog check is the rule `Http\Rules\CatalogModel` on `translator` (a 422 validation error): a model outside `ModelCatalog` passes only for `$user->can('editSettings', Addon::get(Settings::PACKAGE))`, which core's `AddonPolicy` grants to super users, `configure addons` and `edit reachweb/statamic-tolk settings`. Response: `plan` (id), `units` (`[{ id, fields, chars }]`, `fields` the handles of the unit's segments in order, `chars` their source length), `fields` (a list of the planned handles, blueprint order), `skipped` (handle → reason), `ready` (handle → the delivery payload of a field with no batched segment).
    - `unit`: 410 `plan_expired`; 403 when the plan's `userId` is null or not the user's id; 410 `target_missing` when the localization no longer exists (`EditorTranslation::target()`: the target built again from the plan's description, with its source, must exist and have the plan's reference), checked before the unit runs and, for a unit that completes a field, again after it ran, after a Stache refresh (§A11): also when its origin or the term was deleted meanwhile, or the localization detached, or deleted and created again; 403 without `authorize()`; then the time limit is raised, only when one is set (`max_execution_time > 0`), to at least `http.timeout.web + 15`. The unit delivers the fields that hold one of its segments and that no runnable unit holds a segment of (`Pipeline::readyFields()`), so a field spanning units is delivered by the unit that completes it; two units completing at the same moment can both deliver it, and a unit that already has an outcome makes no call and delivers its fields again (with `usage` `[]`). `FieldState::withPending()` now replaces an entry of the same plan, so such a field keeps one pending entry. `spawned` has the shape of `units`. `failed` and `warnings` are always present: in a strict plan a field that throws `FieldFailed` is left out and the others are delivered without it (delivery assembles before it writes), with the reason of its first failed segment and the exception's message as a warning; `warnings` is every delivered field's warnings plus those messages, without duplicates. `usage` holds only this request's calls (`UsageMap::toArray()`, keyed `provider:model`). `remaining` is `count(Pipeline::runnable())`. `UnknownUnit` → 404 `unknown_unit`; `ProviderTimeout` is a `ProviderUnavailable` (503).
    - `confirm` and `baseline`: a reference that names no localization (an origin, an unknown entry) writes nothing and returns the status payload with `not_saved: []` / `baselined: 0`; otherwise `authorize()` (403) comes before any write. `not_saved` is as specified: a delivered field whose earlier override was saved unchanged (`T == target_before`) stays pending and is not listed. `baseline` adds `baselined`, the number of fields it changed. Both commit only when state changed.
    - Empty maps encode as `[]` (`skipped`, `ready`, `fields`, `failed`, `usage`), as in T5.3.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Cp/EditorEndpointsTest.php` with faked agents and `useFileCache()`: plan then its units delivers every text field in publish-form shape; `skip` handles are reported as `edited_in_form`; a spawned repair unit is returned and can be run; a second user cannot run another user's unit; an expired plan gives 410; a paused provider gives 429 with `retry_after`; a rejected key gives 502 without the key in the body; confirm after a simulated CP save promotes the pending entries, and after a save of an edited value writes a baseline; `not_saved` lists a delivered field that was not stored; an out-of-catalog model is refused for an ordinary editor.

A user who cannot edit the item gets 403 instead of a payload.

A container counts its mounts, so a new container created before its predecessor of the same name is destroyed survives that destroy. `comparable(value)` and `equalValues(a, b)` (deep; `_id`, key order and keys holding `undefined` do not count).

- [x] **T5.7 Fill module**
  - `resources/js/editor/fill.js`:
    - `snapshot(ctx, handles)`: deep copies of `values[handle]` and `meta[handle]`, whether each handle was in `localizedFields`, and whether the form was dirty.
    - `applyFields(ctx, fields, { reference, lastChangeAt, planValues, delivered })`:
      1. Check that `ctx.reference.value` still equals `reference`, the item the plan was made for. If the editor switched site or entry in the meantime, apply nothing and report `stale` (otherwise a Greek translation could land in the German form).
      2. Wait until at least 600 ms have passed since `lastChangeAt`. The entry form switches dirty tracking off for 500 ms after a site switch, a localization's creation and a save (R3 §1.3, §4.4); after a save the window opens only once the `entry.saved` hooks have resolved, and the term form has none. T2.2 check 6 verified the 600 ms on 6.30.0 and 6.35.0: a fill inside the window is shown but not marked localized, and Save then drops it silently (all of it at 0 ms; at 300 ms all but the Replicator, list and array fields, which mark themselves through their own re-emit).
      3. Per handle: ignore it when `delivered` already has it for this plan (two parallel units can complete the same field); skip it and report `edited` when the form's current comparable value differs from `planValues[handle]`, because the editor typed in it while the model worked; skip it and report `hidden` when core's `hiddenFields` gives it `omitValue` (a condition hides it, so Save would post its handle in `_localized` without its value and store it empty), unless the payload syncs it; otherwise `setFieldMeta` (when meta is given), `setFieldValue`, `desyncField`. Afterwards, if the handle is still not in `ctx.localizedFields.value`, add it and mark the container dirty with `Statamic.$dirty.add()` (R3 §11 iii).
      4. A payload with `sync: true` sets the origin's value and meta and removes the handle from `localizedFields`.
      5. Record in `delivered` the form's value as read back after the fieldtype's update debounce (300 ms after applying), not the payload. Bard re-emits its own JSON after `setFieldValue`, adding attributes such as `textAlign: null`, list `start` and table-cell `colspan` (T2.2 check 5), so a delivered Bard value is never deep-equal to the form's. A read-back whose text is not the filled text (line breaks read as `\n`, as CodeMirror re-emits them) holds what the editor typed meanwhile: then the filled value is recorded, so Undo keeps their text.
    - `restore(ctx, snapshot, delivered)`: one level of undo. For each handle whose current value still equals what was delivered (as recorded in step 5), put the earlier value and meta back and restore its localized flag; leave handles the editor changed since. If nothing differs from the clean values afterwards, clear the dirty flag. Do not call `syncField`, which opens a native confirm dialog.
    - `keepOriginSlug(ctx, originSlug)`: see T5.9.
    - Built in T5.5: compare values with `equalValues(a, b)` from `registry.js` (`_id`, key order and `undefined` keys do not count); "nothing differs from the clean values" is `registry.dirtyHandles(name).length === 0`.
  - **Self-check:** `resources/js/editor/fill.test.js` with a fake context: a changed reference applies nothing; the timing guard waits; meta is set before the value; a repeated delivery is ignored; an edited field is skipped; the defensive branch runs when `desyncField` does nothing; `sync` removes the localized flag; `restore` returns the context to the snapshot and leaves later edits alone.
  - Built (planner's details):
    - `fill.js` exports `SETTLE_MS` (600; `banner.js` imports it from here now) and `REEMIT_MS` (300; core's fieldtype update debounce is 150 ms, `UPDATE_DEBOUNCE_MS` at v6.35.0), and `clone()` (a deep copy that reads through Vue proxies, which `structuredClone` refuses).
    - `snapshot(ctx, handles, delivered = {})` → `{ reference, dirty, fields: handle → { value, meta, localized, delivered } }`; the optional `delivered` map keeps the record an earlier plan left for a handle, so undoing a second translation (for example "Retranslate everything" over an unsaved fill) brings the first one's record back with its value.
    - `applyFields(ctx, fields, { reference, plan, lastChangeAt, planValues, delivered, now, sleep })` is async and resolves `{ stale, applied, skipped: handle → 'edited' | 'hidden' | 'stale', repeated }` once the read-back is recorded. `plan` is the plan id: "already delivered for this plan" is `delivered[handle].plan === plan`. `lastChangeAt` may be a number or a function (read again after each wait, so a save while waiting restarts the 600 ms). The reference is checked before the wait, after it and after the read-back; a switch during the read-back drops this call's records and reports every handle `stale`. A handle is recorded in `delivered` as `{ plan, value: undefined, pending: true, incomplete, sync, localizedBefore }` the moment it is filled (`localizedBefore`: whether it was in `localizedFields` before the first fill since the last save, carried over from an earlier plan's record), so a parallel unit delivering it during the read-back is `repeated`, then `value` becomes the form's value 300 ms later (the filled value when its text differs, `sameText()`) and `pending` false. `planValues` is compared strictly: a handle missing from it counts as edited unless the form's value is missing too. A filled field is marked dirty by `desyncField`; only the defensive branch and `sync` call `Statamic.$dirty.add()` themselves. A `sync` payload never calls `desyncField`, and is taken out of `localizedFields` again after the read-back while its texts and the booleans and numbers both values hold are the filled ones (`sameContent()`), because Bard's re-emit desyncs the field through its update handler; a change the editor made meanwhile, a switched toggle included, keeps the flag their update gave it, or Save would leave it out. Core's Integer, Float and Range fieldtypes pass a typed number up as a string, which `sameText()` already sees, so the numbers `sameContent()` adds are Bard's attributes (a heading level) and those of fieldtypes that emit numbers. What the field holds decides, not whether anyone was seen typing: an upload finishing or a Replicator set added after its fetch changes a field without input. Changes without text or a shared flag or number (a mark, a node type, a row or set without text) made inside the 300 ms are still left out; a comparison that sees them would misread Bard's re-emit (T6.11).
    - `restore(ctx, snapshot, delivered, { registry, sleep })` is async and resolves `{ restored, kept }`. It skips handles whose record is missing, still pending, or the record the snapshot already held; `kept` lists handles the editor changed since. It writes inside `ctx.withoutDirtying()` (so the values watcher does not flag the form again), puts the snapshot's record back into `delivered` or deletes the handle, and clears `Statamic.$dirty` only when the snapshot was not dirty, `registry.dirtyHandles()` is empty and every restored field still holds the snapshot's content (`sameContent()`), once at once and once more 300 ms later, after restoring the localized flags again (except that a restored field whose texts, booleans or numbers differ from the snapshot's keeps the flag their update gave it, and keeps the form dirty, or switching site or leaving the page would not ask first; the changes without text or a shared flag or number that the sync read-back leaves out are left out here too). Known limit: a change without text in a field Undo does not restore (a toggle switched elsewhere since the fill) keeps its localized flag but not the form's dirty flag, because `dirtyHandles()` compares text. Known limit for T6.11: after Undo, a Bard field re-emits the restored value with its normalised attributes (`textAlign: null` and so on), which differs from the clean values, so the form stays dirty although the texts are back.
    - `keepOriginSlug` is not built yet; T5.9 adds it to `fill.js`.

- [x] **T5.8 Unit runner, banner phases, save guard, confirm**
  - `resources/js/editor/unitRunner.js`: `runPlan({ api, plan, concurrency, onFields, onProgress, signal })`.
    - A dynamic queue that starts with `plan.units` and grows with each response's `spawned`; `concurrency` workers (from `Statamic.$config.get('tolk.concurrency')`). Each response's `fields` go to `onFields` at once, so the form fills while other units are still running.
    - Per response: 429 and 503 wait `retry_after` and repeat the same unit, giving up on that unit after 5 waits; 409 `busy` waits 2 seconds and repeats; 409 `source_changed` and any 410 (`plan_expired`, or `target_missing` when the localization, its origin or the term was deleted, or the localization detached) stop everything and report the code; 502 stops everything with the provider's message; any other error marks that unit failed and continues.
    - Built in T5.4: `editor/plan` returns `{ plan, units: [{ id, fields, chars }], fields: [handles], skipped: { handle: reason }, ready: { handle: payload } }` and `editor/unit` `{ fields: { handle: payload }, spawned: [{ id, fields, chars }], failed: { handle: reason }, warnings: [], usage: { 'provider:model': usage }, remaining }`; a payload is `{ value, meta, incomplete, warnings }` or `{ value, meta, sync: true }`; empty maps arrive as `[]`. Error codes besides those above: 404 `unknown_unit` (mark the unit failed), 403 (another user's plan, or the user may no longer edit). `editor/plan` itself answers 404 `unknown_reference`, 422 `not_translatable` or `no_translator`, 422 with `errors.translator` for a model outside the catalog, and 409 `source_changed`. `editor/confirm` returns the status payload plus `not_saved`.
    - Built in T5.2: `api.js` resolves with the response body and rejects with an `ApiError` (an `Error`) carrying `status` (0 when no response arrived), `code` (the body's string `code`, else null), `message` (the body's `message`, else a translated fallback) and `data` (the body object, so `data.retry_after`; null for a body that is not an object).
    - `signal` (an `AbortController`) stops new units from starting. Units in flight finish and are still applied, unless the abort came from a site or entry switch, in which case the reference check in `applyFields` discards them. After Cancel, the units they spawn still run (a review, which T7.3 makes hold their fields, a repair, a retry), but not after a switch (`finishing`).
    - Resolves with a summary: fields delivered, incomplete, skipped (`edited`, `hidden`, `stale`), failed, warnings, usage, models.
  - Wire it into the banner. Buttons, shown only when they have work: **Translate** (mode `missing`) and **Update** (mode `update`).
    1. `skip` = `registry.dirtyHandles()` plus every handle in `localizedFields` that the status says has no stored override. These hold unsaved edits, and the server, which only sees stored content, would otherwise plan them and overwrite what the editor typed.
    2. `planValues` = a deep copy of the current values of the planned handles; `snapshot` for undo.
    3. `editor/plan` → apply `ready` fields → `runPlan` → `applyFields` per response → summary.
  - Do **not** hold a `Statamic.$progress` task while the model works. While any progress task is active the entry form disables Save and, worse, its site switcher skips the unsaved-changes prompt (R3 §1.2, §6.5). T2.2 check 10 confirmed it: the switch discards the unsaved fill without a prompt. The banner's own progress bar (`components/ProgressBar.vue`, hand-built with Tailwind since the UI kit has none, R3 §6.3) is the indicator; its total grows when units are spawned.
  - Save guard: a save that overlaps a fill is unsafe. The save response replaces the form's values with what was posted while a field delivered in between stays marked as localized, and the next save would store origin-language text as an override (T2.2 check 12 reproduced exactly this). So register `entry.saving` once in `cp.js` (hook callbacks are `(resolve, reject, payload)`): while the base container's phase is `working`, show a toast "A translation is in progress. Wait for it to finish, or cancel it." and `reject(new PipelineStopped())`, with `PipelineStopped` imported from `@statamic/cms/save-pipeline`. Otherwise `resolve()`. T2.2 check 11 showed on 6.30.0 and 6.35.0 that a rejected save leaves the form usable and the next Save works; rejecting with any other value adds core's own "Something went wrong" toast and a console error.
  - After a save: the `entry.saved` hook updates `lastChangeAt`, refreshes the clean values, posts `editor/confirm` with the delivered handles, clears `delivered`, and shows the returned status; `not_saved` handles are listed in a warning. Resolve the hook only after the confirm request has returned (or failed): with the default `after_save` preference (`listing`), the form navigates to the listing right after the `entry.saved` hooks (T2.2 check 12), and the confirm must not depend on the banner still being mounted.
  - Built in T5.5: the save guard reads `useEditorStore().phaseOf('base') === 'working'`; after a save, `store.saved(name)` returns the delivered records for `editor/confirm` and clears them; refresh the clean values in the hook from the form (`refreshClean(name)`): core's request step has already put the response's values into it (SavePipeline's `Request`, before the `*.saved` hooks; with revisions core sets them again after the hooks). No refresh 600 ms later: text typed then is an edit, which a translation must leave alone. Decided in the review of 2026-10-10: no refresh after a save and no page-wide input signal (the absence of input does not prove that nobody edited). A fieldtype that re-emits the saved values with other text after the hook (none in core 6.35.0 now that line breaks read alike, see T6.11's `textsOf()`) keeps reading as edited until the next switch, and a field that keeps inheriting reads so again after each save (the response brings back the origin's value): the field is skipped or "Save first" shows, and nothing is overwritten (`saveHooks.test.js` pins it). The buttons follow `describe(status).localization.translate` and `.update`.
  - Built in T5.7: `applyFields()` and `restore()` are async (each waits for the 300 ms read-back), so `onFields` must keep the promises and the phase becomes `done` only after every `applyFields()` has resolved; until then a delivered handle is `pending` in `delivered`. Pass `plan` (the plan id), `reference` and `lastChangeAt` as `() => store.container(name).lastChangeAt`, and `delivered` as `store.container(name).delivered`. Take `planValues` with `clone(ctx.values.value)` before posting `editor/plan` (a handle missing from `planValues` counts as edited), and `snapshot(ctx, handles, delivered)` at the same time with the requested handles, or every handle of the form. Merge each result's `skipped` and `repeated` into the summary; `stale: true` means the editor switched and the result can be dropped.
  - Phases: `working` shows the progress bar and Cancel; `done` shows "N fields translated with {model}", fields left partly untranslated, fields skipped because they were edited, "not saved yet", and **Undo**; `error` shows the message and **Retry**, or **Start again** after `source_changed` / `plan_expired`, which plans again with `fields` limited to the handles not filled yet. Only a stop ends in `error`: a translation whose units failed (a provider that stays down, say) ends in `done`, which then offers **Retry** too, while a planned field it could not translate is not filled yet (`retryable()`), and the line naming the failed fields adds that Retry translates them again (review fix 2026-10-14).
  - If the editor switches site or entry while working, abort the runner.
  - Built in T5.6: `attachBanner()` in `editor/banner.js` runs the switch (`store.switched()`, which resets the phase, `refreshClean()`, the status load, the guarded refresh 600 ms later): abort the runner from there (an option such as `onSwitch`) rather than with a second watcher. After a save there is no 600 ms refresh, by decision (see "Built in T5.5" above). The buttons go into `components/EditorBanner.vue`; `store.containers[name].status` holds the loaded status.
  - **Self-check:** `resources/js/editor/unitRunner.test.js` with a fake API: concurrency is respected; spawned units are run; each status code behaves as specified; abort stops new units. `tests/Browser/editor-translate.spec.js`: on the German page that has only its title overridden, Translate fills the other text fields with `[de] …`, the stored file is unchanged until Save, after Save the stored values and the state file are right and the banner reads current. `tests/Browser/editor-guards.spec.js`: text typed into `intro` before Translate is kept and the field is listed as skipped; Save during a translation is refused with the message; switching site mid-translation leaves the other site's form untouched; a delivered field edited before saving reads "edited by hand" with the source unchanged after the save; Undo restores the previous values.
  - Built (planner's details):
    - `editor/unitRunner.js`: `runPlan({ api, plan, concurrency, onFields, onProgress, signal, finishing, sleep, maxWaits, busyMs, maxBusyWaits })` and `classify(error)` (`wait` for 429/503, `busy` for 409 `busy`, `stop` for 409 `source_changed`, any 410 and 502, else `fail`), `wait(ms, signal)` (ends at once on abort, so Cancel does not sit out a pause), `MAX_WAITS` 5, `BUSY_MS` 2000 and `MAX_BUSY_WAITS` 60 (the unit lock lives the web timeout plus 10 s). It hands `plan.ready` to `onFields` first and skips empty maps; `onFields` may return the `applyFields()` result, and the runner resolves only once every one has settled. A 429/503 without `retry_after` waits 5 s. Units in flight finish after a stop or an abort; a unit waiting to be repeated is not repeated. After an abort, the units spawned by units already run still start while `finishing()` is true (`translate.js` passes `mine`, false after a switch), so a field whose review or repair was on its way is filled; the plan's own units do not, and nothing starts after a stop. `onProgress({ total, done, waiting })`. Summary: `delivered` (handles really filled), `incomplete` (of those), `skipped` (from the fill results), `failed` (strict `failed`, the fields of failed units, `not_filled` when a fill threw; a field delivered later is taken out), `failedUnits`, `cancelledUnits`, `warnings` (unique), `usage` (summed per model key), `models`, `stopped` (`{ status, code, message }` of the first stop), `aborted`, `stale`, `units`.
    - `editor/translate.js`: the shared `translations` (`createTranslations({ store, registry, api, concurrency, run, fill, undoFill })`) with `translate(name, { mode, fields, overwriteManual, translator })`, `retry(name)`, `cancel(name)`, `abort(name)`, `undo(name)`, `running(name)`; `BUSY` (`planning`, `working`), `RESTART` (`source_changed`, `plan_expired`, `target_missing`), `skipHandles()`, `remainingHandles()`, `undoable()`, `retryable()` (`error`, or `done` while a field of `summary.failed` is among `remainingHandles()`; Cancel alone offers no Retry). AbortControllers stay in the module, everything shown in the store. `translate()` takes the snapshot (requested handles, else every handle of the form), `planValues` and `skip` before posting `editor/plan`, sets `planning`, then `working` with the units' count, and ends in `done` or, after a stop, `error` with `summary` kept; an exception also ends in `error`, so Save is never refused for good. `skip` is `registry.dirtyHandles()` plus `registry.newlyLocalized()` handles whose status is `untranslated`, limited to the status's fields and without fields this addon filled that are untouched since (deviation: "every handle in `localizedFields`" would count core's own Bard desync on load as an edit, `07-spike-findings.md` "Editor flow"). The summary adds `plan`, `planned` (`plan.fields`), `cancelled`, and the plan's `edited_in_form` skips as `edited`; other plan skip reasons are not kept. A run counts as current while its token is in the module and the container and reference are unchanged; `abort()` (a switch, a new container) removes the token, and `applyFields()` gets `active` (new option: a fill is stale once its translation is no longer active, also when the editor switched back to the same item). `cancel()` only acts while `working` and sets `progress.cancelling`. `retry()` (Retry and Start again) repeats `request` limited to `remainingHandles(summary)` (or as it was when the plan request itself failed); it continues the translation, so its snapshot keeps the earlier entries of fields the first run filled and Undo puts back the state before both. `undo()` calls `restore()` and returns to `idle`.
    - `editor/saveHooks.js`: `createSaveHooks({ PipelineStopped, … }).listen()` in `cp.js` (`PipelineStopped` imported there from `@statamic/cms/save-pipeline`). `entry.saving` refuses while the base phase is `planning` or `working` (also `planning`: `ready` fields can land mid-save too), with the toast; a stacked editor's save is refused then as well, since the hook does not name its container. Otherwise it runs `leaveOutHidden(ctx, delivered)` (`fill.js`) on the base container while its store `reference` is the form's: a delivered field, neither pending nor synced and not localized before the fill, that a condition hides (`omitValue`) is taken out of `localizedFields`, which core's request step posts as `_localized` after the hook, and marked `leftOut`; one marked so is put back once it is shown again (a save that failed in between). The summary's `hidden` line names the fields a fill skipped as hidden, and `notSavedMessage()` says to show them and translate them again, since the save response brings the origin's text back. `entry.saved` acts only when `response.data.data.reference` (the hook's own `reference` is stale after an in-place switch) equals the base container's store `reference`: `store.saved()`, `refreshClean()` once, from the form, which core's request step has already filled with the response's values (no later refresh, see "Built in T5.5" above), a `done`/`error` translation back to `idle` without summary, snapshot and request, then `editor/confirm` with the delivered handles the save was meant to store (`savedFills()`: those in `localizedFields`, which core keeps after the save, and those marked `leftOut`; a field the editor put back to inheriting with core's sync button, or a sync fill, holds no override and the server could not tell it from a hidden one); the returned status goes into the store, `not_saved` into `notSaved` and an error toast (`notSavedMessage()`). A failed confirm shows a toast (not for 403). It always resolves, and only after the confirm.
    - `registry.js`: takes the localized handles with the clean values (`attachContext()`, `refreshClean()`) and has `newlyLocalized(name)`. `store.js`: `progress` also has `waiting` and `cancelling`; new keys `request` and `notSaved`. `statuses.js`: `describeSummary(summary, status, { retry })` (`translated`, `incomplete`, `edited`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `unsaved`; with `retry`, which the banner passes while it offers Retry and not Start again, the `failed` line says that Retry translates or reviews them again), `modelName()` (the part after `provider:`), `fieldLabels()`. `banner.js`: `attachBanner({ onSwitch })` runs before every switch (not on a re-mount); `createBanners({ abort })` aborts a container's translation when a container of that name is created.
    - `components/EditorBanner.vue`: in `idle`, **Translate** (`missing`) and **Update** (`update`) under the localization block (`data-tolk-actions`, `data-tolk-action`); `planning` ("Preparing the translation…"); `working` ("Translating into {site}…" or "Cancelling. …", **Cancel**, the progress bar, a line while a unit waits for the provider); `error` (the message, **Retry** or **Start again**); **Retry** while `retryable()`, so also in `done` after failed fields; the summary in `done` and `error` (`data-tolk-summary-*`); **Undo** while `undoable()`; the `notSaved` line. Buttons are hidden in `done` (the status counts are those of the stored entry until Save). `components/ProgressBar.vue`: `value`, `max`, `label`, `role="progressbar"`.
    - Browser: `support/sandbox.js` `entryState(id)`; `editor-translate.spec.js` (2) and `editor-guards.spec.js` (6, including Cancel) hold `editor/unit` with `page.route` to act mid-translation. The German home page is dirty from load (core's Bard desync), so a switch from it confirms core's "Unsaved Changes" modal.

  - Slugs (D16: never translated). In the publish form, core manages the slug of a new localization: on `localization.created` the slug field resets to automatic mode and regenerates the slug from the title, in the target site's language, whenever the title changes. Filling the translated title would therefore produce a translated slug, because the save posts `_auto_slug: true` and the server derives the slug from the posted title. So `keepOriginSlug(ctx, originSlug)` runs on a just-created localization before the first fill, once core's reset has finished:
    - Wait for the reset: it slugifies asynchronously, about a second after `localization.created` in the sandbox, and has finished when `meta.slug.auto` is `true`. Wait at least for the 600 ms guard of T5.7, and give up waiting after 2 seconds.
    - Then `setFieldValue('slug', originSlug)`; the reset has already put `slug` into `localizedFields`. The slug field stops following the title, sets `meta.slug.auto` to `false` itself, and the save posts the origin's slug with `_auto_slug: false`. T2.2 check 9 verified this on 6.35.0 (and that the same steps keep the slug on 6.30.0, which is below the floor now).
    - Do not set `meta.slug.auto` by hand: it is not needed, and it does not help while a slugify request is still running, whose late response overwrites the slug (T2.2 check 9). For the same reason, after the title has been filled, check the slug again after a second and set it once more if it changed.
    - Never touch the slug of a localization that already existed.
    - A localization is stored as `{slug}.md`, without the origin's date prefix and without a `slug` key, so in the browser scenario `entryFile(collection, site, originSlug)` finding the new localization is the check that its slug was kept.
  - Published state: core's form switches a new localization's published toggle on when the origin is published, so its first Save publishes it (T2.2 check 2). The addon leaves the toggle alone: the editor sees it before saving, and D10 covers server-side flows only.

Setting it: while the slug follows the title and the form shows the origin's slug already, `''` first and the origin's slug 50 ms later, because a value equal to the shown one does not stop the following (`07-spike-findings.md` "The offer and the slug").

## Phase 6 — Widen the content

Goal: every handler of §A2, reuse of earlier translations on update, and neighbour context. Pure PHP for the most part and test-heavy: this phase decides whether translations can be written back without damaging content. Read R4 §7 (stored shapes, Bard) first.
