Its `roots()` returns a list, not a lazy iterable; make it lazy here if the run planner needs that.
  - Enumeration of entries:
    - By collection: iterate the collection's entries lazily (`->lazy()`), keep roots (`isRoot()`), and for every requested site in `$collection->sites()` other than the root's own site emit one item per family. A collection that does not include a requested site is reported in `skipped`, not translated.
    - Explicit entry ids: resolve each to its family root, then apply the same rule; de-duplicate on root id + site.
    - The item's source is chosen by §A11: the existing localization's origin; else the root's localization in `sourceSite`; else the root.
    - Skip `excluded_collections`.
  - Order and dependencies (§A12):
    - Structured collections: emit in tree order (the source site's tree, depth first; `CollectionStructure::in($site)->tree()`, T2.3) and set `depends_on` to the item of the parent page for the same site, when that parent has an item in this run. A parent that is neither in the run nor localized is noted as a warning on the child (`parent_not_localized`): the child will sit at the top level.
    - Collections whose structure expects a root: every other item of a site also depends on the root page's item for that site, when the root has one. When the root has no item and no localization in that site, and that site's tree is empty, the other items are skipped with `root_missing` (§A11 "Root page").
    - Chains: when the item's source is itself a localization that has an item in this run, depend on that item.
    - All entry items are `stage: 0`.
  - Authorization: with a `user`, drop targets failing `Target::authorize()` and count them under `skipped.unauthorized` (§A14).
  - Do **not** plan each target here: enumeration must stay fast enough for a web request. An item with nothing to do is marked `skipped` when it is planned, during the estimate (T8.7) or during translation; the estimate skips it only once nothing holds it back (`RunRepository::isReady()`), because the run may still give an item that waits for others something to do.
  - Built in T8.1: `Run::make($driver, $scope, $options, $user)` gives a pending run with a ULID and captures `createdBy`. Items carry their own `index`, which `depends_on` refers to and `RunRepository::create()` keeps (it refuses a duplicate). A skipped item's reason goes in `RunItem::$reason`. `Run::$estimate` is a nullable array: store `Estimate::toArray()` there. `save()` writes only what changed, so an estimate request that runs after Start does not set the run back to `pending`.
  - Built in T8.2: `Estimator::forPlan($plan, $context)` takes the `PromptContext` of the plan's target site (`PromptContext::fromSettings($settings, $site)`, which builds the glossary, so make it once per site, not per item). Add to the run with `Estimate::fromArray($run->estimate ?? [])->add($estimate)->toArray()`; an empty array reads as a zero estimate. `Estimate::$cost` is null once any model without a price would be called, and stays null when added up. A plan without batches estimates to zero.
  - `estimate(Run $run, int $fromIndex, int $budgetMs): EstimateProgress`: plans items from `$fromIndex` on (without storing the plans) until the budget is used, adds `Estimator::forPlan()` to the run's `estimate`, marks items whose plan is empty as `skipped` unless they wait for other items, and returns the next index or `null` when finished. The CLI calls it to completion; the run page calls it in small requests.
  - Built (planner's details):
    - `RunRequest` (final readonly) also takes `driver` (`RunDriver`, required): the CP passes `RunDispatcher::resolveDriver()` (T8.6), the CLI `Cli` or `Queue`. `sites` are de-duplicated; `RunRequest::ALL` is `'*'` and `all($handles)` tests for it; `options` are normalised against `RunRequest::OPTIONS` (null = the settings decide; unknown keys, an invalid mode or `new_status`, and a model that is not a string are refused); `scope()` gives the snake_case scope.
    - `build()` refuses an unknown site or source site (`UnknownSite`), collection (`InvalidArgumentException` "Collection [x] not found.") or entry (`TargetNotFound`), and resolves the options once through `PlanOptions::fromSettings()`: the run stores `mode`, `overwrite_manual`, `review`, `translator`, `reviewer`, `new_status` and `publish_changes` resolved, so a later settings change does not alter it. A reviewer counts only with review on, as in `PlanOptions`; "naming a reviewer asks for a review" stays the CLI's rule. `collections: ['*']` means every collection with more than one site. The run's `scope` is `RunRequest::scope()` plus `skipped`.
    - `PlannedRun` (final readonly: `run`, `items`, `skipped`). `items` is a list, not lazy: `skipped` must be complete when the run is created, and a dependency may point at a later item. A few thousand items, the file driver's limit, fit in memory.
    - `skipped` counts targets (family × site): `excluded_collection` (`RunPlanner::EXCLUDED_COLLECTION`, checked first), `site_not_in_collection` (`RunPlanner::SITE_NOT_IN_COLLECTION`), `unauthorized` (`RunItem::UNAUTHORIZED`, new, for T8.5 too). A root's own site is not a target and is not counted.
    - Items are indexed 0 to n − 1: family by family in `EntryFamilies::roots()` order, and inside a family a chain source's site before the sites translated from it.
    - Memory (review R10): `EntryFamilies::rootIds()` orders the root ids from the index (`pluck('id')`, the trees' positions, entry ids through `root()`), and `roots()` is a generator over them that loads `EntryFamilies::CHUNK` (50) roots per query (`where('collection')->whereIn('id')`) as the caller reads them and, before the next chunk, forgets what core memoized in Blink meanwhile through `Support\EntryMemo` (`mark()` before the chunk, `forget()` after it: the keys `entry-descendants-…`, `origin-Entry-…` and `entry-…-blueprint` added since the mark, and every entry added to the `structure-entries` store, which `Page::entry()` fills); keys that were there before stay. `entryTargets()` builds each target from the root it read (`TargetFactory::forLoadedEntry()`, which takes the assumed sites), never through `Entry::find()`, and keeps per target only the scalars `entryItem()` needs (root id, site, source id and site, whether the source is a localization, title, and for a localization the run creates the root page's and the parent's family ids, the `RootMissing` message and root page, and the parent's title); `entryItems()` resolves the dependencies from them once every family is indexed. So creating a run holds the entries of one chunk of families, not of the whole collection or site, which on PHP's common 128M limit ended in "Allowed memory size exhausted" for a few hundred long, already-translated entries. `estimate()` marks and forgets the memo around each item in the same way, because the CLI's scan (`tolk:translate`, `tolk:site`) plans every item of the run in the process that created it. `RunsController::store()` raises the time limit to `STORE_SECONDS` (120): core's `in()` and `origin()` still filter an index per root, so the time grows faster than the number of roots.
    - Source (§A11): an existing localization's origin; for a missing one `$root->in($sourceSite)`, else the root. A source-site localization that this same run creates is not waited for; the root is the source then.
    - Dependencies. Chain: in every mode, when the source is a localization with an item. Placement: only for a localization the run creates (not in mode review, not for existing targets), because an existing localization is never moved. Waiting for its parent would only serialise the run and pass a parent's failure on as `dependency_failed`. The root dependency applies only when `EntryTarget::rootMissing()` holds (an empty target tree). Without a root item, that item is `skipped` with reason `root_missing`, the `RootMissing` message in `error` and no dependencies. The parent dependency applies only when the source's parent page (in the source site's tree, which `makeLocalization()` uses) has no localization in the site. There is no parent dependency for the root page (core puts its children at the top level) or for orderable collections (core does not place them). A parent that is neither localized nor in the run adds the item warning "Its parent page, {title}, has no localization in {site}, so this page will be placed at the top level." Item warnings are English strings, like `ApplyResult`'s.
    - `estimate()` plans only `pending` items, without `StateWriter::confirm()` (as the dry run; the resolver reconciles pending state in memory), at least one per call, with one `PromptContext` per site and call, and `planReview()` in mode review, forgetting what core memoized while an item was planned once it is (`EntryMemo`, see Memory above). An item whose target cannot be built (`TargetNotFound`, `InvalidTarget`, `UnknownSite`, `NotSupportedYet`) adds nothing; translating fails it. An empty plan makes the item `skipped` with reason `nothing_to_do` (`TargetResult::NOTHING_TO_DO`), re-read just before the write; `ClaimLost` is ignored. An item that `RunRepository::isReady()` says waits (a dependency, or an item of an earlier stage in its site, is unfinished) stays `pending` with its empty plan: translating its source changes a chained localization, and the localizations the run creates give a navigation the branches that link them, so it is planned again when it is claimed, as `tolk:translate --dry-run` says ("nothing to do yet"). The run's `estimate` holds `EstimateProgress::toArray()`: the `Estimate` keys plus `next`, which is null when finished. A call from index 0 starts over; a call from any index other than the stored `next` returns the stored progress and adds nothing, so a retried request cannot count twice.
    - `planOptions(Run $run): PlanOptions`: the run's options with profile `background` for the CLI driver and `web` otherwise, and `userId` from `createdBy`. T8.4 uses it.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Runs/RunPlannerTest.php`: collection × two sites gives families × 2 items; a localization selected in a listing resolves to its family; a child depends on its parent's item; on a collection with a root, every page depends on the root's item, and without the root in the run or in the target site the pages are skipped with `root_missing`; a chained target depends on its source's item; excluded and unconfigured collections are reported; an unauthorized user gets fewer items; `estimate()` called repeatedly with a tiny budget reaches the same total as one call with a large one, and marks fully translated targets `skipped`.

- [x] **T8.4 `RunProcessor::translate()`**
  - `translate(Run $run, string $workerId, CarbonInterface $deadline, int $claimTtl): WorkReport`. Loop while the deadline has not passed and the run is `running`: `claimNext()`; when there is none, stop and report `nextAvailableIn()`; otherwise work on the item.
  - Working on one item, inside `Cache::lock("tolk:item:{run}:{index}", $claimTtl)` (a second caller skips the item):
    1. Build the `Target` from the item (`TargetFactory::fromDescription()`). A source that no longer exists fails the item (`fromDescription()` throws `TargetNotFound`, T4.7).
    2. No live plan: `StateWriter::confirm()`, then `Planner::plan()` (or `planReview()` for mode `review`) with `PlanOptions` from the run's options; profile `background` for the CLI driver, `web` for queue and browser. An empty plan marks the item `skipped`. Otherwise store the plan and its id on the item.
       - Since T6.11 the `web` profile splits a small entry into up to `concurrency.editor` batches of at least `batch.min_chars`, for the editor's parallel requests. A lane runs an item's units one after another, so for runs that only adds calls (each with the system prompt). Give `PlanOptions` a way to keep the web timeout without the split (for example a `parallel` option the editor sets), or accept it and say why in the done log.
    3. While the deadline has not passed: `extendClaim()`, then `Pipeline::runUnit()` for the next runnable unit. A unit is started only before the deadline, so the call returns at most one provider timeout after it.
    4. When the plan is finished, mark the item `translated`. Nothing is saved to content here.
  - Failure handling:
    - `ProviderPaused` / `ProviderUnavailable`: release the item with `available_at` = now + the wait, and count a wait. `waits` is reset whenever a unit succeeds. After 10 waits in a row, fail the item with "provider unavailable"; when three items in a row fail that way, set the run to `aborted` with that message and finalize it.
    - `ProviderRejected`: set the run to `aborted` with the sanitised message, release the item as `pending`, finalize.
    - `UnitBusy`, `ClaimLost`: stop working on this item quietly; another worker has it.
    - Any other exception: `attempts++`; release with a growing delay; after 3 attempts mark `failed` with the sanitised message. Report the exception with `report()`.
  - The first claimed item sets the run's `startedAt`: built in T8.1, `claimNext()` sets it under the run lock (a `save()` from the processor could race with a cancel). `start()` sets `progressedAt` to now, so `stale()` and the `stalled` flag (T8.7) measure from the start. `claimNext()` refreshes the `Run` it is given, so the loop sees a cancel or an abort after every claim. A worker that gets its own item back gets a fresh token, so an older request still working on it stops with `ClaimLost` at its next `extendClaim()` or `saveItem()`; the item lock then lets the newer request continue on the next call. Fire `Events\RunStarted` when a run moves from `pending` to `running` (T8.7's `start`, the CLI, and the push flow all go through one `RunProcessor::start(Run $run)`).
  - `WorkReport`: units run, items finished, whether work remains, and `wait` (seconds until something can be claimed, or the provider pause).
  - Built in T8.2: usage. Pass `runUnit()` a closure that calls `UsageRecorder::record($model, $usage, UsageSource::Run)` (the monthly log; `UsageSource::Cli` for a run with the `cli` driver), `RunRepository::addUsage($run, $model, $usage)`, and adds the call to the item's `usage`. The closure runs between the paid call and the storing of its outcome, so whatever in it throws makes the unit run, and be paid for, again. The recorder never throws (it reports); do the same for `addUsage()` (a lock timeout, `RunNotFound` after a delete).
  - Built in T8.3: `RunPlanner::planOptions($run)` gives the `PlanOptions` of the run's items (the run's resolved options, profile by driver, `userId`); add a `parallel` switch there if the web split is turned off. Items skipped with `root_missing` at build, or with `nothing_to_do` by the estimate, are never claimed.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Runs/RunTranslateTest.php` with faked agents and `useFileCache()`: a 3-item run reaches three `translated` items and no content has changed yet; a deadline that passes after one unit leaves the item `translating` with its plan, and a second call continues without repeating the first unit; a rate limit releases the item with a future `available_at` and the report carries the wait; a provider that stays unavailable fails the item after 10 waits and aborts the run after three such items; a rejected key aborts the run; an item that throws three times is `failed`; two overlapping calls with the same worker id make one model call per unit.
  - Built (planner's details):
    - The web split: `PlanOptions::$parallel` (default false, in `toArray()` / `fromArray()`): `Planner::budget()` splits a small target over `concurrency.editor` batches only for the `web` profile with `parallel`, which only the editor sets (`EditorController::options()`). Runs, their estimate included, batch by `batch.max_chars.{profile}` alone, so an item costs one call per batch and one system prompt less per split. `config/tolk.php` and the `PlanOptions` docblock now say that `web` serves the editor and browser and queue runs, `background` the CLI.
    - `RunProcessor` (final readonly) has `start()`, `translate()` and the first half of `finalize()`. `start()`, the abort and `finalize()` change the run's status under the processor's own lock `tolk:run-status:{id}` and re-read the run through `save()` first. `start()` turns a `pending` run into a running one and fires `Events\RunStarted` (run id, driver, `createdBy`, counts per status); on a running run it only sets `progressedAt` again, and a finished run is left alone. For a pending or running run it also resets the count of items failed in a row for an unavailable provider, so a resumed run starts that count over.
    - `translate()` does not trust the status of the `Run` it is given: it loops until the deadline on `claimNext()`, which hands out nothing unless the stored run is running. The `PlanOptions` come once per call from `RunPlanner::planOptions()`. An item whose lock `tolk:item:{run}:{index}` (TTL `$claimTtl`, not blocking) another request holds is left claimed with this call's fresh token, and the call ends with `wait` 1; the older request then stops with `ClaimLost` at its next write. `ClaimLost`, `UnitBusy` and `RunNotFound` also end the call with `wait` 1. Going on would claim the same item again (a worker gets its own item back) and take it from the newer request.
    - Planning: when the item has no `planId` or its plan is no longer in the `PlanStore`: `StateWriter::confirm()`, then for entries outside mode review `ensureRootExists()` (`RootMissing` fails the item with reason `root_missing` before any call), then `plan()` or `planReview()`. An empty plan makes the item `skipped` with `nothing_to_do` and the plan's `skipped` as its `fields`; otherwise the plan is stored and its id saved on the item at once, so a takeover continues it.
    - Units: before each, the deadline, `extendClaim()` (which refreshes the run) and the run's status. A run cancelled or aborted meanwhile makes the call release the item as `pending`, with its `planId`, so a resumed run does not repeat finished units. `cancel()` and `abort()` themselves put every `translating` item back the same way (`RunRepository::releaseTranslating()`), because a browser tick stops after one unit and a lane whose page went away never comes back to release its item; a worker still inside a unit then meets `ClaimLost` at its next write. After each unit `saveItem()` stores the item's usage and resets `waits`. A finished plan makes the item `translated` and resets the count of unavailable items.
    - Failures: `TargetNotFound`, `InvalidTarget`, `UnknownSite` and `NotSupportedYet` (reason `not_supported`) fail the item at once. A transient failure releases the item until now + max(1, seconds), counts a wait and ends the call with that `wait`. As in `TargetTranslator`, the failure after the 10th wait fails the item with reason `provider_unavailable` and "The AI provider [x] was still unavailable after 10 waits.". Those failures are counted in a row per run in the cache (`tolk:run-unavailable:{id}`, TTL `plan_ttl`, reset by a translated item and by `start()`), and the third aborts the run with that message. `ProviderRejected` aborts the run, which releases the item with the others. Any other exception is reported, counts an attempt and releases the item for 5 × 2^(attempts − 1) seconds (5, then 10) while the loop goes on with other items; the third attempt fails it (`RunProcessor::MAX_ATTEMPTS`). Every stored error goes through `Sanitizer::message()`.
    - `finalize()` (T8.5 adds the rest): a running run without unfinished items becomes `completed`, or `completed_with_errors`, and a finished run gets `finishedAt`. `translate()` calls it after an abort, and when an item it failed or skipped was the last unfinished one.
    - `WorkReport` (final readonly, `toArray()`): `units` (units run), `items` (items that finished translating: `translated`, `skipped`, `failed`), `remains` (the run is running and an item is `pending` or `translating`), `wait` (0 at once; the provider's seconds after a transient failure; 1 for a busy item; otherwise `nextAvailableIn()`; null when nothing remains or no item has a known time).
    - Usage: the closure records each call with `UsageSource::Run`, or `UsageSource::Cli` when the run's driver is `cli`, so the Overview counts `tolk:translate` and `tolk:site` under the command line (a CLI run resumed in the CP takes the CP's driver, and its later calls count as runs), adds it to the item's `usage`, and calls `addUsage()`, whose exceptions are reported, never thrown.

- [x] **T8.5 `RunProcessor::applyReady()` and `finalize()`**
  - `applyReady(Run $run, string $workerId, CarbonInterface $deadline): ApplyReport`. Loop while the deadline has not passed: `ContentWriter::write()` around each item, so the global write lock is taken **before** anything touches the content stores:
    1. `claimTranslated()`; stop when there is none.
    2. Rebuild the target fresh. When the run has a creator, re-check `Target::authorize()` for that user; a failure marks the item `skipped` with `unauthorized`.
    3. Load the plan. A plan that expired from the cache sends the item back to `pending` (it will be planned and translated again).
    4. Assemble every field. `SourceChanged` drops the plan and sends the item back to `pending`; the second time, fail the item. In strict mode a `FieldFailed` field is listed as failed and the others are still applied.
    5. `Target::apply()` with `ApplyOptions` from the run. Fill `fields`, `warnings`, `reference`, `editUrl`. Status `done` when at least one field was applied or nothing was planned to change; `failed` when every field was skipped or failed. Forget the plan.
       - For a review plan (mode `review`, T7.4) apply only the fields with `corrections` and record the others as `no_corrections`, as `TargetTranslator::review()` does; an item whose fields were all accepted is `done` with nothing saved. Share that rule with `TargetTranslator` rather than repeating it: `Pipeline::reviewed()` sets `AssembledField::$unchanged` on a reviewed field without corrections, and `TargetTranslator::apply()` and `EditorDelivery::deliver()` read that flag.
  - `WriterBusy` (another process is applying) ends the call with a short wait; it is not an error.
  - `finalize(Run $run): void`, idempotent per state: under a lock, decide the final status when no item is unfinished (`completed`, or `completed_with_errors` when any item failed), set `finishedAt`; then, if anything was applied since the last finalize, `GitListeners::commit(ContentWriter::COMMIT_MESSAGE)` once (the message `tolk:translate` uses since T4.18); fire `Events\RunFinished` (run id, status, counts, usage map). `finalize()` is called when the last item finishes, on cancel, on abort, and by the sweep for stalled runs (T9.8), so content saved by an interrupted run is always committed.
    - Built in T8.1: the repository's `tolk:run:{id}` lock is private and not re-entrant (a repository call inside it would wait 5 seconds and fail), so `finalize()` takes its own lock (for example `tolk:finalize:{id}`), re-reads the run with `find()`, and saves with `save()`, which writes only the changed values.
  - Built in T8.4: `RunProcessor::finalize()` exists with the first half: under the processor's own lock `tolk:run-status:{id}` (shared with `start()` and the abort, not re-entrant, so never call `finalize()` inside it) it re-reads the run through `save()`, makes a running run without unfinished items `completed` or `completed_with_errors`, and gives a finished run `finishedAt`. Add `finalizedAt`, the commit and `RunFinished` here. `translate()` already calls it when it fails or skips the last unfinished item and after an abort; `applyReady()` calls it when it finishes the last one. Items that a cancel released mid-plan are `pending` with their `planId`; `translate()` plans again when the item's `planId` is no longer in the `PlanStore`, so step 3 may send an item back to `pending` without clearing it. Failed items carry `reason` where one exists (`root_missing`, `provider_unavailable`, `not_supported`); messages stored in `error` go through `Sanitizer::message()`.
  - Built in T8.3: the run's options are resolved when it is built (`new_status`, `publish_changes`, `overwrite_manual`), so `ApplyOptions` come from them and not from the settings. The skip reason is `RunItem::UNAUTHORIZED`. An item may already carry a planner warning (`parent_not_localized`, an English string): add the `ApplyResult` warnings to `RunItem::$warnings` rather than replacing them.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Runs/RunApplyTest.php`: after `translate()` and `applyReady()` every target is current and the items are `done`; a child is applied under its localized parent; an item whose origin changed between translate and apply goes back to `pending` and is done on the next round; an item whose target field was edited meanwhile keeps the human value and reports `target_changed`; a creator who lost access is skipped at apply time; `finalize()` twice fires `RunFinished` once and dispatches one commit; cancelling after one applied item still dispatches a commit; with git enabled, the listener is suspended during the save and no commit job is dispatched per entry.
  - Built (planner's details):
    - `Translation\PlanApplier` (final readonly): `apply(Plan, Target, ApplyOptions): TargetResult` is `TargetTranslator`'s former `apply()`, moved so the run shares it: assemble every field from the target the caller resolved after the units ran (strict `FieldFailed` listed, the others kept), leave out reviewed fields that are `unchanged`, give `skipped` + `no_corrections` or `failed` + `fields_failed` without taking the lock when nothing is left to save, else save the rest through `ContentWriter::write()` (re-entrant inside the run's own), whose `Target::apply()` checks the sources again (§A11 step 1), giving `done`, or when it skipped every saved field `failed` + `fields_failed` if others failed and else `skipped` + `target_changed`. `TargetTranslator` calls it.
    - `applyReady()` loops while the deadline has not passed and an item is `translated` or `applying` (read from `counts()` first, so a lane that has nothing to apply does not take the global lock). Each step is `ContentWriter::write()` with a wait of the seconds left before the deadline (new optional `$wait` argument; null keeps `write.wait_seconds`); inside it `claimTranslated()` with a claim TTL of `write.lock_seconds` and the item lock `tolk:item:{run}:{index}` (busy: the call ends with `wait` 1, as in `translate()`). Target, authorization (a creator `User::find()` no longer finds counts as unauthorized), plan, then `PlanApplier::apply()` with `ApplyOptions` from the run's resolved options and the creator.
    - Item results: `done` when anything was saved (`reason` null) or a review accepted every field (`reason` `no_corrections`, nothing saved); `skipped` with `target_changed` when every planned field was edited meanwhile, instead of the task's `failed`: `TargetTranslator` and the CLI report it as skipped (T8.10 builds its report from items), and a failed item would skip its children with `dependency_failed` although the target is fine; `failed` with `fields_failed` when no field assembled in strict mode. `fields`, `corrections` (new `RunItem::$corrections`, by handle, as `TargetResult`), `reference`, `editUrl` are filled; the result's warnings are appended to the planner's, without duplicates; `planId` is cleared and the plan forgotten in every final case.
    - Sent back to `pending` (`ApplyReport::$returned`): an expired plan (`reason` null), and a `SourceChanged` from assembly or apply (`reason` `source_changed`, plan forgotten). A `SourceChanged` on an item that already carries `source_changed` fails it with that reason and the exception's message. `TargetNotFound`, `InvalidTarget`, `UnknownSite`, `NotSupportedYet` (`not_supported`), `RootMissing` (`root_missing`) and `ApplyFailed` (`apply_failed`) fail the item at once; any other exception is reported, counts an attempt and releases the item as `translated` for 5 × 2^(attempts − 1) seconds, and the third attempt fails it. For that `claimTranslated()` now honours `available_at` (contract and §A12 updated). `TargetTranslated` fires for every `done` item that saved, with the item's usage.
    - Commits: `Run::$saves` counts the items whose content the run saved, or may have saved (any exception from the apply other than `SourceChanged`; an extra commit of nothing is a no-op in Statamic's git), incremented under the write lock after a refresh, so appliers never lose a count; `finalize()` commits when `saves > committedSaves` and sets `committedSaves`. `RunFinished` (run id, status, counts, usage) fires when the run is finished and `finalizedAt` is null, and sets it; a resumed run must clear `finishedAt` and `finalizedAt` (T8.7). A running run (the sweep, T9.8) only gets its commit. Both are decided under `tolk:run-status:{id}`; the commit and the event happen after it. `applyReady()` finalizes after the last item finishes, and also when the run stopped running while it applied, so an item saved after a cancel's finalize is still committed.
    - `ApplyReport` (final readonly, `toArray()`): `items` (finished applying), `returned`, `remains` (the run is running and an item is `translated` or `applying`), `wait` (null when nothing remains; 0 when the deadline ended the call; 1 after a busy writer, a busy item or when nothing was claimable now).

- [x] **T8.6 `RunDispatcher` and the queue lanes**
  - `src/Runs/RunDispatcher.php`:
    - `resolveDriver(): RunDriver`: `runs.execution` `queue` or `browser` are taken as given; `auto` means `Queue` when the driver of the default queue connection (or of `runs.queue.connection`) is not `sync`, otherwise `Browser`.
    - `start(Run $run): void`: `RunProcessor::start()`; for `Queue`, increment the run's `generation` and dispatch `concurrency.queue` lane jobs carrying it. For `Browser` and `Cli` nothing more (the page or the command drives it).
    - `takeOver(Run $run): void`: switch a queue run to the browser driver and bump the generation, for a site whose queue has no worker.
  - `src/Jobs/WorkRun.php` (`ShouldQueue`): constructor `string $runId`, `int $lane`, `int $generation`, `int $restarts = 0`.
    - `handle()`: exit at once when the run's generation is newer than the job's. Otherwise `translate($run, "queue-{$lane}", deadline, runs.claim_ttl)`, then `applyReady()` for a few seconds; when work remains and the run is `running`, dispatch itself again, delayed by the reported wait (at least 1 second when nothing was claimable, so lanes never spin).
    - Timing: read the connection's `retry_after`. Lane deadline = `min(30, retry_after − http.timeout.web − 20)` seconds, at least 5; `$timeout` = `retry_after − 10`. A job must end before the queue would hand it out again.
    - `$tries = 1`. `failed()`: dispatch the same lane and generation again with `restarts + 1`, up to 5 restarts, so a killed lane does not stop the run.
    - Use `runs.queue.connection` and `runs.queue.name` when set.
  - Built in T8.3: `RunRequest` takes the driver, so the CP's callers (T8.7 `store`, T8.11, T8.13) pass `resolveDriver()` when they build the request.
  - Built in T8.4: `WorkReport` has `units`, `items`, `remains` and `wait`. `remains` counts only work for `translate()` (an item `pending` or `translating` while the run is running); a lane that also applies has work while items are `translated` or `applying` too. `wait` is null when no item has a known time (it waits for a dependency or another worker's live claim): that is where the 1-second minimum applies. A call that found its item busy in another request reports `wait` 1. `RunProcessor::start()` accepts a running run (it sets `progressedAt` again) and leaves a finished one alone.
  - Built in T8.5: `applyReady($run, $workerId, $deadline)` returns `ApplyReport` (`items`, `returned`, `remains`, `wait`); it waits for the global write lock only until its deadline and reports `wait` 1 when another process held it. `returned` counts items it sent back to `pending` (expired plan, changed source): a lane decides whether to dispatch itself again from the counts after applying (any item `pending`, `translating`, `translated` or `applying` while the run is running), not from the `WorkReport` taken before. `applyReady()` finalizes the run itself when it finishes the last item, and both `translate()` and `applyReady()` finalize a running run without unfinished items before they return (the repository may have failed the last item when its claim expired a third time), so a lane needs no finalize of its own. `tests/Support/RunFixtures` has `started()`, `translate()`, `items()`, `statuses()` and `stored()` for run tests.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/Runs/RunDispatcherTest.php`: driver resolution for `sync`, `database` and each explicit setting; with `Queue::fake()`, `start()` pushes `concurrency.queue` jobs; a job of an old generation exits without claiming; running the job on the sync connection completes a small run by re-dispatching itself; `failed()` re-dispatches up to the limit; the computed timeout is below `retry_after`.
  - Built (planner's details):
    - `RunDispatcher` (final readonly: `RunRepository`, `RunProcessor`). `resolveDriver()` is static, as T8.3 and T8.7 call it. For `auto`, a connection has no worker when its driver is in `RunDispatcher::WITHOUT_WORKER` (`sync`, `deferred`, `background`: they extend `SyncQueue` and run the job in the process that dispatches it; `null` drops it) or the connection is not configured; a `failover` connection is read as its first connection (`connectionConfig()`). An unknown `runs.execution` throws `InvalidArgumentException`. `connection()` is `runs.queue.connection`, else `queue.default`.
    - `start()` calls `RunProcessor::start()` itself. Only a queue run that is running afterwards gets a new generation, counted under the lock `tolk:run-generation:{id}`, and lanes 1 to `concurrency.queue` (at least one); a finished run is left alone. Every call makes a new generation, so `resume` and `retry` replace the lanes a run may still have. `takeOver()` changes only `driver` and `generation` of a queue run, not its status or `progressedAt`; items a queue lane still holds stay claimed until their claim expires.
    - `Jobs\WorkRun` (final). The constructor sets `onConnection(RunDispatcher::connection())`, `onQueue(runs.queue.name)` and `$timeout`. `retryAfter()` reads the connection's `retry_after`, or 90 (`WorkRun::RETRY_AFTER`) when it sets none, as SQS connections do. `deadlineSeconds()` and `timeoutSeconds()` are the task's formulas: 25 and 80 seconds by default, which leaves 5 seconds between the latest end (25 + 45 + 5) and the timeout. The job is safe while `retry_after > http.timeout.web + 20` (T9.6); the config comment recommends + 40, so the lane's deadline stays at 20 seconds or more. A lane works as worker `queue-{lane}`, only while the run is `running` and has the job's generation; `applyReady()` gets `WorkRun::APPLY_SECONDS` (5).
    - Dispatching again: after both calls the lane reads the run again. A run that still runs with the job's generation has work left, because both calls finalize a running run without unfinished items. The delay is the smallest of the `WorkReport`'s wait (when it `remains`), the `ApplyReport`'s wait (when it `remains`) and 0 when the apply sent items back; 1 second when none is known. A queue that runs jobs at once (`SyncJob`: `sync`, `deferred` and `background` with `runs.execution` `queue`) ignores delays, so there the lane sleeps through the delay (`Sleep`) before it dispatches the next lane, which runs inside the same process.
    - `failed()` dispatches the same lane and generation with `restarts + 1`, at most `WorkRun::MAX_RESTARTS` (5) times in a row, and only while the run is running with that generation; a lane that ends normally dispatches its successor with `restarts` 0. `RunNotFound` (the run was deleted) ends a lane quietly. A lane killed at its timeout leaves the item lock `tolk:item:{run}:{index}` held until its TTL (`runs.claim_ttl`) expires; the restarted lane gets its own item back, finds it busy, and comes back every second until then.
    - `RunFixtures::pending()` creates a stored run without starting it (`started()` uses it).

- [x] **T8.7 Runs over HTTP**
  - Routes (add them to the route test): `GET runs` (`runs.index`), `POST runs` (`runs.store`), `GET runs/{run}` (`runs.show`), `GET runs/{run}/state`, `POST runs/{run}/estimate`, `POST runs/{run}/start`, `POST runs/{run}/tick`, `POST runs/{run}/apply`, `POST runs/{run}/cancel`, `POST runs/{run}/resume`, `POST runs/{run}/retry`, `POST runs/{run}/take-over`.
  - Authorization (§A14): `index`, `store`, `show`, `estimate`, `start`, `resume`, `retry`, `take-over` need `run bulk translations`; `state`, `tick`, `apply` and `cancel` are allowed for the run's creator or a user with that permission, checked in the controller.
  - `RunsController`:
    - `index`, `show`: Inertia pages (T8.9). `store`: body is a `RunRequest`; creates the run as `pending` and returns its URL. Nothing starts yet (D19).
    - `state`: JSON with the run, counts, a page of items, the estimate so far, and `stalled` (running, queue driver, no progress for longer than `runs.claim_ttl`; or no item claimed within 30 seconds of the start). Built in T8.1: `Run::lastActivityAt()` (the latest of its times, `progressedAt` included) measures progress; "no item claimed" is `startedAt` still null while `progressedAt`, set by `start()`, is older than 30 seconds.
    - `estimate`: body `from`; `RunPlanner::estimate()` with a budget of about 1.5 seconds; returns the estimate so far and the next index.
    - `start`: `RunDispatcher::start()`. `resume`: back to `running` and `start()` again. `retry`: failed items back to `pending` with attempts reset, and the items skipped with reason `dependency_failed` back to `pending` too (T8.1 skips them at once when their dependency fails, and would skip them again only if it failed again), run back to `running`, and `start()` again. An unknown run id is a 404 (`RunRepository::find()` returns null; item writes on a deleted run throw `RunNotFound`). `take-over`: `RunDispatcher::takeOver()`.
    - `tick`: body `nonce` and `lane`. The worker id is built here, `browser-{userId}-{nonce}-{lane}`, after validating both parts; it is never taken from the request as a whole. `RunProcessor::translate()` with a deadline of `runs.tick_seconds` and a claim TTL of `runs.tick_seconds` + `http.timeout.web` + 30 seconds. Raise the PHP time limit accordingly. Returns the state plus `wait`.
    - `apply`: `RunProcessor::applyReady()` with a deadline of about 10 seconds; returns the state plus `wait`.
    - `cancel`: sets `cancelled` and finalizes.
  - Built in T8.3: `store` builds `new RunRequest(..., driver: RunDispatcher::resolveDriver(), user: $request->user())`, after checking `translator` and `reviewer` against the catalog (§A14); `RunPlanner::build()` throws `UnknownSite`, `InvalidArgumentException` (unknown collection, invalid options), `TargetNotFound` and the model exceptions of `PlanOptions::fromSettings()` (`InvalidModelRef`, `MissingDefaultModel`, `NoProviderConfigured`), which become 422s. Then `RunRepository::create($planned->run, $planned->items)`. `estimate` answers `EstimateProgress::toArray()` (the estimate keys plus `next`); `state` can read the same from `run.estimate`, where `next` null means finished and a missing estimate means not started. A call from `from: 0` starts the estimate over.
  - Built in T8.4: `tick` answers the state plus `WorkReport::toArray()` (`units`, `items`, `remains`, `wait`). `RunProcessor::start()` turns only a `pending` run into a running one and leaves `cancelled`, `aborted` and completed runs alone, so `resume` and `retry` set the status to `running` (and clear `error`) first and then call `start()`, which sets `progressedAt` again and resets the count of items failed in a row for an unavailable provider. `retry` resets `attempts` and `waits` of the items it puts back; their `planId` may stay, because `translate()` re-plans when the plan expired and otherwise continues it without repeating finished units.
  - Built in T8.5: `apply` answers the state plus `ApplyReport::toArray()` (`items`, `returned`, `remains`, `wait`); `applyReady()` waits for the global write lock only until its deadline. `cancel` sets `cancelled` and calls `finalize()`, which commits the content saved so far and fires `RunFinished`; an apply that was running meanwhile finalizes again for what it saved. `resume` and `retry` must also clear the run's `finishedAt` and `finalizedAt`, or `RunFinished` does not fire at the next end; `retry` must clear `reason` and `error` of the items it puts back (a `source_changed` reason left on a pending item makes the next change of its origin fail it at once). Items carry `corrections` (by handle) and `reason` `no_corrections` on a `done` review item that saved nothing; a `skipped` item with `target_changed` was edited by someone meanwhile.
  - Built in T8.6: `RunDispatcher::resolveDriver()` is static. `RunDispatcher::start()` calls `RunProcessor::start()` itself, so `start`, `resume` and `retry` call only the dispatcher (after `resume` and `retry` set the run running). For a queue run every call makes a new generation and pushes `concurrency.queue` lanes; the lanes of the older generation end at their next step. `takeOver()` changes only the driver (to `browser`) and the generation; the page then starts its lanes. Items a queue lane held stay claimed until their claim expires (`runs.claim_ttl`). On the `sync` connection with `runs.execution` `queue`, `start()` runs the lanes inside the request, so test the queue cases with `Queue::fake()` (`Queue::assertPushed(WorkRun::class, n)`). A lane that failed 5 times in a row is not dispatched again, so a queue run whose lanes all stopped shows as `stalled`.
  - Built (planner's details):
    - Routes: `can:run bulk translations` guards `index`, `store`, `show`, `estimate`, `start`, `resume` and `retry`. `state`, `tick`, `apply`, `cancel` and `take-over` (route name `tolk.runs.take-over`, method `takeOver`; for the creator since the 2026-10-14 review, so the editor's banner can move a stalled queue push to its page) take `Http\Requests\RunFollowRequest`, whose `authorize()` lets through `run bulk translations`, or the run's creator (`createdBy.id`) who still has `translate content`; for anyone else a missing run is a 403, for a user with the permission a 404 (`code` `run_not_found`). Its `authorize()` runs before validation, so a forbidden `tick` without a body is a 403, not a 422.
    - `Http\RunState` (final readonly): `for($run, $page, $perPage, $status)` gives `run` (`Run::toArray()` without `generation`, `saves` and `committed_saves`, plus `cost`: `Pricing::total()` of its usage, null when a model has no price), `counts`, `items` (one page, without `claim` and `plan_id`), `pagination` (`page`, `per_page`, `total`, `last_page`; a page past the last is answered with the last, so a filtered list that shrank below the page asked for still shows its items) and `stalled`. The estimate is `run.estimate`, not repeated. `summary($run)` (run, `counts`, `url`) serves the index; `urls($run)` gives `index`, `show`, `state`, `estimate`, `start`, `tick`, `apply`, `cancel`, `resume`, `retry`, `take_over`. `stalled()`: running, driver `queue`, and either `startedAt` null with `progressedAt` older than `RunState::FIRST_CLAIM_SECONDS` (30), or `lastActivityAt()` older than `runs.claim_ttl`.
    - Every answer that carries the state takes `page`, `per_page` (at most 200, default 50) and `status` (`RunStateRequest`). `tick` and `apply` answer the state plus `report`: `WorkReport::toArray()` or `ApplyReport::toArray()` (merging them flat would clash with the state's `items`). They work only on a run whose driver is `browser`; for another driver they do nothing and answer an empty report (`remains` false, `wait` null), so the page's lanes end and it polls.
    - `tick`: `nonce` is 8 to 64 letters and digits, `lane` 1 to `concurrency.browser`; `RunTickRequest::workerId()` is `browser-{userId}-{nonce}-{lane}`. Deadline `runs.tick_seconds`, claim TTL that + `http.timeout.web` + 30, time limit that + 15. `apply`: worker `browser-{userId}-apply`, deadline `RunsController::APPLY_SECONDS` (10), time limit 60. `estimate`: budget `RunsController::ESTIMATE_BUDGET_MS` (1500).
    - `store`: body `sites`, `source_site`, at least one of `collections`, `taxonomies`, `globals`, `navs`, `entry_ids`, `term_ids` (lists), and `options` with only `RunRequest::OPTIONS` keys; translator and reviewer pass `ModelReference` and `CatalogModel`. A misconfigured `runs.execution` is not caught (a 500), because it is the site's error, not the request's. The planner's exceptions are 422s with `code` `invalid_run`. The answer is 201 with `run` (the id) and `url`.
    - Status changes go through `RunProcessor`, under its `tolk:run-status:{id}` lock: `cancel()` (a pending or running run becomes `cancelled`, its `translating` items `pending` with their plans, then `finalize()`; a finished run is left alone), `resume()` (a cancelled or aborted run runs again with `error`, `finishedAt` and `finalizedAt` cleared; true for a running one; false otherwise, which the controller answers with 409 `not_resumable`), and `retry()` (nothing for a pending run; otherwise `RunRepository::retryFailed()` and, when it put any item back, the run runs again as in `resume()`). `retryFailed()` (new in the contract) puts every failed item and every item skipped with `dependency_failed` back to `pending` in one write, so `commit()` does not skip one again for a dependency reset later in the loop; it clears `reason`, `error`, `attempts`, `waits`, the claim and `available_at`, and keeps `planId`, `warnings` and `usage`. The controller calls `RunDispatcher::start()` after `resume`, and after a `retry` that put items back; `retry` also answers `retried`.
    - `index` renders `tolk::Runs/Index` with `runs` (`RunState::summary()` of the 50 most recent); `show` renders `tolk::Runs/Show` with `state`, `urls` and `lanes` (`concurrency.browser`). The Vue pages come in T8.9.
    - `EditorController`'s `fail()` and `raiseTimeLimit()` moved to the traits `Http\Controllers\Cp\Concerns\FailsWithJson` and `RaisesTimeLimit` (which now takes the seconds), shared with `RunsController`. `RunFixtures::commits()` replaces `RunApplyTest`'s `runCommits()`: turn git on after the test's users are saved, or their saves are counted as commits.

- [x] **T8.8 Lanes in the browser: `resources/js/runs/ticker.js`**
  - `startLanes({ api, urls, lanes, nonce, onState, signal })`: each lane posts `tick` in a loop until nothing remains or the run is final; honours `wait`; retries a failed request with backoff up to 5 times, then stops that lane and reports it.
  - `startApplyLoop({ api, urls, onState, signal })`: one loop posting `apply` while translated items exist or lanes are still working; honours `wait`.
  - `pollState({ api, url, interval, onState, signal })` for queue-driven runs.
  - `estimateLoop({ api, url, onEstimate, signal })`: calls `estimate` until the next index is `null`.
  - The nonce is generated once per page load.
  - Built in T8.3: `estimateLoop` starts from `run.estimate.next` when the run already has an estimate (null: finished, nothing to do), else from 0; the server ignores a call from any other index and answers with the stored progress, so continue from the `next` it returns.
  - Built in T8.5: an `apply` answer's `returned` counts items sent back to `pending` (their plan expired or their origin changed), which need the lanes again: restart lanes that already stopped when it is above 0, or when the state shows `pending` items while no lane runs. Its `wait` is null when nothing remains to apply, 1 while another process saves content.
  - Built in T8.7: `tick` and `apply` answer the run's state plus `report` (`response.report.wait`, `.remains`, and for `apply` `.returned`), not a flat report. The tick body is `nonce` (8 to 64 letters and digits, so generate one without dashes) and `lane` (1 to `concurrency.browser`, which the run page gets as its `lanes` prop). A `tick` or `apply` of a run whose driver is not `browser` does nothing and answers `report.remains` false, so lanes end when a run is not the browser's. `estimate` answers the `Estimate` keys plus `next`. Errors carry `code`: 404 `run_not_found`, 409 `not_resumable`. Every state answer takes `page`, `per_page` and `status`, so the loops can keep the page the user is on.
  - Built (planner's details):
    - Every loop also takes `onFailure`, `query` (a function giving the item page the state answers carry: `page`, `per_page`, `status`), and `sleep`, `retries`, `backoffMs` for tests. A failed request is repeated after 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 seconds while it is retryable (no answer, 408, 429, 5xx); a 401, 403, 404, 419 or 422 stops the loop at once. `onFailure` gets `{ lane, error }` from a lane and `{ error }` from the other loops, `error` being `{ status, code, message }` (`describeError()`). After an abort nothing more is reported, and a wait or backoff ends at once.
    - `startLanes()` returns a handle: `active` (lanes running), `failed`, `restart()` (starts again the lanes that ended because nothing remained, not those that failed; nothing after an abort), `idle()` (a promise for no lane running) and `subscribe()` (`{ lane, state }` per answer, `{ lane, ended }` per end). A lane ends when the run is final or `report.remains` is not true, and sleeps `report.wait` seconds, `IDLE_MS` (1 s) when it is null. `nonce()` is made once per module load from `crypto.getRandomValues()` (24 hex digits): `crypto.randomUUID()` does not exist on a CP served over plain HTTP, as the sandbox is.
    - `startApplyLoop({ ..., lanes })` takes that handle and resolves `{ state, error }`. While nothing remains to apply and a lane runs, it waits for a lane answer with `counts.translated` above 0, or a lane's end, instead of polling; an answer that came during its own request counts. It restarts the lanes whenever its answer shows `pending` items on a running browser run, which covers `returned` (returned items are `pending` in the same answer). `pollState()` fetches at once, then every `interval` (`POLL_MS`, 2 s), until the run is final; `estimateLoop({ ..., estimate })` takes the run's stored `run.estimate`.
    - `latest(onState)` wraps the page's callback: lanes and the apply loop answer in parallel, so a tick that read the run before the apply that completed it can arrive last. It drops a state whose `run.progressed_at` is older than the last one passed on, and a non-final state of the same second as a final one (`progressed_at` has second precision).
    - `api.js` sends a full URL (`http://` or `https://`) as it is, so the server's `urls` (absolute, from `cp_route()`) go to `api.post()` and `api.get()` directly.
  - **Self-check:** `resources/js/runs/ticker.test.js` with a fake API: lanes stop when done; `wait` is honoured; a failing lane stops after its retries; only one `apply` request is in flight at a time; the estimate loop accumulates.

Start ends the estimate: once the run is no longer `pending`, an unfinished estimate keeps its numbers and reads "Only N of M items were estimated before the run started." instead of "Estimating…".

Resume shows for a stalled run and for a cancelled or aborted run with unfinished items, Retry failed for a finished run with failed items. Leaving the page stops its lanes (the run stays `running`, and opening the page again continues it), except for a run the editor's push of the same page load follows, which the push drives again after a visit inside the CP (`pushes.lend()` hands it back with the page's last state). While the page drives the run, the "Keep this page open" notice says so and the page asks first: core's dirty state (`tolk-run`) for a visit inside the CP or Back, its own `beforeunload` listener for closing or reloading the tab; for a run the push follows the notice reads "Keep this tab open" and only closing the tab asks. Addon CSS: core's utility layer wins over the addon's, so a responsive class such as `sm:grid-cols-5` does not override a `grid-cols-2` that core also defines; use flex wrapping. The nav item is `ServiceProvider::registerNavigation()`.

The plan: a row per content type and site ("N to translate, M skipped"), "Segments" (with characters), "Estimated tokens" and "Estimated cost" (below a cent with two significant digits; "unknown" when a model has no price).

It reads the run again each round, updates the bar from the counts, sleeps a second at a time when nothing happened, and reports workers that exited with an error after the bar.
    - Ctrl-C and SIGTERM: `trap()` counts them; the loop then calls `RunProcessor::cancel()`, which commits and fires `RunFinished`, and waits for the workers, which ignore SIGINT (the terminal sends it to them too) and stop before their next unit once the run is cancelled; a second Ctrl-C stops them at once. During the scan Ctrl-C deletes the run. The question is Laravel Prompts' `confirm()`, which reads the terminal in raw mode, so Ctrl-C arrives as a key: `Prompt::cancelUsing()`, set while it is asked, counts it as an interrupt and declines, and the command deletes the run, prints "Nothing was translated." and exits 1. The trap is still removed while the question is asked, because a pending read holds back PHP's signal handlers until a key, so a signal ends the command at once there, and so does Ctrl-C where Prompts falls back to Symfony's question (Windows; under tests, where `expectsConfirmation()` answers it). Without a terminal (stdin piped) the question is Symfony's, because Prompts would take the default without reading the piped answer. `tests/Support/TerminalTranslate.php` runs the command on a pseudo-terminal outside the testing environment to hold the Ctrl-C key to this. It also runs it with "no" piped in, which must decline. Any exception in the loop cancels the run (rescued) and still waits for the workers; a run deleted meanwhile ends with "The run was deleted while it was translated." and exit 1. Laravel traps no signals under tests: the tests turn `Signals::resolveAvailabilityUsing()` on and send themselves SIGINT.

Unfinished items are counted with the run page's URL on its own line, those translated but not saved (`translated`, `applying`) apart from those not translated: for the first the advice is to resume the run within `plan_ttl`, since a new run would pay for them again, and after an abort the advice is to resume once the cause is fixed, never to run the command again; an aborted run prints its error; `summarize()` gains a table of the failed targets and their errors; the out-of-scope table comes from `run.scope.out_of_scope` (text-bearing fields only, so `unsupported_fieldtype` rows are gone) and ends with "Run php please tolk:doctor for details.". `savedCount()` and `reportPlan()` are gone (`describePlan()` returns the line). Exit code 1 when an item failed or the run was aborted or cancelled. A page skipped at build with `root_missing` is skipped, not failed, so that case now exits 0 (the planner's status since T8.3).
    - `tolk:work`: `$hidden` and `$hiddenInPlease`, validates the run and the lane, worker `cli-{lane}`, deadline `WorkCommand::DEADLINE_SECONDS` (30), claim TTL `runs.claim_ttl`, `Settings::fresh()` before every call, sleeps the reported wait (1 second when none is known), and ends when `WorkReport::$remains` is false or the run was deleted.
    - CP: since the command starts its runs with `RunProcessor::start()`, `RunDispatcher::start()` hands a `cli` run to `resolveDriver()`'s driver first, so Start, Resume and Retry on the run page work for a CLI run whose command has ended (Ctrl-C, a crash).

It has no `confirm` (changed after 1.0.0-beta.1): core's confirmation is a dialog without a trigger, so it leaves the focus on the page's body when it closes (WCAG 2.4.3), and on Esc the action's `run` is never called. `run` appends Tolk's own `components/TranslateFieldConfirm.vue` (`Statamic.$components.append()`, registered in `cp.js` as `CONFIRM`; core `Modal` through `createModal()`) and removes it once it answered; Esc and Cancel give the focus back to the field's actions menu (core's `[data-ui-dropdown-trigger]` in the header of the field whose label is `field_{handle}`), and Translate moves it into the banner (its Cancel, or its planning line until Cancel appears, `[data-tolk-focus]`), whose focus keeper keeps it there.
  - `visible`, all of: the field is top-level (`!payload.fieldPathPrefix`); it is writable and `payload.config.localizable`; the user has `translate content`; the base form is a localization whose origin has this handle; **no container other than `base` is mounted** (`registry.others()` is empty). The last condition matters: actions are registered per fieldtype, so they also render in stacked editors, global-set forms and the addon's own settings form, and for `text` fields the payload gives no way to tell which form the field belongs to (R3 §3.3). When the payload has `vm`, also require `vm.publishContainer.name === 'base'`. Read these from the reactive store: `visible` is evaluated inside a cached computed. Built in T5.5: `registry.others()` (names) and `registry.base()` are reactive, and so is `useEditorStore().phaseOf(name)`.
  - `run`: do **not** return a promise, which also means `run` must not be declared `async`. On `text` fields and other composition-API fieldtypes the payload has no `vm`, and core's loading handler throws (R3 §3.4). Start the work and return nothing; show progress through the banner's store (phase `working`, with the field's label) and report errors with `Statamic.$toast.error()`.
  - Before applying, require `payload.value` to equal the base context's value for that handle; otherwise do nothing and show an error.
  - It plans with `fields: [handle]`, mode `all`, `overwrite_manual: true`, and applies through `fill.applyFields()` on the base context.
  - Built in T5.7: `applyFields(ctx, fields, { reference, plan, lastChangeAt, planValues, delivered })` (see T5.7's "Built" bullet); use the base container's store entry for `delivered` and `lastChangeAt`, and `planValues` `{ [handle]: payload.value }` so the check above and the edited check agree.
  - Built in T5.8: after the value check, `translations.translate('base', { mode: 'all', fields: [handle], overwriteManual: true })` does the plan, the units and the fill, sets the phases (Save is refused meanwhile, `BUSY`) and takes a snapshot of that field only; it returns null when a translation already runs. It sends the form's other unsaved edits as `skip`, which a single requested field does not need but does no harm.
  - Built in T5.9: on a localization created in the form, `translations.translate()` first puts the origin's slug back (the store's `created` and `slugKept`), so translating the title through the action does not change the slug either.

  - Built (planner's details): `createFieldActions()` in `editor/fieldActions.js` (`register()` once, `action`, `visible`, `run`; the shared `fieldActions` is registered in `cp.js`). `visible` also needs the store entry of `base` to belong to the open item (`container.reference === ctx.reference.value`: the CP keeps the store across Inertia visits, and a global set's form is also named `base`), a model (`!needsSetup(status)`), the handle in the status's `localization.fields` with a status other than `not_applicable`, and a phase outside `BUSY`: the dropdown ignores `disabled`, so the action hides while a translation runs. The value check compares deeply without regard to key order. `translations.translate()` gained `replace`: the requested fields are kept out of `skip`, because a field the editor typed into would otherwise be skipped as `edited_in_form`, against the confirmation's text; Retry keeps it. After the translation a toast reports the error the phase ended with, or a field that was not filled (`describeSummary()`'s failed, edited or "No fields were translated." line), but nothing after Cancel or a switch. The banner's working line names the field of a one-field translation ("Translating :field into :site…", `data-tolk-working-field`).

Build the URLs with `RunState::urls()` (keep only those the creator may call, `EditorPush::URLS`) and start the run with `RunDispatcher::start()`. Store `createdBy` through `RunRequest::$user`.

The worker id contains the page's `nonce()`, made once per page load, so after a reload the lanes are new workers: an item the closed page held stays claimed until its claim expires (`runs.tick_seconds` + `http.timeout.web` + 30 seconds, 80 by default) and is translated then. A reload mid-run therefore resumes the progress display at once, but its last item can take that long.

It needs only `state`, `tick`, `apply` and `cancel` for a running run (`estimate`, `start`, `resume`, `retry` and `take_over` only for its other buttons), so the banner can use it as it is. `describeProgress()` and `describeResult()` in `runs/describe.js` give the counts and the closing sentence.

It reads `EditorStatus::for()` first, so a user who may not edit the item gets 403, and an unknown reference 404 `unknown_reference`. The sites must be listed in the origin block (the item's direct localizations and, for the root of its family, the sites the family has no localization in, among the sites the user may view): otherwise 422 `invalid_sites`, and 422 `not_an_origin` without an origin block. While the user's push from the item runs, a second one is a 409 `run_active` carrying `active_run`. The `RunRequest` has `entryIds: [id]`, `sourceSite` the item's site, the user, and the new `RunRequest::$reference` (`run.scope.reference`, null for every other run). `NoProviderConfigured` and `MissingDefaultModel` are 422 `no_translator`, the planner's other exceptions 422 `invalid_run`; a plan without items is a 403 when targets were skipped as unauthorized, else 422 `nothing_to_translate` (with its own message for a collection in `excluded_collections`). The answer is 201 with `EditorPush::payload()` (`run`, `driver`, `lanes` = `concurrency.browser`, `urls` with only `state`, `tick`, `apply`, `cancel`) plus `state` (`RunState::for()`), so the banner follows it at once.
    - `Http\EditorPush::active($reference, $user)`: the user's `running` run with that `scope.reference` among the `EditorPush::RECENT` (50) most recent runs. A pending one never counts: the banner could not start it, since `start` needs `run bulk translations`. `editor/status` has `active_run` (that payload, or null) only when it has an origin block, and `defaults.new_status` (the `new_localization_status` setting) for the modal's default.
    - `resources/js/editor/push.js`: `createPushes()` / the shared `pushes` (`start`, `adopt`, `resume`, `cancel`, `takeOver`, `dismiss`, `lend`, `following`) and the pure `pushChoices()`, `updateSites()`, `missingSites()` and `describePush()`. The store's new `push` key holds `phase` (`starting`, `running`, `finished`, `error`), `run`, `driver`, `sites`, `state`, `failures`, `missing` (the sites without a localization before the run), `cancelling`, `takingOver` and `error`. There is one follower (`createFollower()`) per run id, not per form: it keeps driving when the form switches site or the user goes to another CP page (except while the run's own page drives the run, §A12 `browser`), and it writes to every container whose `push.run` is its run. `attachBanner()` gained `onStatus`, called with every status it keeps, which is `pushes.adopt()`: a switch back or a reload reads the run's state and follows it again (an existing follower restarts its loops). A final state sets `finished` and reloads `editor/status`, which turns the new localizations into links; `reload` is offered when a site missing before the run now exists. A push answered with `run_active` adopts the named run instead.
    - `resources/js/components/OriginPush.vue`, inside the banner's origin block: **Translate into…** opens a modal with a checkbox per site with `translate` (all checked), "New localizations" with Drafts and Same as origin (only while a missing site is checked; default `defaults.new_status`) and "Review after translating" (default `defaults.review`), and sends mode `missing`. **Update translations** sends the sites with `update` in mode `update` and leaves review to the setting. While it runs: "Translating into Greek, German…", a progress bar, ":finished of :total translations finished.", Cancel, for the browser driver a note to keep the page open (the run continues when the entry is opened again), for a stalled run a warning (`describePush().stalled`) with, on the queue, Translate in this page instead (`pushes.takeOver()`), and when a loop gave up its error with Try again (`pushes.resume()`). The result: one sentence, a line per site with a link, the item's status and its reason or error, Reload when there are new localizations, and Close.
    - "Save first" is decided by `registry.dirtyHandles('base')` (text that differs from the clean values, D27), not by core's dirty flag: in the sandbox core flags every article and page form dirty about 250 ms after it loads, because fieldtypes re-emit normalised values after core's 500 ms window, which would disable both buttons on every form that was just opened.
    - Tests: `tests/Feature/Cp/EditorPushTest.php` (18), `EditorRequestsTest` (push validation), `RoutesTest` (the route), `EditorStatusTest` (`active_run` and `defaults.new_status` in every payload), `resources/js/editor/push.test.js` (27), `banner.test.js` (`onStatus`), `store.test.js` (`push`); `tests/Browser/editor-push.spec.js` (3).

## Phase 9 — Other content types and the remaining commands

Goal: "translate the whole website to a new language". Revisions, terms, global sets and navigation join the pipeline, and the whole-site, status, doctor and maintenance commands are added. The CLI-only commands for translation files and forms come last (§A16). All commands live in `src/Console/Commands/`, follow rule 15, declare `handle(): int`, and keep their logic in services so the CP can reuse it. Commands are registered only in the console (R4 §14.1).

- [x] **T9.1 Revisions**
  - `EntryTarget`: when `$entry->revisionsEnabled()`, `hasOverride()` and `targetValue()` read the localization through `fromWorkingCopy()` (§A6), and `apply()` follows the revisions rules of §A11 instead of throwing `NotSupportedYet`:
    - a new localization is created with `store([...])` and, when it is to be published, published through `publish([...])`;
    - a published localization is updated in its working copy and the live entry is left alone;
    - `publish_changes` publishes only when no working copy existed before this operation and the user may publish; otherwise the changes stay in the working copy and the item gets a warning;
    - an unpublished localization is saved directly.
  - Verify each call in vendor first (R4 §11): `makeWorkingCopy()`, `fromWorkingCopy()`, `store()`, `publish()`, `hasWorkingCopy()`.
  - In T4.16's `EntryTarget`: `applyFresh()` throws `NotSupportedYet::revisions()` first; `createLocalization()` makes the first, empty save (replace it with `store()` here); `publishesAsOrigin()` decides the published state of a new localization; `save()` wraps `updateLastModified()` and the `false` check.
  - `StateWriter::confirm()` and `StatusResolver` need no change: they read the target through `EntryTarget`. `EntryTarget::formFields()` (T5.3) already parents the blueprint to the localization's `fromWorkingCopy()`, as core's edit page does.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/RevisionsTest.php` on a collection with revisions enabled: a new localization created as a draft has a revision; one created with `mirror` from a published origin is published through a revision, never saved directly as published; a published localization updated by a run changes only its working copy, and its status reads current from the working copy so a second run does nothing; `publish_changes` publishes when there was no working copy; with a foreign change already in the working copy, `publish_changes` leaves the live entry unchanged and warns; the editor flow on a published localization (simulate the CP save into the working copy, then `confirm`) promotes the pending entries.
  - Built (planner's details): `EntryTarget::working()` (the localization's `fromWorkingCopy()`, cached per target object) backs `hasOverride()` and `targetValue()`; `applyFresh()` normalises and writes into it, then `saveChanges()` saves as §A11 "Revisions" says and returns its warnings. The two warnings are "The {site} localization already had unpublished changes, so the translation was added to them and not published." and "The translation was saved as unpublished changes: the user who started it may not publish this entry." `NotSupportedYet::revisions()` is gone. Tests point Statamic's revisions store at a temporary folder (`TestCase::defineEnvironment()`, `statamic.stache.stores.revisions.directory`). `tests/Feature/RevisionsTest.php` (13).

  - Built in T9.2: a taxonomy's terms are its default site's (`Term::query()->where('taxonomy', …)->where('site', $taxonomy->sites()->first())`, as `RunPlanner::terms()` reads them), and the taxonomy's first site is never a target; `TargetFactory::forTerm($term, $site)` builds each target, which always exists, so terms are never `missing`.
  - Built in T9.3: a global set has one target per site, `TargetFactory::forGlobalSet($set, $site)`. It builds a target for any known site, so the scanner itself leaves out the sites for which `GlobalsTarget::untranslatableReasonFor($set, $site)` is not null (a site the set lacks would read `missing`); the origin-less site the others translate from is the source (`RunPlanner::globalItems()`). Add `--globals=*` to `tolk:status`, as `tolk:translate` has it.
  - Built in T9.4: a nav has one target per site other than the default site, whether or not it has a tree there (`TargetFactory::forNav($nav, $site)`, which translates from the default site's tree; a site without a tree reads `missing`); a nav without a tree in the default site has nothing to translate (`RunPlanner` reports `no_source_tree`), so leave it out. Add `--nav=*` to `tolk:status` as `tolk:translate` has it, so the Overview (T10.5) can scan nav rows.
  - **Self-check:** `tests/Feature/State/StatusScannerTest.php` (counts on a fixture with one target in each status; a tiny budget returns a cursor, and resuming gives the same totals) and `tests/Feature/Console/StatusCommandTest.php` (table and `--json`).
  - Built (planner's details): `State\ScanResult` (final readonly): `counts` keyed by every `ItemStatus` value in its order, `untracked` (manual fields without a known source fingerprint), `scanned`, `cursor`; `empty()`, `withTarget(TargetStatus)`, `add()` (sums, and takes the later call's cursor), `isFinished()`, `toArray()` / `fromArray()` (lenient: a damaged count reads 0). `StatusScanner::scan()` resolves at least one target per call; the cursor is the position of the next target as a digit string, so content that changes between calls can shift a target into or out of the totals; an invalid cursor throws `InvalidArgumentException`, one past the end finishes at once; an unknown container throws `InvalidArgumentException` with `RunPlanner`'s messages ("Collection [x] not found."), an unknown site `UnknownSite`; a site the container has no targets in gives an empty, finished result. Entries go through `EntryFamilies::roots()` / `targets()` and `TargetFactory::forEntry()` on the loaded root (the T9.3–T9.7 review: `fromDescription()` ran an `Entry::find()` per target), so an existing localization is read against its own origin (chains). Two more public methods for the Overview: `containers(ContentType)` (what is covered when nothing is named: collections and taxonomies with more than one site minus `excluded_collections` / `excluded_taxonomies`, global sets with more than one site, every nav) and `sites(ContentType, $container)` (the sites with at least one target, in the container's order: a collection's sites that some family's root is not in, none without entries; a taxonomy's sites but its first, none without terms; a global set's sites with `untranslatableReasonFor() === null`; every site but the default for a nav with a tree in the default site). `tolk:status {--site=*} {--collection=*} {--taxonomy=*} {--globals=*} {--nav=*} {--json}`: without a container option it covers `containers()` of each type, named containers are covered even when the settings exclude them; one row per container × `sites()`, filtered by `--site`, every container checked before anything is scanned; each cell scanned in calls of `StatusCommand::BUDGET_MS` (1,000 ms). Table columns Type (Collection, Taxonomy, Global set, Navigation), Handle, Site, the five counts and "Untracked fields", then "{n} fields were translated by hand without a record of their origin … Run php please tolk:baseline to record it." `--json` prints a list of `{type, container, site, counts, untracked, scanned}`, `[]` when there is nothing. Exit 1 for an unknown site or container, else 0. In the sandbox it takes about 1.2 s on the demo content.

  - `Audit::run(): array` returns checks, each `['id', 'level' (ok, warning, error), 'title', 'detail', 'items']`:

At least one provider is configured; the translator, reviewer and fallback models resolve (a stored reference that does not parse throws `InvalidModelRef` from `Settings`, T3.4; report it as an error naming the setting); the fake engine is off outside local environments; the host configures no `ai.providers.tolk_{provider}` entry (`Providers::name()`), which would make `Ai::build()` refuse the addon's provider names (error, T2.1, T3.7). The error condition is exact: at `retry_after <= http.timeout.web + 20` the lane's shortest deadline (5 seconds), one provider call and the apply step (5 seconds) reach the job's timeout. Below `http.timeout.web + 40` the lane's deadline is under 20 seconds, which works but makes more jobs.

    5. State driver: the path is writable and inside a folder Statamic's git integration tracks, when that is on.
    6. Per blueprint (collections, taxonomies, global sets): top-level fields with a supported fieldtype that are not localizable (reason `not_localizable` from `TranslatableFields::explain()`, given `auto_title` for collections that generate titles and the `excluded_fields` setting as context, §A2). `title` is the common one.
    7. Nested fields that look technical (`TranslatableFields::nestedTechnicalFields()`), as suggestions to switch translation off.
    8. Collections and taxonomies not enabled for every site; global sets with a site that has no origin.

An origin-less site that other sites of the set name as origin is the set's source, not a problem (as `RunPlanner::globalItems()` decides). A set without a blueprint has only inferred `text` fields, none localizable, so check 6 cannot fix it with `makeLocalizable()`: report `no_blueprint` here (create a blueprint with localizable fields) instead of listing its fields as `not_localizable`.
    9. Information only: revisions, git automation, static caching, and what the addon does in each case; how many PHP workers browser-driven runs occupy (`concurrency.browser` lanes plus one). For revisions (T9.1): name the collections that have them; new localizations get a revision, translations of a published localization go to its working copy, and `publish_changes` (`--publish-changes`) publishes them only when it had no working copy before.
  - `Audit::makeLocalizable(string $blueprintNamespace, string $handle, array $fields): int` sets `localizable: true` on the named top-level fields and saves the blueprint (verify the blueprint mutation API in vendor). Fields that come from an imported fieldset cannot be flagged this way; report them instead.
  - `tolk:doctor {--fix-localizable} {--json}`: prints the checks; with `--fix-localizable`, asks per blueprint before changing it. Exit code 1 when any check is `error`.
  - `CLAUDE.md`'s Commands section names `php please tolk:doctor` as the example of a sandbox command, marked *(T9.6)* (written in T1.6). Remove the marker once the self-check below has run it in the sandbox.
  - `ReportsTranslations::summarizeOutOfScope()` (T4.18) ends with "Make it localizable in its blueprint to translate it. Run php please tolk:doctor for details." when a field is `not_localizable` (T8.10, as the run page says); point it at `tolk:doctor --fix-localizable`.
  - From T6.8: `nestedTechnicalFields()` returns `{path, handle, type, label}` per field (`body.text.anchor`, "Body › Text › Anchor"), walks only top-level fields that are or could be in scope, and leaves out `text` fields with another input type; in the sandbox the article blueprint lists `body.text.anchor` and nothing else.
