T3.5 done 2026-10-02: `src/Support/Language.php` (final readonly: `site`, `name`, `locale`, `lang`, `region`, `direction`; `describe()` gives `Greek (Greece), locale el_GR`, or `Greek, locale el` without a region) and `src/Support/Languages.php` (`forSite(Site|string)`, stateless, resolved through the container; an unknown handle throws the new `Exceptions\UnknownSite`). The name is looked up for the primary subtag of `Site::lang()` (`pt` for `pt_BR`), not the whole `lang`, because the dictionary's regional entries (`'pt_BR' => 'Portuguese (Brazil)'`) would repeat the region in `describe()`; the lookup is `Lang::get('statamic::dictionary-languages.{code}', [], 'en', false)`, with the fallback locale switched off, since `trans(…, 'en')` falls back to `app.fallback_locale`, which may not be English on the host. Then `Locale::getDisplayLanguage($code, 'en')`, then the site name (its handle when the name is empty). Region comes from `Locale::getRegion()`/`getDisplayRegion($locale, 'en')` and is null without intl, without a region subtag, or when ICU only echoes the code back (unknown or private-use codes such as `qaa`/`QM`, which ICU 77.1 returns unchanged). `locale`, `lang` and `direction` are Statamic's own (`Site::direction()`); a protected `intl()` seam lets the test switch intl off. `tests/Unit/LanguagesTest.php` (18 tests): `en_US`, `el_GR`, `de_DE`, `pt_BR` (also with `lang: pt_BR`), a locale without a region, `el_GR.UTF-8`, `de-AT`; `nb_NO` with `lang: no` (Norwegian) and without it (intl's Norwegian Bokmål); an unknown language falls back to the site name; with the app locale and the translator fallback set to `el` or `de`, the German site is still "German" (core's own lookup is not); a `de`-only dictionary line is never used; a host override of the English dictionary wins; Hebrew is `rtl`; without intl regions are null and an unknown dictionary code falls back to the site name. T4.1 done 2026-10-02: `src/Content/Fingerprint.php` (final, static): `of()` (16 hex of the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON), `ofLeaves()` (16 hex of the JSON list, `array_values` first), `ofText()` (12 hex), and public `canonical()`. Canonical form as §A4: lists keep order and every item (nulls and `''` included); maps are `ksort`ed with `SORT_STRING` and drop entries whose canonical value is `null`, `''` or `[]`, so a nested map that empties is dropped too; `true`/`false` become `'true'`/`'false'`; integers become their decimal string; floats become the plain decimal form, computed without the `precision` ini settings (shortest `%e` that parses back, expanded without an exponent; `1.0` → `1`, `-0.0` → `0`, `0.1 + 0.2` → `0.30000000000000004`); strings get `\r\n` → `\n`. Any other value (an object set by code) is normalised through its JSON form. JSON is encoded with `JSON_UNESCAPED_UNICODE | JSON_UNESCAPED_SLASHES | JSON_THROW_ON_ERROR`, so an unencodable value throws instead of hashing `false`. `ofLeaves()` and `ofText()` do not normalise line breaks, as §A4 specifies. `tests/Unit/FingerprintTest.php` (23 tests): golden hashes for the canonical JSON (sorted keys, unescaped Greek and slashes, the empty list `4f53cda18c2baa0c`), key order at every depth, list order and items, empty map entries at every depth, scalars to strings (data set of 12, ints, bools and floats), zero and `false` kept, CRLF, a YAML dump-and-parse round trip of a Bard/Replicator-like value, and hand-written YAML in another style (key order, `~`, flow map, quoted numbers, CRLF file line endings, which Symfony's parser turns into `\n`) against the same value built in PHP; `ofLeaves()` order and the fixed empty value; `ofText()` length. Mutations (no CRLF rule, no sort, no dropping, bools or floats cast with `(string)`) each fail tests. `vendor/bin/pest` 379 passed, Pint clean. §A4 now says keys are compared as strings and spells out the plain decimal form; no later task changed.

T4.3 done 2026-10-02: `src/Content/Markup/Tokenized.php` (final readonly: `text`, `kind`, `map`, `placeholders`), `Tokenizer.php`, `Placeholders.php` and `Renumber.php` (final, static), plus `SegmentKind::escapes()` (true for `plain` and `inline`). `Tokenizer::tokenize()` supports `plain` only and throws `InvalidArgumentException` for the other kinds; Antlers atoms are numbered `x1`, `x2`… in order (pairs and atoms count separately, as in the T2.1 probe), and the atom regex takes comments `{{# … #}}` and PHP blocks `{{? … ?}}` / `{{$ … $}}` whole, so a `}}` inside them does not end the tag, and includes the `@` of an escaped `@{{ … }}`; an unclosed `{{` stays text. Escaping uses `strtr` both ways, so `&amp;lt;` round-trips; `restore()` leaves an unknown atom in place (validation rejects it first). `Placeholders::validate()` returns every problem joined with `; ` as one sentence (usable as the repair `note`) and returns early only for an empty translation. Planner's details, written into §A3: rule 2 counts a nested placeholder as content and lets a pair that is empty in the source stay empty; rule 5's "tag-like" is a `<` before a letter, `/`, `!` or `?` (the HTML tag-open definition), closed by `>` or not, so `<img … ` with no `>` is caught and `Kids < 12` is not; rule 5 also rejects more `{{` than the source has (Antlers injection into fields with `antlers: true`); rule 7 counts `](` and line-initial `[label]:` not followed by an atom (T6.3 notes it). `Renumber` matches pairs only with pairs and atoms only with atoms (an HTML pair and an unpaired tag can share a descriptor); `back()` is `like()`'s mapping flipped. TASKS.md: §A3 rules 2 and 5, T6.2 (extend `restore()` to pairs), T6.3 (rule 7 already built).

T6.2 done 2026-10-04: `Tokenizer::tokenize()` supports `html` through one lexer regex (Antlers first, then text shaped like a paired placeholder, comments, whole `script`/`style`/`pre`/`code` elements, `<!…>`/`<?…>`, then tags whose Antlers and quoted attribute values are taken whole, possessively); a closing tag closes the nearest open tag of its name and the tags opened after it stay atoms, so pairs always nest; void and self-closing tags, comments and unmatched tags are atoms; no escaping. `Tokenized::$map` keeps a pair's closing tag under `/n`, and `restore()` puts pairs back for every kind. `splitHtmlBlocks()` uses the same lexer and cuts after top-level `p`, `h1`–`h6`, `ul`, `ol`, `blockquote`, `table`, `figure`, `div` and `pre`. `TextHandler` makes one `html` leaf of a string with HTML (no warning any more) and applies `LeafRules` to the visible text of the tokenized value; `Placeholders::visible()` decodes character references for `html`. `alt` and `title` attributes stay untranslated, for the docs. Details in T6.2's "Built" bullet. Tests: `TokenizerHtmlTest` (89: every HTML sample and 21 edge cases restore exactly; numbering and descriptors; crossed, implicitly closed and void tags stay nested; atoms; a script and the other whole elements; Antlers inside tags and quoted `>`; placeholder-shaped text; no escaping; a reordered restore; seven rejected additions; `Renumber`; a 425 KB document and a 120 KB tag that never closes; block splitting), `TextHandlerTest` (the HTML cases are now leaves: 12 identity cases, the `textarea-html` round trip, six HTML values that are not leaves), `PlaceholdersTest` +3 (`visible()`), `SegmentValidatorTest` +1 assertion, `TargetTranslatorTest` +2 (an HTML textarea is translated, restored, current and not sent again; reordered pairs read current). Four mutations of the tokenizer (closing tags not stored, intervening opens kept, no Antlers inside tags, void elements opening pairs) each fail a test. `vendor/bin/pest` 1436 passed, Pint clean; the built CSS gained `.table{display:table}`, because Tailwind reads `table` in `Tokenizer.php`. TASKS.md: §A3 (`html` placeholders, `visible()`), the T4.4 note, T6.3 (reuse the lexer), T6.5 (leaf rules on the visible piece), T13.1 (the attribute limit).
T6.3 done 2026-10-04: `Tokenizer::tokenize()` supports `markdown` (atoms in §A3), through `lex()`, the HTML lexer generalised to a pattern (`MARKDOWN_TOKENS` = the Markdown constructs, then T6.2's `HTML`), and `build()`; public `Tokenizer::markdown($text, $labels)`, `referenceLabels()` and `splitMarkdownBlocks()` (blocks at even indexes, separators at odd; fences and line-start comment/`script`/`pre`/`style`/`textarea` blocks are never cut and are cut out of their surroundings); `Tokenized::$references` turns a collapsed or shortcut reference into a full one in `restore()` once its text changed. `MarkdownHandler` registered for `markdown`: one leaf per block, whitespace kept outside, indented code by list context, no `maxLength`. `Placeholders::visible()` keeps `markdown` as written (decided: it holds its syntax anyway and the fieldtype has no limit). Every tokenizer scan is now possessive, so unclosed constructs in a 1.4 MB text stay within PHP's default backtrack limit (the lazy T4.3/T6.2 patterns failed above about 800 KB; Statamic lifts the limit by default). Tests: `TokenizerMarkdownTest` (76: every sample and 17 edge cases restore exactly; fences, code spans, destinations and the three title styles, an image in a link, definitions with a next-line title, collapsed and shortcut references before and after translation, footnotes, autolinks, raw HTML, hard breaks, attribute lists, no escaping, six rejected additions, `Renumber`, 400 KB, ten unclosed constructs at 1 MB under the default limit, which the old lazy patterns fail, and block splitting), `MarkdownHandlerTest` (33: identity, the `markdown-long` leaf list, separators, indented code and list continuations, same leaf count for short and long, a translation reads the same leaves), `TargetTranslatorTest` +1 (a markdown field translated block by block, current, nothing to do afterwards), `ScriptDataTest` and three browser scenarios (`editor-banner`, `editor-options`) updated, since the Greek article's hand-written Markdown excerpt is now a field (2 edited by hand, 2 untracked). `vendor/bin/pest` 1545 passed, `npm run test:browser` 26 passed, Pint clean, no CSS change; `tolk:translate --collection=articles --site=de` with the fake engine translated the excerpts in the sandbox. T8.3 done 2026-10-05: `src/Runs/RunRequest.php` (final readonly: the task's fields plus `driver`; `OPTIONS` with defaults, null meaning the settings decide; refuses an empty or malformed list, unknown options, an invalid mode or `new_status`, a model that is not a string; `ALL`, `all()`, `scope()`), `src/Runs/RunPlanner.php` (final readonly; `build()` validates sites, source site, collections and entries, resolves and stores the options through `PlanOptions::fromSettings()`, enumerates entry targets through `EntryFamilies::roots()` and returns `PlannedRun` with the unsaved pending run, the items and `skipped`; `estimate()`; `planOptions()` for T8.4), `src/Runs/PlannedRun.php`, `src/Runs/EstimateProgress.php` (`estimate`, `next`, `isFinished()`, `toArray()` / `fromArray()`), `RunItem::UNAUTHORIZED`. Planner's details in the task's Built note. In short: `items` is a list, not lazy, because `skipped` must be complete when the run is created and a dependency may point at a later item. Placement dependencies (root, parent) and `root_missing` apply only to localizations the run creates, outside mode review: an existing localization is never moved, so making it wait would only serialise the run and spread a parent's failure. The root dependency and the `root_missing` skip follow `EntryTarget::rootMissing()` (an empty target tree), and parents are read from the source site's tree as core's `makeLocalization()` does. The estimate stores `next` in the run's `estimate`: a call from 0 starts over, and a call from any other index than the stored one adds nothing, so a retried request cannot count twice. Tests: `tests/Feature/Runs/RunPlannerTest.php` (24: families × sites in order, the resolved options and scope of an unsaved run, request options over settings and the profile per driver, a selected localization resolved to its family, a source-site source, chains in update mode and through the source site, excluded, unconfigured and single-site collections with `'*'`, an unauthorized user, four invalid inputs, tree order with parent dependencies, an existing parent or child that waits for nothing and the warning for a parent outside the run, the source site's tree, a child of the root page in a tree that is not empty, root dependencies, `root_missing`, mode review, the estimate in tiny steps against one call with a fully translated target skipped and no model call, restart and stale calls, a stale run copy keeping its status, a review estimate, claimed items left alone) and `tests/Unit/Runs/RunRequestTest.php` (11). 31 mutations of `RunPlanner` each fail a test, except caching `PromptContext` per site (only speed); an `array_unique` that never had duplicates to remove was dropped, and the re-read before skipping is reached only by a race, so no test covers it. T8.4 done 2026-10-05: `src/Runs/RunProcessor.php` (`start()`, `translate()`, the status half of `finalize()`), `src/Runs/WorkReport.php`, `src/Events/RunStarted.php`, `PlanOptions::$parallel` (the web split is the editor's only, so runs make no extra calls; the editor's controller sets it). The planner's details are in the task's Built note. In short: the item lock is held for the whole step, and a busy item, a lost claim or a busy unit ends the call with `wait` 1 instead of claiming again. The plan id is saved as soon as the item is planned, and a plan that expired is made again. A cancel or abort seen between units releases the item with its plan. Transient failures end the call with the provider's wait, and the failure after 10 waits fails the item. Three such items in a row, counted in the cache and reset by a translated item or `start()`, abort the run. A rejected key aborts the run first and then releases the item. Other exceptions are reported and retried after 5 and 10 seconds, and fail at the third attempt. `finalize()` runs after an abort and when the last item ends in `translate()`. T8.5 done 2026-10-05: `RunProcessor::applyReady()` and the rest of `finalize()`, `src/Runs/ApplyReport.php`, `src/Events/RunFinished.php`, and `src/Translation/PlanApplier.php`, which holds `TargetTranslator`'s former apply step so that runs and `TargetTranslator` share it. `RunItem::$corrections`, `Run::$saves` and `Run::$committedSaves` are new. `ContentWriter::write()` takes an optional lock wait, and `claimTranslated()` now honours `available_at`. The planner's details are in the task's Built note. In short: each item is claimed and applied inside the global write lock, which the call waits for only until its deadline. A creator who lost access, or whose user no longer exists, is `skipped` with `unauthorized`. An expired plan, or the first change of the origin, sends the item back to `pending`; the second change fails it with `source_changed`. One deviation from the task: an item whose every field was edited meanwhile is `skipped` with `target_changed`, not `failed`, as `TargetTranslator` and the CLI report it, so its children are not skipped with `dependency_failed`. A review that accepts every field is `done` with reason `no_corrections`. Other exceptions are reported and retried after 5 and 10 seconds as `translated`, and fail at the third attempt. `finalize()` commits once when content was saved since its last call (also for a run that is still running), and fires `RunFinished` once per end (`finalizedAt`). `applyReady()` finalizes after the last item, and again when the run stopped running while it applied. `TargetTranslated` fires for every item that saved. Tests: `tests/Feature/Runs/RunApplyTest.php` (26: every case of the self-check, plus the write lock held elsewhere and bounded by the deadline, a passed deadline and a cancelled run, planner warnings kept, the second source change, an expired plan, `overwrite_manual`, every field edited, the creator's publish option, strict fields one and all, a review with and without corrections, a deleted source, a vetoed save that still commits, retries, a busy item, a stalled run committed without ending, and `RunFinished` again after a resume). Commits are counted through a mocked `Git::dispatchCommit()`, because `CommitJob` is unique and a faked queue drops a second one. `RunObjectsTest` is updated. 28 mutations each fail a test. Full suite 2,139 passed, Pint clean, CP build unchanged. In the sandbox with the fake engine, a browser run of `articles` into Greek translated and applied in one round: 11 items `done` as drafts with `[el]` text, 2 skipped, the run `completed` with 11 saves committed, and a fresh process found 13 Greek articles. The run was deleted and the sandbox reset. TASKS.md: §A1, §A12, T8.6, T8.7, T8.8, T8.10, T9.8.
T8.6 done 2026-10-05: `src/Runs/RunDispatcher.php` (static `resolveDriver()`, `connection()`, `connectionConfig()`; `start()`, `takeOver()`) and `src/Jobs/WorkRun.php` (lane job: `$tries` 1, `$timeout` from the connection's `retry_after`, `deadlineSeconds()`, `timeoutSeconds()`, `failed()` with up to 5 restarts in a row). The planner's details are in the task's Built note. In short: `auto` runs on the queue unless the connection's driver is `sync`, `deferred`, `background` or `null`, or the connection is missing, and reads a failover connection as its first one. `start()` calls `RunProcessor::start()` and gives only a running queue run a new generation and its lanes. `takeOver()` moves a queue run to the browser with a new generation. A lane works only while the run is running with the job's generation, and dispatches itself again with the smallest known wait (1 second when none is known, at once after a deadline or when the apply sent items back). On a queue that runs jobs at once it sleeps through the wait instead. A missing `retry_after` reads as 90. Defaults: lane deadline 25 seconds, job timeout 80. Tests: `tests/Feature/Runs/RunDispatcherTest.php` (28 cases, 44 tests: every case of the self-check, plus `deferred`, `background`, `null`, a missing connection, failover, the runs connection over the default one, an invalid setting, connection and queue name on the jobs, a second start, browser and CLI runs, a finished run, take-over, a cancelled or deleted run, a run deleted during work, the sleep on the sync connection, the provider's wait as the delay, the 1-second minimum, at once after the translate or apply deadline and after an item sent back, no successor for a finished run, restarts counted from zero after a normal end, no restart for a newer generation or a cancelled or deleted run, and failover timing); `RunFixtures::pending()` is new. 30 mutations each fail a test. Full suite 2,185 passed, Pint clean, CP build unchanged. In the sandbox (`sync` queue) the driver resolves to `browser`, with a 25-second deadline and an 80-second timeout. TASKS.md: §A12, T8.7, T8.10, T9.6, T13.1.

T8.10 done 2026-10-05: `tolk:translate` on the run engine and `src/Console/Commands/WorkCommand.php` (`tolk:work {run} {--lane=1}`, hidden). The command keeps its options and rules and adds `--all`, `--from=`, `--publish-changes`, `--concurrency=`, `--queue` and `--no-estimate`. It builds and stores a `cli` run (`queue` with `--queue`), scans it with `RunPlanner::estimate()` behind a "Scanning" bar, and prints the items per content type and site, the segments, the estimated tokens and the cost. A dry run, a declined question and a run with nothing to do leave no run behind. `--queue` calls `RunDispatcher::start()` and prints the run page's URL, and is refused on `sync`. Otherwise the command starts `tolk:work` processes through the site's `please` file and applies in its own process; without workers it translates one item before each apply. Ctrl-C cancels the run, waits for the workers, and a second Ctrl-C stops them. The report is built from the run's items with the T4.18 line format, plus the unfinished count with the run's URL, a table of failed targets, and the run's out-of-scope fields with the doctor hint. The planner's details are in the task's Built note. In short: new `RunItem::$created`; `translate()`'s optional `$maxItems`; `estimate()`'s optional plan callback, and its skips keep the plan's field reasons; `RunDispatcher::start()` hands a `cli` run to the CP's driver, so Resume works on the run page; `ReportsTranslations` gains `reportItem()`, `noteRunOutOfScope()` and the failed table, and loses `savedCount()` and `reportPlan()`. A page skipped for a missing root page now exits 0, and a cancelled run exits 1. T12.1 done 2026-10-08: `tests/Feature/ConcurrencyTest.php` (group `slow`, which the default run and CI's `vendor/bin/pest --parallel` include, so the workflow is unchanged): four PHP processes from `Process::pool()` run `tests/Support/concurrency-worker.php`, which boots the suite's application (`tests/Support/ConcurrencyWorker.php`, a `TestCase` subclass on its own copy of Testbench's skeleton inside the test's `temporaryPath()`) on the test's runs, state and `file` cache folders; in three phases started together by file barriers they make 200 `claimNext()` calls on a 200-item run, 200 state updates on 20 references and a `finalize()` of that run and ten done ones. Asserted: every item handed out once with its own token and `done`, no state field lost, `RunFinished` once per run, each run completed and finalized, no worker error. About 1.4 s. Removing each lock fails it 5 of 5 times (run repository: `ClaimLost` on an item two workers got; state: lost fields; run status: `RunFinished` twice); a first version with interleaved state updates and one run to finalize caught the last two only 2 and 1 of 5 times. Finding fixed: `FileStateRepository`'s lock waited Laravel's default 250 ms between attempts (workers queued ~290/570/830 ms behind one that took 30 ms; the editor's parallel unit requests record pending state for the same target), now 10 ms like the run, status and usage locks. Harness notes (`07-spike-findings.md` "Processes and locks (T12.1)"): Laravel's handler ends a script outside the console kernel with exit 0 after an uncaught exception, so the worker script catches and exits 1; Testbench's `tearDown()` needs PHPUnit's runner; `ensureDirectoryExists()` races on the last folder only, which the state store already retries. Sandbox (fake engine, `STATAMIC_STACHE_WATCHER=false`): a browser run started on its run page in headless Chromium and `tolk:translate --collection=articles --collection=pages … --concurrency=4 --yes` at the same time, once both into `el` and `de` (CLI 35 done, browser 1 done, the rest skipped `target_changed` or `nothing_to_do`) and once split (browser 17 into `el`, CLI 19 into `de`); each time a fresh process found every article and page localization exactly once on disk, by `Entry::query()`, by `Entry::find()` and by `in($site)`, and both page trees with every page under its origin parent's localization and Home's as the root. Sandbox reset, watcher back to `auto`. Also fixed: `TranslationEngineTest`'s server-error pause case failed once in a parallel run, because a second boundary fell between `pause()` and `pausedFor()` (likelier with four more processes); it now freezes time like its 429 sibling. Pest 2,975 passed (`--parallel` three times, and serially), Pint clean, CP build unchanged. TASKS.md: T12.1, T12.2 (the worker and the two-runs outcome), the risks row; `CLAUDE.md` names the concurrency test.
T12.2 done 2026-10-08: `tests/Feature/FailureModesTest.php` (15 tests, through the CLI translator, the editor's `plan`, `unit` and `confirm` requests and a browser run's `store`, `start`, `tick` and `apply` requests, with the real `TranslationEngine` on SDK agent fakes or on Anthropic's HTTP API faked): 300 Bard paragraphs go out in units of 80, 80, 80 and 61 segments, answered in reverse order and saved in order, and the editor delivers the field with its last unit whatever the order; an 11,199-character paragraph is a unit of its own in both profiles; a paragraph whose placeholders the model keeps dropping is sent three times, delivered in the source language with a warning, reads outdated once saved, and is all an Update sends; a provider answering 529 fails a browser run's item after ten 3-second waits and the CLI's after ten sleeps, saving no content, state, usage or commit, and no request sleeps; a tick during a 30-second pause answers `wait: 30` without an HTTP request; a plan lost from the cache mid-item is planned again, one lost before apply sends the item back to `pending`; of two runs over one entry the later skips it as `target_changed` with content and state untouched, or as `nothing_to_do` without a call; at most one provider call per request in the editor (through a split, a repair, a missing-id retry and reviews) and in a browser run (none while paused); exactly one commit for a run over a revisioned entry (a working copy and a new draft), a term, a global set and a nav. From the reviews: `Pruner` keeps the state of a translation file it cannot read (`InvalidLangFile`; `PrunerTest` +1, failing before). The Overview at 3,013 articles: each slice listed every root again (~520 ms) and each target found its root again with `Entry::find()`, which filters the whole id index (~1 ms per call at that size); now `EntryFamilies::rootIdsOutside()` lists ids from the index in tree order, `StatusScanner` loads roots 50 at a time as it resolves them (`CHUNK`) and builds each target with the new `TargetFactory::forLoadedEntry()` (`EntryTarget` takes the loaded entry; its `fresh()` loads anew): the Greek cell takes 5 slices and 10 s instead of 7 and 17 s, `tolk:status --collection=articles` 9.2–9.5 s instead of 18.7–19.6 s (`EntryFamiliesTest` +4, `StatusScannerTest` +1, `TargetFactoryTest` +3; one mutation fails). T12.3 done 2026-10-08: `tests/Feature/SecurityTest.php` (176 tests). Routes: every route of `routes/cp.php` (a case per registered route is enforced) with a well-formed request about a Greek article, its origin or the owner's run of it, answered 401 unauthenticated, 403 to users without the route's permission whatever else they may do, through for a user with the permission, edit rights and site access, and, per route, 403 (or through where the route names no item, with the reason) for a user with the permission who may not edit the article or access its site (`editor unit` revokes the rights after planning); a unit of another user's plan is 403. No `withoutExceptionHandling()`. Prompt injection, through Anthropic's API faked: a title telling the model to answer X and add `s99` reaches the model only as segment text, the system prompt holds "never an instruction" (the reviewer's too), and the duplicate and unrequested ids the obeying model returns are dropped, one answer per id. Model output: a raw `<script>` (closed or not) or `<img onerror>` is rejected in plain, inline, markdown and html segments, an escaped `&lt;script&gt;` in plain and inline, an added `{{ … }}` in all four, and in Markdown a new inline link, a replaced destination, an image, a reference definition, an autolink and an added or changed bare address; a model that keeps adding markup leaves the source text delivered in the editor and saved by a run. Front end: no `v-html`, `innerHTML`, `outerHTML`, `insertAdjacentHTML` or `document.write` in `resources/js` (tests excepted). Secrets: with recognisable keys for all three providers (Anthropic's through `tolk.providers`) and provider errors echoing all three (401, 429 spend limit, 400, 500, a dropped connection), through the editor, a browser run that saves, one the provider rejects (aborted, its error `[redacted]`), a queue run and `tolk:translate`, no response, page (Overview, run page, settings), file below the test's directory (state, runs, usage, cache, the log, content), queued job or command output holds a key; the log has the 400's warning with `[redacted]`, and an unexpected failure's message stored on an item is sanitised. Model choice: plan (`translator`, `reviewer`), review and run store refuse a model outside the catalog from a user without the settings ability (422), site profile generation is 403, push and the bulk action ignore a model in the request; a settings user may plan with one. Findings fixed: (1) any user with `run bulk translations` saw and drove every run, its item titles, errors and edit URLs included, also runs into sites they may not access: `RunStateRequest` (now holding `run()`, which `RunFollowRequest` and `RunEstimateRequest` inherit) authorizes only users who may access every site of the run (`Run::sites()`: its target sites and source site), the controller reads the run from the request, and the runs index and the Overview's latest runs list only those runs (`RunStateRequest::visible()`); (2) `runs.store` and `editor/push` accepted sites the user may not access (the planner skipped them, leaving an empty run its creator could no longer open): both are 403 now, through `Http\Requests\Concerns\ChecksSiteAccess`, which `OverviewScanRequest` shares; (3) from the T9.3–T9.7 review, `editor/confirm` and `editor/baseline` recorded state for a nav or a global set a user may edit, and every editor endpoint resolved any reference type before authorizing it (for a `lang::` reference a file-existence check on a path built from the request, 403 or 404): the editor accepts only entry and term references, checked before the target is resolved (others: 422 `not_translatable` from plan and review, the unknown-reference status from confirm and baseline); (4) §A3 rule 7 let a Markdown translation add or change a bare web or email address, which a field with `automatic_links` links: `Placeholders::validate()` refuses an address the translation holds more often than its source (`PlaceholdersTest` cases). Reviewed and left: provider messages are sanitised where classified (`ErrorClassifier`) and wherever they are stored or returned; an unexpected exception inside a run is still passed raw to `report()` (no path puts a key in one; its stored copy is sanitised); a user who may run bulk translations but not edit a run's items may still start or resume another user's run, which applies only what its creator may edit (§A14); the Overview and the audit list every container. Tests changed with the fixes: `EditorPushTest` (a site the user may not access is 403, not `invalid_sites`), `EditorRequestsTest` (its user may access the sites), `RunsControllerTest` (unauthorized targets come from a collection the user may not edit; a site or source site they may not access is 403). Pest 3,182 passed (parallel), Playwright editor-push, runs and editor-guards 13 passed, Pint clean, CP build unchanged. TASKS.md: §A3 rule 7, §A14, T13.1; `CLAUDE.md` names the security test.

T13.4 done 2026-10-08: acceptance run with the owner's Anthropic and Gemini keys (`TOLK_*` in the sandbox `.env`, passed to `composer test:live` through the environment); there was no OpenAI key, and the owner deferred the OpenAI pass (its probe, OpenAI as translator, its Test connection), so GPT-6.1 Sol's and GPT-6 Luna's options and headroom stay unverified. Probe (`07-spike-findings.md` "Live probe (T13.4)"): Claude Haiku 5.5 answers `thinking: between_tools` with a 400, so its catalog options are now `thinking: {type: disabled}` (Anthropic's thinking page: Haiku 5.5 accepts `disabled` at its default effort, Sonnet 5.5 only `between_tools`); every other option was accepted; no model spent thinking tokens, so Gemini 3.8 Flash's `reasoning_headroom` is 0 (was 4,000); `<1>` lost 2 and `<g1>` 1 of 160 placeholder segments, so §A3 keeps `<1>`; 6,000 characters into Greek took Sonnet 5.5 42.8 s against `http.timeout.web` 45, so `batch.max_chars.web` is 4,000 (was 6,000). Found and fixed: the output cap left no room for each segment's JSON, so 80 short strings stopped with `Length` at 2,048 on all four models (they need up to 2,618); `TranslationEngine` adds 32 tokens per segment (`TranslationEngineTest` +1, the cap datasets of both engine tests updated). Estimates were about half the real cost: output is now characters × 1.5 ÷ `tokens.chars_per_token` + 20 tokens per segment and review output characters × 1.0 ÷ it, and two whole-site runs now estimate $0.23 and $0.28 for $0.21 and $0.28 spent ($0.13 and $0.17 before; `EstimatorTest` updated). The owner read Greek and German output (Sonnet 5.5; Gemini 3.8 Flash reviewed by Sonnet 5.5) and accepted it; at the owner's choice the translation prompt gained rule 9 (one form of address, as the language instructions say or else the one usual for this kind of website), because Sonnet mixed "man" and "Sie" in three German articles with formality default (prompt snapshots, `docs/context-and-glossary.md`). Acceptance criteria, all 2026-10-08 in the sandbox: (1) ✓ the offer filled six fields of a new Greek article localization, Bard and Replicator included, stored nothing before Save and the origin's structure after (Sonnet 5.5); (2) ✓ a field action translated only the German title (Sonnet 5.5); (3) ✓ the listing action's run of four articles into Greek and German showed its estimate and progress, was cancelled after one item and resumed to 8 done (Sonnet 5.5); (4) ✓ `tolk:site de` after its preflight and estimate: 26 targets, entries with translated titles and kept slugs, five terms, the global set and the navigation, in 38 s (Sonnet 5.5), and `tolk:translate --all --site=el` (Gemini 3.8 Flash reviewed by Sonnet 5.5); (5) ✓ an origin edit read outdated, Update sent the one changed paragraph and kept a hand-edited excerpt, `--force` replaced it, and opening and saving in the CP changed no status (Sonnet 5.5); (6) ✓ site profile, instructions, German informal formality and a glossary row were in the captured prompt and turned the German from Sie, Schlucht and a dash into du, Klamm and none (Sonnet 5.5); (7) ✓ Sonnet 5.5's review of Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite's Greek applied three corrections listed with reasons, and Review again with Gemini 3.8 Flash three more; (8) ✓ both keys come from `.env` and pass Test connection (Haiku 5.5 3.2 s, Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite 1.4 s); OpenAI deferred by the owner; (9) ✓ `tolk:lang` and `tolk:forms` into German (Sonnet 5.5) and Greek (Gemini 3.8 Flash) kept every `:parameter` and plural variant, a second run sent nothing and a hand edit was kept. A real queue worker ✓ (a database queue on a temporary SQLite file: the listing's run of 13 articles into Greek on the worker, Sonnet 5.5, while a four-worker CLI run re-translated the German site, Gemini 3.8 Flash, and a `--queue` run of the pages); parallel runs with the Stache watcher off ✓ (every page and article once per site, queries matching the files before and after `stache:clear`); golden structure and one provider call per web request ✓ (the suite); no key in any file, log, cache, queue payload or report ✓.
