T11.6 done 2026-10-09: at the owner's request, Tolk's model catalog moved from `config/tolk.php` to `resources/models.php`, and `config/tolk.php` ships `models.{anthropic,openai,gemini}` as `[]`. `ModelCatalog::forProvider()` merges a site's entries into Tolk's by id (an entry replaces the top-level keys it names and keeps its place; another id follows Tolk's models; an entry without an id is dropped), so a published config no longer freezes the catalog and Tolk's first model of a tier stays the default. The tests that replaced a provider's catalog or edited it by index now change Tolk's entries by id; `RequestShapeTest` read the config's list, which is empty now, and passed with no request, so it reads the catalog instead. `docs/configuration.md` (the catalog section, with an example and the note for configs published from 1.0.0-beta.1), `docs/troubleshooting.md`, the Boost guideline and `CHANGELOG.md` updated. Checked in the sandbox through tinker: a price changed for Sonnet keeps its other keys, an added Opus follows Haiku, and Sonnet stays the default. 3,345 tests pass, Pint is clean, and the CP build is unchanged.
T11.1 done 2026-10-09: built at the owner's request, off by default, with the owner's two choices: a run of the entry instead of a job translating each localization with `TargetTranslator` (that job could outlast a queue connection's `retry_after` and be handed out twice, and its updates would show nowhere), and `auto_update_create`, which also creates the missing localizations. `QueueTranslationUpdates` on the initial `EntrySaved` of a published origin dispatches `UpdateTranslations` (10 seconds on the queue with a worker, unique until processing; otherwise after the response), which under a lock per entry and once no earlier run from the entry is moving makes the run with `AutoUpdater` (an update as the saver, estimated first and not kept when nothing is pending) and starts it with queue lanes or drives it in its own process as `cli`. Runs carry `scope.automatic`; usage counts under the new source `auto`; `EntryFamilies::originSites()` is now the one rule for the sites an entry is the origin of. Banner: the origin says when saving updates it and, after a save, looks for the run and follows it; the localization says when an automatic update last saved it and whether that waits in a working copy. Runs list and page, `tolk:doctor` and the Audit page name automatic updates. Docs (`configuration.md` Automation, `editor.md` and `bulk-and-runs.md` "Automatic updates", `troubleshooting.md`, `extending.md`, README, CHANGELOG) and the Boost guideline. `tests/Feature/AutoUpdateTest.php` (18 tests; dropping the `isInitial()` check fails the chain case), Pest 3,363 passed (parallel), Vitest 622, Pint clean, `resources/dist/build` rebuilt. Sandbox, through artisan with the fake engine and `auto_update` forced on in that process only (the owner's `.env` has the fake engine off, and the sandbox holds the owner's uncommitted content and untracked settings, which `reset()` would remove): a throwaway article translated into Greek, its excerpt edited and saved; the Greek excerpt was unchanged until the process ended and then updated, title kept, by one completed `cli` run with one save, in about a second; the entry, its state, the run and the three zero-token usage entries it recorded were removed again. The browser check followed (the owner asked for it): with the sandbox's content, settings and runs backed up, the fake engine on for its duration, and everything restored and compared by checksum afterwards. It found that a run finished before the banner's first look left the save without a word, and that a missing site the settings do not create kept the banner looking for 30 seconds: the origin block now lists `auto_update_sites`, and the banner shows the phases `expecting` and `updated`. Through PHP-FPM, the save's `editor/confirm` already named the running run, so the banner went from nothing to running to the completed result; the Greek banner read "Updated automatically from English on Oct 9, 2026, 9:50 PM." in light and dark mode. `tests/Browser/editor-auto-update.spec.js` (3 scenarios); Playwright 69 passed, 4 skipped (the screenshots).
T11.1 review fixes 2026-10-09 (three findings on PR #1, each reproduced by a failing test first): a chain saved by a queue worker dispatched its update with no user, so it ran unrestricted; `RunProcessor` now applies items inside `RunSaves::as()` with the run creator, and the listener takes the user from `RunSaves::user()`. The run's scope listed sites the saver may not access, which the planner skipped but `RunStateRequest::allows()` still checked, so the saver got 403 following or cancelling it; `AutoUpdater::sites()` (and the banner's `auto_update_sites`) keep only the sites the user may access. `busy()` searched the 50 most recent runs, so an import making many runs hid an earlier run of the entry that was still moving; it searches all runs now. `AutoUpdateTest.php` 23 tests.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): the estimate marked every item with an empty plan `skipped`, also one that waited for other items of the run, so a run never translated it: a German localization translated from the Greek one the run updated, or a navigation whose branches all link entries the run creates (dropped while those had no localization). `RunPlanner::estimate()` now skips an empty plan only when `RunRepository::isReady()` (new; the file driver's claim rule) says nothing holds the item back; otherwise it stays pending and is planned again when claimed. `tolk:translate --dry-run` says "nothing to do yet" for it. `RunPlannerTest` +2, `TranslateCommandTest` +1; `docs/bulk-and-runs.md` and `docs/cli.md` updated.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): `applyFields()` recorded as delivered whatever the form held 300 ms after a fill, so a correction typed in that window counted as the untouched fill and Undo put the snapshot back over it. The read-back is recorded only while its text is the filled text (a fieldtype's re-emit changes structure, not text); otherwise the filled value is recorded, so Undo keeps the editor's text and `skipHandles()` treats it as an edit. `fill.test.js` +2.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): `registry.js` `textOf()` joined every string of a value, so text moved to another field of a set, another row or cell, or another Bard paragraph (pressing Enter mid-sentence) read the same, and `applyFields()` overwrote the edit; `dirtyHandles()`, `skipHandles()` and Undo missed it too. `textsOf()` keeps the strings by key and position (without empty items at a list's end) and reads a Bard value as its runs of text between node boundaries plus each node's attributes, so Bard's re-emit (joined runs, a wrapped block image, `textAlign: null`, link attributes) still reads the same; `sameText()` compares their JSON. `registry.test.js` +2 (and the `textOf` cases rewritten), `fill.test.js` +1. Not checked in the browser: the sandbox holds the owner's uncommitted work.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): `applyFields()` took a `sync` field out of `localizedFields` 300 ms after the fill, and `restore()` put back the snapshot's flag 300 ms after Undo, whatever the field held then, so a correction typed in that window stayed visible while Save left it out and the form went back to inheriting the origin. Both reset the flag only while the field still holds the filled or restored text (`sameText()`); otherwise core's desync from the editor's update stands. `fill.test.js` +2.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): the `entry.saved` hook took the clean values again 600 ms after a save, whatever the form held then, so a correction typed right after Save read as stored: `skipHandles()` no longer sent it and a translation overwrote it. The hook now hands the response's values to `refreshClean(name, saved)`; `dirtyHandles()` counts a handle clean when its text is the clean values' or the saved values', which covers core putting the response's values into the form after the hooks (with revisions), and the later refresh is gone. The banner's 600 ms refresh after a switch stays: it takes in the fieldtypes' re-emit after a form loads, and there is no response to tell that from typing. `saveHooks.test.js` (the refresh case rewritten, +1), `registry.test.js` +1.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): an origin another process saved while the model translated passed the source check, because a localization's target reads its source through `origin()`, which this process keeps in Blink, and `fresh()` does not drop that. `editor/unit` answered 200 with the old source's translation instead of 409 `source_changed`, and `TargetTranslator` (and so a run) saved it. `PlanApplier::apply()` now assembles inside `ContentWriter::write()`, after its Stache refresh and on the target resolved there, and `EditorTranslation::unit()` calls `ContentWriter::refreshStache()` before it delivers. `CreatesContent::saveEntryElsewhere()` changes an entry's file and cached Stache item without this process's save side effects; `EditorEndpointsTest` +1, `TargetTranslatorTest` +1.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): `CreatesContent::saveEntryElsewhere()` wrote the entry's file and a Stache item key it built by hand in this process, against rule 6, so it left the indexes stale for good (a title it changed was still not found after `ContentWriter::refreshStache()`), saved no localizations, ran no listeners and could not delete. It now runs `Entry::save()`, with events, in a real PHP process (`tests/Support/OtherProcess.php` through `other-process.php`: a `TestCase` on the test's own directory and `file` cache, with the test's sites and the addon's default settings, jobs named in a `match`), and the new `deleteEntryElsewhere()` deletes the same way. Both need `useFileCache()` (a `LogicException` otherwise); a failure (one the other process reports, or input it never gets, a failed start or the 30 s timeout) is thrown again at tearDown, so one the code under test swallows still fails the test, and `TestCase::tearDown()` now deletes the test's folder in a `finally`. The two ed9d635 regression tests pass, and fail with `PlanApplier.php` and `EditorTranslation.php` from before ed9d635; `EditorEndpointsTest`'s 409 test calls the helper once. About 0.2 s per call. `Contracts/OtherProcessTest` +5; `07-spike-findings.md` "Processes and locks", `CLAUDE.md` and T12.2 name the helpers.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): since ed9d635, `PlanApplier::apply()` assembled inside `ContentWriter::write()`, so a review without corrections and a strict plan whose every field failed, which save nothing, waited for `tolk:write` and failed with `writer_busy` while another process held it, assembly (a cache read per field and unit) held the lock, and the source check depended on each caller remembering a refresh. `Target::apply()` now checks every field's scope and source fingerprint on the target it resolves inside the lock, before `StateWriter::confirm()` and before anything is written (`Translation\SourceCheck`, the predicate `Pipeline::assemble()` uses too; `keyByHandle()` only keys), so a source another process saved is caught under the lock whatever the caller's target still holds in Blink; `PlanApplier` assembles from the caller's target again and takes the lock only to save, with no `fresh()` of its own. Correction to the ed9d635 line: runs were never affected, because they resolve and assemble inside their own lock. `ReviewPipelineTest` +1 and `TargetTranslatorTest` +1 (`writer_busy` before), `EntryApplyTest`, `TermTargetTest`, `GlobalsTargetTest` and `LangTargetTest` +1 each (the old source's translation was saved), and `FormsTargetTest` +1, a guard that passed before (a form text is its own handle, so the old scope check caught it); both ed9d635 tests pass, and the `TargetTranslatorTest` one fails without the new check. `Target::fresh()` and `07-spike-findings.md` "Stache and parallel writers" name what core keeps in Blink.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): `editor/unit` checked the target only before the unit, so when another request deleted the localization, its origin or the term, or detached the localization, while the model worked, it answered 500 instead of 410 `target_missing`: since ed9d635 the delivery's Stache refresh drops the stale `entry-descendants-` key, so `EditorDelivery` threw its `LogicException` for a localization that was gone and the target threw `TargetNotFound` for a deleted origin or term (before ed9d635 a deleted or detached localization answered 200 and got pending entries), and a localization deleted and created again answered 200 with pending entries on the new one. `EditorTranslation::target()` builds the plan's target again from its description, reads its source and requires it to exist with the plan's reference, else `TargetNotFound` (the new type-neutral `TargetNotFound::reference()`, also for an `InvalidTarget` or `UnknownSite`); `EditorController@unit` uses it before the unit, `unit()` (no longer given a target) after it ran, and the controller maps a `TargetNotFound` from either to 410 through `targetMissing()`. A unit that completes no field delivers nothing and no longer refreshes the Stache. `CreatesContent::deleteEntryElsewhere()` takes `'delete'` or `'detach'` for an origin's localizations, as the CP's Delete action does, and `detachLocalizationsElsewhere()`, `localizeEntryElsewhere()` and `deleteTermElsewhere()` are new `OtherProcess` jobs. `EditorEndpointsTest` +7 (a localization deleted and created again after planning, 200 before; while the unit ran, the localization deleted, detached, or deleted and created again, and the origin deleted with its localizations or after detaching them, 500 or 200 before; a unit completing no field keeps a Blink sentinel), `EditorTermsTest` +1 (a term deleted while the unit ran, 500 before), `Contracts/OtherProcessTest` +6 (one case per new job or variant, the origin's deletion over both), `StringsTest` +1 message. Five mutations (no reference check, no guard for an empty unit, no refresh, no catch around the unit, no second resolution) each fail a new test. TASKS.md §A11, T5.4, T5.8 and T12.2, `CLAUDE.md` and `07-spike-findings.md` ("Stache and parallel writers", "Processes and locks") updated.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): core's request step puts the save response's values into the form before the `entry.saved` and `term.saved` hooks (SavePipeline's `Request`; with revisions the entry form sets them again after the hooks), so the `saved` values the hook kept since this review's earlier save-hook fix were the clean values, and the two tests that told them apart ran in an order core never uses (deleted); `registry.js` takes `refreshClean(name)` and the one condition in `dirtyHandles()` again, and `saveHooks.js` drops `savedValues()`. CodeMirror-based fieldtypes pass a value stored with `\r\n` up with `\n`, which no refresh fixes: an untranslated, inherited Markdown `content` whose origin's `.md` body holds CRLF was re-emitted at an in-place switch and `skipHandles()` sent it as `edited_in_form` through its origin branch, a CRLF fill recorded the filled value so Undo reported it kept, and an inherited CRLF field stayed localized after Undo. `textsOf()` reads `\r\n` and `\r` as `\n` in strings and in Bard's runs of text (no CP input lets a person type a carriage return; the server's `ofLeaves()` and `ofText()` still keep line breaks). Decided: no refresh after a save and no page-wide input signal; a fieldtype that re-emits the saved values with other text reads as edited until the next switch, a field that keeps inheriting again after each save (skipped, never overwritten), pinned by a test and recorded in T5.8's "Built in T5.5". `saveHooks.test.js` (a `respond()` helper in core's order, the correction case rewritten with it, +2), `registry.test.js` +2, `translate.test.js` +1, `fill.test.js` +2; `07-spike-findings.md` "Save order and re-emits". Not checked in the browser: the sandbox holds the owner's uncommitted work.
Review fix 2026-10-10 (reproduced by failing tests first): `applyFields()` took a `sync` field out of `localizedFields` after the read-back, and `restore()` put back an inherited field's flag after Undo, while the field held the filled or restored text, so a toggle or heading level the editor changed in the 300 ms window (a toggle in a synced Group or in a Grid row padded to `min_rows`, a toggle in a set or a heading level right after Undo) stayed visible while Save left the field out and it inherited again. After Undo the same step also cleared `Statamic.$dirty`, because `dirtyHandles()` compares text, so the form read clean with the change unsaved: switching site or leaving the page did not ask, and autosave skipped it. `registry.js` `sameContent()` is `sameText()` plus the booleans and numbers both values hold in one place (types, ids and marks skipped, a place only one value has ignored, so Bard's re-emit still reads the same); both resets use it, Undo keeps the form dirty while a restored field's content differs from the snapshot's, and the read-back record stays on `sameText()`. Core's Integer, Float and Range fieldtypes pass a typed number up as a string, which `sameText()` already saw, so the numbers `sameContent()` adds are Bard's attributes and those of fieldtypes that emit numbers. Decided: what the field holds decides, with no input signal (an upload finishing, a Replicator set added after its fetch or a fieldtype with a longer debounce changes a field without input, and a reset on no input would drop it); a mark, a node type, or a row or set without text changed inside the 300 ms is still left out, and a change without text in a field Undo does not restore still lets Undo clear the dirty flag (T5.7 Built). A sync field kept by mistake stores an empty override, which reads manual with the source changed, not untranslated (`isCopy()` excludes the empty fingerprint), so the comparison stays this narrow. `fill.test.js` +9 (5 of them guards that passed before: a synced Bard field re-emitted while the editor types elsewhere still inherits, a number typed into a padded Grid row arrives as a string and stays localized, a Bard list re-emitted with its default attributes after Undo inherits again, and an upload landing in either window without input keeps the field localized; the toggle and heading level cases after Undo also hold the dirty flag), `registry.test.js` +2; `07-spike-findings.md` "The localized flag after a sync fill or Undo". Not checked in the browser: the sandbox was not touched.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `NavTarget::mapBranches()` left out an entry branch without a localization in the target site also when it was the root page of a navigation set to **Expect a root page**, so the next branch moved to index 0; when it had children, the saved tree failed core's `validateTree()` ("Root page cannot have children") on every read after the save, so the Greek pages rendering the nav, the CP navigation page, the Overview and `tolk:status` threw until the YAML was fixed by hand, and without children the next item silently became the root. The root branch is now kept as a text-only root titled with its entry's title (translated with the tree, its own warning, a link again after Update once the entry is localized), `withoutLink()` no longer reads an unguarded `children`, and `applyFresh()` runs `validateTree()` before it records pending state or saves, throwing `ApplyFailed::invalidTree()` ("The tree of [nav::main::el] was not saved: …"). Statamic accepts a text-only root: the nav tag and the CP's tree endpoint read it. `NavTargetTest` +2 (the root case, read after `Stache::clear()` through the nav tag and `navigation.tree.index`, then Update; a tree core rejects is not saved and leaves no pending state; both failed with "Root page cannot have children" before), `StringsTest` +1 message; docs/content-types.md "Navigation", §A11 "Navigation", `07-spike-findings.md` "Navigation (T9.4)".
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `FormsTarget` left out only the texts that are keys of `lang/{source}.json`, so a form title, label, placeholder or email subject written as a key of a PHP translation file (`forms.contact.name`, translated in `lang/*/forms.php`) or of a package (`acme::forms.name`) was translated and written to `lang/{lang}.json`, where Laravel's translator finds it before the PHP file: the German form showed `[T] forms.contact.name` instead of `Ihr Name`, and every German notification email had that subject, while the command reported done. Such a text is now no field: `group.key` whose group is a PHP file of the source language with a line under `key` (read through `LangFiles`, as `Arr::get()` reads it) is left to `tolk:lang`, and `namespace::key` whose namespace the translator's loader knows is left to the package; `leftToLang()` names the texts by file and `packageKeys()` the package keys, which the command prints, and `hidingKeys()` lists the entries an earlier run wrote for a text that became such a key, which the command shows (in a dry run too) for the owner to delete instead of reporting them as unused. `tolk:forms` reads the PHP files a form text names before it sends anything, so one that cannot be read stops it with exit code 1. `FormsTargetTest` +2 (the PHP and package keys left out, a key the file lacks still translated, a source language without the file; entries written before the key existed named as hiding, not unused), `FormsCommandTest` +3 (Laravel's translator returns the German line of `lang/de/forms.php` after a run, which returned `[T] forms.contact.name` before; the hiding entries listed after the PHP file appears, in a dry run too; an unreadable `lang/en/forms.php` exits 1 before any call), `FormFixtures::keyed()` and `keys()`; docs/cli.md "`tolk:forms`", §A16, the Boost guideline.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `tolk:lang` and `tolk:forms` commit once with Statamic's git integration, but Statamic stages only `statamic.git.paths`, whose defaults hold `content/` and so the state under `content/tolk/lang/` and `content/tolk/forms/`, but not `lang/`, so with automatic git in production the commit held `content/tolk/lang/_json/de.yaml` without `lang/de.json` (probed with a real repository), and a deploy that replaces the site's files lost the translations while the state still recorded them, with no warning anywhere. Tolk does not change the host's git config, and a queued `CommitJob` reads the worker's config anyway, so the fix warns instead: `GitListeners::tracks()` (moved out of `Audit`, now also making the checked path absolute) tells whether a path or a folder above it is in `statamic.git.paths`; the audit's `state` check adds a warning (or an OK) about `lang_path()` while git is on and a `lang/` or `forms/` state folder exists, so `tolk:doctor` and the Audit page show it; and both commands warn after they write while git is on and `lang/` is not tracked. Documented in docs/installation.md "Where Tolk keeps things", docs/cli.md (`tolk:doctor`, `tolk:lang`, `tolk:forms`), the Boost guideline and §A16 "Git". Tests: `AuditTest` "checks that Statamic's git integration tracks the translation files once tolk:lang or tolk:forms has written them" (both commands) and `LangCommandTest` / `FormsCommandTest` "warns when Statamic's git integration does not track the folder of the files it wrote" failed before the fix.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): a navigation's whole tree is one field and only its text decides manual (D27), so pages an editor added, removed or moved in a translated tree without a text change left it current, and once it read outdated (a source change, or an entry localized) Update rebuilt it from the source tree, putting the removed pages back in the source's order, while the navigation docs did not say so and statuses.md's first remedy, Protect, exists only in the entry and term forms. `tolk:baseline` now takes `--protect` (`StateWriter::baseline(includeMachine: true)`, the editor's Protect for every target of the containers named), which refuses to run without `--collection`, `--taxonomy`, `--globals` or `--nav`; docs/content-types.md "Navigation", docs/statuses.md (Update rebuilds, Protecting translations), docs/cli.md `tolk:baseline`, docs/troubleshooting.md, the Boost guideline, §A11 "Navigation" and T9.8. `NavTargetTest` +2 (a tree with a page removed and one moved reads current and Update rebuilds it as the source's, which passed and confirmed the behaviour; protected with `--protect` it reads manual, a source change needs attention and Update sends nothing and keeps it, which failed because the option did not exist), `MaintenanceCommandsTest` +2 (current and outdated machine fields turn manual in the named collection only, one commit; no container option exits 1 and changes nothing; both failed the same way).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): Start ends the run page's estimate loop, but `describeEstimate()` read any estimate whose `next` was not null as in progress, so after an early Start the card said "Estimating… 3 of 10 items" for the rest of the run, completed included; an unfinished estimate now gives both a `progress` line and a `partial` one, "Only 3 of 10 items were estimated before the run started.", and the card's template picks by the run's status (`Estimating…` with the progress while it is `pending`, else the partial line, `data-tolk-estimate-partial`) with the numbers so far; the run page's docs say so. The choice sits in the template rather than in a status argument from `Show.vue`'s script, which no test runs (a review found that dropping that argument left the suite green). Tests: `describe.test.js` (an unfinished estimate has both lines, a finished one neither) and `Show.test.js` (the card described from the estimate alone, as the page does, for a pending run, for each status after Start, which showed "Estimating…" before, and for a finished estimate); dropping the status check in the template, or narrowing it to `completed`, fails them.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `RunState::for()` answered the page it was asked for without clamping it, and the run page shows its pager only when `last_page` is above 1, so on page 3 of the "Waiting" filter, once 50 or fewer items waited, the table said "No items." with no pager to go back; the page is now clamped to `last_page`, and the run page's `onState` takes the answer's page through the new `itemsPage()` when the server moved it back to the last, so the table stays on that page as the list grows again, while an answer to an earlier request for another page does not move it. Tests: `RunsControllerTest` (a pending filter on page 3 of 3 after two items failed answers page 1 with the waiting item, a page past the last of all items answers the last, an empty filter answers page 1; failing before) and `describe.test.js` (`itemsPage()`: kept, moved to the last, not moved by an earlier answer).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): with `strict_fields`, a field that failed holds its first part's reason (`invalid`, `missing`, `too_long`, `refused`, `request_rejected`), which the run page's `describeItem()` did not know, so it listed the field under "Not changed" with the raw code ("Not changed: body (invalid)"), and the PHP warnings named the codes too (`was left untranslated (invalid)`, `FieldFailed`'s `s2 (refused)`, and the untranslated `review skipped (unavailable)`); the new `UnitOutcome::reasonLabel()` words each reason through `__()` ("the model kept breaking its formatting", "the model left it out", "the model's answer was cut off", "the model refused it", "the provider rejected the request", "the provider was unavailable") for those three messages, the skipped review's now through `__()` as a whole, and `describeItem()` gives such fields as `failed` with the same English words, which the run page lists apart as "Could not be translated: …" (`data-tolk-item-failed`). `fields_failed` stays out of the item reasons, because the item's error already says it. Tests: `describe.test.js` and `Show.test.js` (the failed fields apart, in words, through `__()`; listed under "Not changed" with the codes before), `StringsTest` (`FieldFailed`'s reason through `__()`; `describe.js` holds `reasonLabel()`'s words for each reason a field can hold), and the warning texts in `PipelineTest`, `FailureModesTest`, `TargetTranslatorTest`, `EditorEndpointsTest`, `ReviewInPipelineTest`, `ReviewPipelineTest` and `EditorReviewTest`; the run page's and the statuses page's docs say so.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): the Overview's Try again queued a failed cell with `fresh` false, and a fresh scan left the cell's finished cache entry in place, so Try again after a Count again that failed answered the counts from before it as finished (`cached`, missing 1 where a fresh count gives 0); `overview/cells.js` now keeps the `fresh` of the last load and `retry()` queues the cell with it, and `OverviewScanner::scan()` forgets the finished entry (`OverviewCache::forget()`) when a scan starts without a cursor, so the replaced counts are not answered after a Count again stopped midway, by any request. Tests: `cells.test.js` (a retry after a failed fresh load is fresh, after a cached load not; sent `fresh` false before) and `OverviewTest` (after a fresh count's first slice, a request without `fresh` counts again and finds the quiet change; answered the old counts from the cache before); the Overview's docs say so.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `LangFiles::write()` kept a key only the target PHP file had and nested it after the source's keys, so where the source nests its keys differently (a target `password` string next to the source's `password.letters` and `password.mixed`, as a `validation.php` from an older Laravel has, or a target `title.short` under the source's `title` string) `place()` replaced the translations just set, `tolk:lang` said done and every run translated them again. `nest()` now places the source's and the set keys first and then each key only the file had where nothing is (`taken()`), dropping the others; `write()` returns the dropped keys, which `WritesLangFile` names in the result's warning ("Removed password: lang/en/validation.php nests its keys differently there."), and refuses (`InvalidLangFile::unholdable()`, before anything is written) an array to export that does not hold every set key with its value. `LangFilesTest` +2 (both directions and a carried value, the file unchanged before; keys one file cannot hold, written before), `LangCommandTest` +1 (the translations written, the warning, a second run without calls). Disabling the drop makes the guard fail both R13 tests instead of reporting done. TASKS.md §A16 and T9.10, `docs/cli.md`.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `LangFiles::write()` called `opcache_invalidate()` after replacing the file, which warns where `opcache.restrict_api` leaves the running script out, with OPcache on or off, and Laravel throws the warning, so on such a host `tolk:lang` and `tolk:forms` reported every file they wrote as not written, committed no state and fired no `TargetTranslated`, although the file held the translations; the call is now silenced (`@opcache_invalidate()`), and a `LangFilesTest` test writes a PHP file in a child process started with `-d opcache.restrict_api=…` and Laravel's error handling (`tests/Support/lang-write.php`), which failed with that `ErrorException` before.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): nothing rejected a `|` in the translation of one form of a pluralised lang string, so a model answer such as "Ein Apfel | ein Stück" for "One apple" was written as `Ein Apfel | ein Stück|[T] Many apples`, `trans_choice()` returned the wrong form for every count after it, the string read current and an update sent nothing; the handler's leaves now carry `Leaf::$pluralForm`, `Planner::segment()` stores it as the segment's `plural_form`, and `SegmentValidator::structure()` (the placeholder rules plus `pluralForm()`, which the pipeline now checks instead of `placeholders()` alone) treats such a `|` as invalid, so the form gets a repair with a note and after two stays in the source language, and a reviewer's correction holding one is discarded. `LangCommandTest` +2 (the repair, with the note, the file Laravel reads and an update without calls; a form that keeps its `|` left in English and a correction with one discarded), `LangStringHandlerTest` +1, `SegmentValidatorTest` +1 (a `|` outside a plural form stays valid), `PlannerTest`'s plan shape. TASKS.md §A8, §A10, §A16 and T9.10, `docs/cli.md`.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `LangStringHandler` cut every string at its `|` and told the model each part was "Form n of m of a pluralised string", and `tolk:lang`'s note for a language with more plural forms (Russian, Polish, Czech, Arabic) asked to add the missing forms by hand to every such string, so a string such as `Blog | :site` was presented as pluralised and an editor could add a third "form" to a title separator; form texts, which core looks up with `trans()` and never as plural forms, were cut the same way. Cutting at every `|` stays (a file does not say which strings `trans_choice()` reads), but the hint and the repair note now speak of parts of a string divided by `|` ("Part n of m of a string divided by |, used for the count {…}. Translate only this part, without a | character."), the note says that Laravel reads every `|` as dividing plural forms and to leave a string whose `|` is text as it is, and `FormsTarget`'s fields set `tolk_plural: false` (`LangStringHandler::PLURAL`), so a form text is one leaf whatever its `|`. `LangStringHandlerTest` +2 (`Blog | :site` without "pluralised", a field with `tolk_plural: false` whole) and the hint of the pluralised one, `LangCommandTest` +1 (the hints of `Blog | :site` and a plural string and the new note listing both for Russian) and the note and repair note of two, `FormsCommandTest` +1 (`Rating | 1 to 5` sent and written whole, cut into two hinted parts before), `SegmentValidatorTest`'s note. The two targets then fingerprinted a text with `|` differently, so a record that moved between `lang/en.json` and the forms kept the other target's fingerprint, and its machine translation read as manual ("manual edits kept") and was never updated without `--force`; `StateWriter::adopt()` now takes the other target's field (`sharedFileField()`), and `FieldState::rewalked()` replaces that target's fingerprint of the value the file holds with the adopting target's own, in the record's target hash, history and pending entries. `FormsCommandTest` +1 and `LangCommandTest` +1 (`Rating | 1 to 5` moved each way reads current without "manual edits kept", and the forms read it manual after an edit by hand), `StateObjectsTest` +1. TASKS.md §A16, T9.10 and T13.1, `docs/cli.md`.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): Google answers a wrong or revoked Gemini key with HTTP 400 `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, reason `API_KEY_INVALID`, in a body the Interactions endpoint wraps in a JSON list, and `ErrorClassifier` took every 4xx other than 401 to 404 for `request_rejected`, so each segment failed alone, D28 delivered every field in the source language, and a run with Gemini as translator wrote English over the published Greek translations and ended Completed (with Gemini as reviewer it saved the translations with "review skipped (request_rejected)"), while Test connection said "refused the request (HTTP 400)" with the raw body. `ErrorClassifier::body()` unwraps a list-shaped body for every reading (the message and the codes); for Gemini, a 4xx whose `error.details[].reason` starts with `API_KEY_` is `Rejected` with the new code `ProviderError::INVALID_KEY` (`invalid_key`), and one whose `error.status` is `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (billing, or a region the free tier does not serve) or whose `error.code` is `failed_precondition` (the shape Google's API errors page documents for the Interactions API, plain or list-wrapped; compared without regard to case) is `Rejected` with the code `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, so runs abort and save nothing; Test connection says "refused the API key" for `invalid_key`. `ProviderFixtures::geminiError()` and `geminiInvalidKey()` build Google's shape, and the Gemini fixtures that assumed a 401 (`ReviewEngineTest`, the Gemini row of the engine's key-sanitising test) use it. Tests: `FailureModesTest` "a wrong or revoked key" (a browser run with Gemini as translator or as reviewer is Aborted with the key message after one call, saves nothing and keeps `[el] Hello`; before, Completed with the title "Hello"), `ErrorClassifierTest` (+10; the two rows of the Interactions shape were `request_rejected` before), `TranslationEngineTest` (+4), `ProvidersTest` (+2), `ExceptionMappingTest` (+1, the SDK leaves the list body as sent). §A10's table, `07-spike-findings.md` "Exceptions" and `docs/troubleshooting.md` updated. Not done (owner decision, D28): leaving out a field none of whose segments was translated because of `request_rejected`.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): the classifier rejected a Gemini 429 only when its body carried `quota_exceeded`, a guessed shape, while the quota 429 bodies developers posted are Google's usual error (`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, "You exceeded your current quota …", a `QuotaFailure` naming the quota, a `Help` link and a `RetryInfo` delay, no `retry-after` header), so a used-up daily quota, which resets at midnight Pacific time, was paused as a rate limit with `RetryPolicy`'s backoff (1 s at first, at most 20 s) until its item failed after ten waits and the run aborted after three such items with "still unavailable", and a per-minute quota was retried after that backoff instead of the delay Google gave. `ErrorClassifier` now rejects a Gemini 429 with code `quota_exceeded` also when a `QuotaFailure` violation's `quotaId` contains `PerDay` (`GEMINI_DAILY_QUOTA`) or its `quotaValue` is `0`, keeps the code Google documents for the Interactions API (`{"error": {"code": "quota_exceeded"}}`, also list-wrapped since the previous fix), and pauses any other for the `RetryInfo` `retryDelay` when the provider sends no `retry-after`. Shapes taken from Google's API errors page, `google.rpc.error_details.proto` and bodies posted on Google's developer forum; no live call. `ProviderFixtures::geminiQuota()`, `geminiRateLimit()` and `geminiInteractionsError()` build them, replacing the guessed Gemini rows of `ErrorClassifierTest`, `TranslationEngineTest` and `ExceptionMappingTest`. Tests: `FailureModesTest` "a used-up Gemini quota" (a run on a used-up daily quota is Aborted with Google's message after one call, without a pause, and saves nothing, where it had not finished in 20 rounds; a per-minute quota makes the tick wait 40 s, where it waited 1 s, then translates), `ErrorClassifierTest` (+5: four quota cases paused before, two delay cases without a delay before), `TranslationEngineTest` (+2, three cases failing before), `ProvidersTest` (+2 rows; Test connection said "is limiting requests" for a daily quota), `ExceptionMappingTest` (+1, the SDK leaves the list body as sent). §A10, T4.12's classifier note, `07-spike-findings.md` "Exceptions", `docs/troubleshooting.md` and `docs/bulk-and-runs.md` updated. A live 429 is still to be seen (T13.4).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `Throttle` read a stored pause only when the cache gave back an integer, but Laravel's Redis store keeps numbers unserialised and returns them as strings (phpredis without a serializer, Laravel's default, and predis), so on a Redis cache `pausedFor()` was always 0: a 429 or an overload paused nothing, other lanes and the editor's parallel units kept calling the provider, a paused fallback provider still got units, and a shorter pause replaced a longer one, while `docs/configuration.md` promises a provider-wide pause and the docs recommend Redis for several servers. `Throttle` now reads any numeric value as an integer timestamp. Test: `ThrottleTest` "reads a pause back that the cache store returns as a numeric string, as the Redis store does" (a store that returns numbers as strings, as `RedisStore::unserialize()` does: the pause read 0 and a 5 s pause replaced a 30 s one before the fix).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): laravel/ai up to 1.2.0 maps every OpenAI `status: incomplete` to `FinishReason::Length`, so an answer the content filter stopped (`incomplete_details.reason: content_filter`) read as a truncation on the documented minimum SDK: each filtered segment, once its unit was split down to it, was sent again with a doubled output cap and then failed as `too_long` instead of `refused`, and a filtered review was skipped as `too_long`. `TranslationEngine` now checks for a refusal before `Length`, and its OpenAI refusal check also reads `incomplete_details.reason: content_filter` from the raw response, so translations and reviews report `refused` on every SDK from 1.0.1 (1.2.1 reports `ContentFilter` itself); the SDK floor stays `^1.0.1`. Tests: fixture `ProviderFixtures::openaiContentFilter()`; `TranslationEngineTest` and `ReviewEngineTest` "reports an openai … stopped by the content filter as refused, not as length" (both gave `length` before the fix); `FailureModesTest` "an answer the content filter stops" (one call and the warning `(refused)`, where two calls and `(too_long)` came before); `ResponseHandlingTest` holds the SDK's mapping, `Length` before 1.2.1 and `ContentFilter` from 1.2.1 (checked against 1.2.1's OpenAI parser).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (docs): the catalog docs promised `prompt_cache_options: {mode: explicit}` to "OpenAI's models from GPT-5.6 on" and showed a fine-tune of GPT-6 Luna as their example, but `StructuredAgent` adds it only to ids that start with `gpt-`, so the fine-tune `ft:gpt-6-luna:…` got none and OpenAI may write each of its requests to the cache at 1.25 times the input price. Whether fine-tunes accept the key is unchecked (gpt-5-mini refuses it with a 400), so the code still does not add it: `configuration.md` says only `gpt-` ids get it and that a fine-tune can have it in its own options, but leaves it out of the example and says to try it first by translating one entry in the editor with the fine-tune without saving, because OpenAI refuses an option a model does not accept with a 400, which `ErrorClassifier` reads as `request_rejected` for that request alone, so a run with a refused option does not abort but saves each field in the source language over its translation and ends Completed (seen with a fake 400 `invalid_parameter` in a run of mode `all`: `[el] Hello` became `Hello`); it says what a refusal looks like (the fields filled with the source text, the banner's "partly untranslated", the log entry "Tolk: the AI provider rejected a request." with OpenAI's message, "left untranslated (request_rejected)" in runs and the command line), where the first version of this fix put the option into the example and named a message that appears only in the log; `context-and-glossary.md` "How much context costs" points fine-tunes there, and §A9 and the `StructuredAgent` spec say so. `AgentsTest` pins it: the dataset of "switches off OpenAI's implicit prompt caching from GPT-5.6 on" has a fine-tune of GPT-6 Luna that gets none, and "sends OpenAI's cache mode to a fine-tuned model only from its catalog entry's options".
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `applyFields()` filled and localized every planned field, and core posts a field a condition hides without its value but with its handle in `_localized`, which `EntriesController` and `TermsController` store as `null`, so Translate over a hidden field saved an empty override with no warning (the field stopped inheriting, read manual, `not_saved` skipped it because it had an override, and Update left it alone); `docs/editor.md` promised a warning that never came. `applyFields()` now skips a field whose `hiddenFields` entry has `omitValue` (reason `hidden`, unless the payload syncs it) and records `localizedBefore` in `delivered`; the `*.saving` hook runs `leaveOutHidden()`, which takes a delivered field that a condition hides and that held no override before the fill out of `localizedFields` (core's request step reads that array after the hooks), so it keeps inheriting and `editor/confirm` lists it, and puts it back once shown after a failed save; the summary gains a `hidden` line (banner `data-tolk-summary-hidden`, the field action's toast), and the not-saved message says to show the field and translate it again, since the save response brings the origin's text back. A field that held an override before is stored empty by core, as without Tolk, and is documented. `EditorController`'s comment corrected. Tests: `fill.test.js` +3 (a hidden field skipped and left inheriting; a field still posted while hidden, and a sync, filled; `localizedBefore` carried across plans) and one record assertion, `saveHooks.test.js` +4 (taken out of `_localized`, left alone when localized before, pending or synced, put back after a failed save, untouched while the save is refused) and the message, `statuses.test.js` +1, `fieldActions.test.js` +1 case; `EditorEndpointsTest` +2 through core's update route with `_localized` as the form posts it (a hidden field left out is listed in `not_saved` and keeps inheriting; a handle in `_localized` without its value is stored as `null` and reads manual). `docs/editor.md`, `07-spike-findings.md` "Fields a condition hides", T5.4, T5.7 and T5.8 in place. Not checked in the browser.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): the `*.saved` hook sent every field delivered since the last save to `editor/confirm`, and a filled field the editor then put back to inheriting with core's sync button holds no override, which the server cannot tell from a field a condition hid, so the save showed a 10-second error toast and a banner line telling them to show the field and translate it again. `store.saved()` returns the delivered records, and the hook sends only those the save was meant to store (`fill.js` `savedFills()`: still in `localizedFields`, which core keeps after the save and posted as `_localized`, or marked `leftOut` by `leaveOutHidden()`), so a synced field, or a sync fill, is no longer listed and a hidden one still is; a form showing another item sends every handle as before. Tests: `saveHooks.test.js` +2 against a confirm that answers as the server does (a field synced before the save and a sync fill not sent, nothing listed; a hidden field left out still sent and listed), two confirm tests localize their fields, `store.test.js` expects the records. `docs/editor.md`, T5.4 and T5.8 (with its "Built in T5.5") in place. Not checked in the browser.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `registry.js` `textsOf()` left out a Bard text node's marks and joined adjacent runs, while the server reads a block's text with each formatted part in a numbered pair and each link title as a leaf of its own, so a word linked or made bold, formatting moved, or a link title changed in the form was no edit to `dirtyHandles()`, `skipHandles()`, `applyFields()`'s `edited` check or Undo: Update overwrote a link added to an outdated field, and Undo took back bold added after a fill without the "kept" note, although the same values saved read manual. A run with marks is now the list of its parts, split where the marks change as `Fingerprint::canonical()` reads them (keys sorted, empty attributes dropped, so Bard's `rel`/`target`/`title: null` still read the same), a formatted part `{ text, title }` with its link's non-blank title, and the title of a link on another node a text of its own; a link target changed or bold turned italic still read the same, as on the server. Tests: `registry.test.js` +2 (linked, bold, moved, split, heading made bold, title added or changed, an image's link title; and the guards: target, italic for bold, `null` and blank titles, nodes the re-emit joins, an empty text node, CRLF in a part) and two shape expectations, `fill.test.js` +2 (a linked word skipped as `edited`; bold and a link added after the fill kept by Undo), `translate.test.js` +1 (`skipHandles()` lists a linked word and a changed title), `StatusResolverTest` +1 pinning the server's side through `targetHash()`. `docs/statuses.md`, `docs/editor.md`, T6.11 and `07-spike-findings.md` ("The localized flag after a sync fill or Undo") in place. Not checked in the browser.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): a translation whose units all failed (a provider that stayed down past its five waits, say) ended in `done`, since only a stop ends in `error`, and `done` offered no Retry, Translate or Update, so the banner said "No fields were translated." with only Options, the field action or a save as ways out, while `docs/editor.md` said Retry repeats a translation that failed. `translate.js` `retryable()` offers Retry in `error` and in `done` while a field of `summary.failed` is not filled yet (Cancel alone offers none), the banner shows Retry by it, and `describeSummary(…, { retry })` adds "Retry translates them again." (or "reviews") to the failed line while Retry, not Start again, is offered; the field action's toast is unchanged. Tests: `translate.test.js` +3 (`retryable()` true and false cases; with the real runner, 503s until the unit fails end in `done` with both fields failed and Retry plans those two again), `statuses.test.js` +1 (the hint, singular, plural, review, and none without `retry`), `components/EditorBanner.test.js` (new: Retry in `done` and `error`, Start again, Undo alone, nothing while working). `docs/editor.md` and T5.8 in place. Not checked in the browser.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): the options menu's Model and Reviewer selects were bound with `v-model`, and for users who may edit the settings they are taggable, so core's `Combobox` committed text typed only to search the list as the model when the input lost focus (a click on Translate) or on Enter: typing "son" and pressing Translate posted `son`, which `editor/plan` refused with a 422, stored it as `tolk.editor.translator`, and Options preselected it on every later open. `options.js` `pickedModel()` keeps of a commit only a model the select offers or text typed as `provider:model` (`modelSelect.js` `isReference()`, the guard of the settings' model fields), else the model as it was, and both selects take their choice through it; `rememberedModel()` ignores a stored value that is not `provider:model`, and choosing the default replaces it with null. Tests: `components/TranslateOptions.test.js` (new: both selects keep the model for committed search text and take a listed or typed model), `options.test.js` +4 (`pickedModel()`, a stored value that names no model ignored, and replaced when the default is chosen). `docs/editor.md` and T5.10 in place. Not checked in the browser.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): creating a run held several copies of every entry of the request until `RunPlanner::build()` returned: `EntryFamilies::roots()` loaded every root first, `entryTargets()` kept root, source and an `EntryTarget` per target, each target found its source again with `Entry::find()` (a fresh copy, and a filter over the whole id index), and core's Blink kept each family's localizations and origins, so an Overview cell or site button, the listing action, `tolk:translate --collection` or `tolk:site` over a few hundred long, translated entries exhausted a 128M `memory_limit` and no run was created. `EntryFamilies::roots()` is now a generator over `rootIds()` (index-ordered ids) that loads `CHUNK` (50) roots per query and forgets the Blink keys core added for the chunk's entries before loading the next (keys memoized before are kept); the planner builds targets with `forLoadedEntry()` (which now takes the assumed sites), keeps only scalars per target and resolves dependencies from them; `RunsController::store()` raises the time limit to 120 s. Measured in the suite with 18 KB entries localized into one site: 300 roots into two sites peaked 38.6 MB above the baseline before and 4.4 MB after, and 1,000 roots into two sites 5.6 MB (1,081 ms before for 300, 330 ms after). The scan that `tolk:translate` and `tolk:site` run in the same process after creating the run (`RunPlanner::estimate()` over every item, unless `--no-estimate`) still kept core's memo of each item's localizations, origin and blueprint, about four keys per family, so the CLI paths ran out of memory one step later, and left the run they had created pending: the memo's mark and forget moved out of `EntryFamilies` into `Support\EntryMemo`, and `estimate()` forgets after each item what was memoized while it was planned. Measured with 400 families of 40-paragraph Bard bodies localized into one site, a `--mode=update` dry run into two sites: with 100 MB of headroom it died with "Allowed memory size exhausted" before and peaked 38.3 MB above the baseline after; with bodies three times longer it held 341 MB after the scan before and under 1 MB after, its peak (87 MB) now that of building the run, as with `--no-estimate`, and the scan a few percent slower. `EntryFamiliesTest` +3 (the second chunk is read after the caller reached it, the chunk's Blink keys are gone when the next is read while an earlier key stays, `rootIds()` order), `RunPlannerTest` +3 (each family's targets are authorized on the one root it loaded, at most one entry of the first chunk, which a blueprint's shared fields keep, is alive while the second chunk is planned against 100 before, and a guard that passed before: a page of the second chunk waits for the root and parent items of the first), `TargetFactoryTest` +1 (assumed sites), `RunsControllerTest` +1 (time limit), and for the scan `RunPlannerTest` +1 (no entry of the items planned before is alive while the next is planned, against up to 22 entries over 24 items before, and a key memoized before stays) and `TranslateCommandTest` +1 (a dry run leaves no entry memo key in Blink, against four per family before); the existing `roots()` tests read the generator.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): placing a new localization in its tree resolved the family of every page at its level, each with an `Entry::find()` that filters the id index of every collection and an `origin()` query after the writer cleared Blink, inside the write lock, and an orderable collection holds every entry at one level, so each apply into it grew with the collection. `EntryTarget::moved()` now walks the origin siblings nearest first, finds each within the collection and its family's page in the target site with `in()`, and stops at the first one at the level. Measured in the suite on an orderable collection of 600 entries, 300 of them in Greek: a page placed after its localized neighbour 183 ms per apply before and 20–24 ms after, one whose nearest localized sibling is 148 places back 221 ms before and 95 ms after, one with 594 unlocalized siblings before it 273 ms before and 234 ms after; a page inserted before 150 others still takes 169 ms (289 before), most of it core's `UpdateStructuredEntryOrderAndParent`, which reads every moved page with `Entry::find()` to reindex its order. `EntryApplyTest` +2 (an apply into an orderable level of 40 makes as many queries as one into a level of 6, 58 against 24 before, with both placed in order; and a guard that passed before: pages translated into `de` from Greek localizations land among siblings translated from English).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): with revisions on, `EntryTarget::saveChanges()` saved the working-copy view directly whenever that view said unpublished, as core's `EntriesController::update()` decides, but restoring a published localization's `unpublish` or creation revision through core's restore route leaves a working copy that says unpublished, so the next run or automatic update unpublished the live page and kept the stale working copy, which every later run read and translated again. Only a draft with no working copy is now saved directly; a published localization, or any localization with a working copy, gets its fields in the working copy, the view its values are read from, and the live entry is left alone. Tests: `RevisionsTest` "stays live when an editor restored a revision saved while it was unpublished, and is updated in that working copy" (through core's restore route, then two runs: the live entry stays published and unchanged, the working copy holds the translation, the second run makes no model call), which failed on the live entry's published flag, and "updates the working copy of an unpublished localization that has one", which failed on the live entry's data. `docs/bulk-and-runs.md` and `docs/cli.md` (Revisions), §A11 "Revisions" and `docs/research/07-spike-findings.md` "Revisions (T9.1)" updated.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): `AppliesFields::writable()` dropped the `target_changed` check whenever `overwrite_manual` was set, but a reviewed field's value is the target's text when the plan was made plus the corrections, so a review with **Overwrite manual edits** (`tolk:translate --mode=review --force`, the listing's review with the toggle, `tolk:lang`/`tolk:forms --mode=review --force`) wrote the old text back over an edit saved while its units ran and recorded it as current. `Pipeline::reviewed()` sets the new `AssembledField::$fromTarget`, and `writable()` skips such a field as `target_changed` whatever `overwrite_manual` says (§A11 step 2, T4.16 step 3); a translated field is still overwritten. Tests: `ReviewPipelineTest` "keeps a field a human edited while the reviewer worked, with overwrite_manual too" (a machine and a manual field, both kept and still manual) and `RunApplyTest` "keeps a field a human edited meanwhile in a review run that overwrites manual edits".
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): reconcile rule 1 dropped every pending entry but the promoted one, so a delivery another save overtook (a form left open while a run, a push, an automatic update or a second tab saved the same field) read **edited by hand** once that form was saved untouched: with the origin corrected meanwhile, a translation of the old text read manual with the item current, so nothing updated it again. `FieldState::promote()` now moves the other entries' outputs to `history`, newest first and ahead of the previous machine pair, leaving out a reviewed manual field's entry (`baseline: true`), so such a save reads **outdated** and Update translates it from the current origin (§A5 rule 1, §A6). Decided: outdated rather than keeping the overtaken entries pending, which would have needed them exempt from rule 2 to keep a hand edit of the promoted text from taking an older entry's source. Tests: `StateObjectsTest` (two existing tests now expect the history, +3: a stale save reads as older machine output, three outputs kept, a reviewed manual field's output left out) and `EditorDeliveryTest` +2 (a stale form saved after a server-side translation of the changed origin, and the second of two forms, both outdated).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): a run item whose apply threw after its save (an `EntrySaved` listener of the host that failed once) was retried with the same plan, and the retry's `AppliesFields::writable()` read the run's own output as somebody's edit, because the target no longer held `target_before`: the item ended `skipped` with `target_changed` and "edited by someone", no `TargetTranslated` fired and the run's counts were wrong; and when the attempt threw between the empty creation save and the content save, the retry treated the draft it had created as an existing localization, so a run with **New localizations** set to mirror left it a draft and a collection of `translated_slugs` kept the origin's slug. `writable()` now treats a target that already holds what the field writes (`AssembledField::$targetHash`, null for a removal) as unchanged, so the retry writes it again, also when the editor's confirm promoted the pending entries meanwhile; `RunProcessor::applyPlan()` records `created` on the item when the attempt that threw created the localization, keeps it when the item is applied, and passes it, for a retry of the plan made before the localization existed, as the new `ApplyOptions::$createdEarlier`, with which `EntryTarget` decides the published state and slug as for a new localization, but only where nobody changed them between the attempts (the same plan serves a retry 5 or 10 s later, and up to `plan_ttl` later after a closed page, a pause or a stalled run resumed): it translates the slug only while it is still the origin's or the translated one, with or without a suffix of `-2`, `-3`, …, so a slug an editor gave the draft is kept (D16), and `mirror` never unpublishes it, so a draft an editor published while its origin is not stays published (D10); a narrower "only while unpublished" would have left a working copy beside a localization with revisions that the attempt published before it threw (§A11 step 2, "Slug", "Published", `ApplyOptions`; §A12 "Applying"; T4.16 steps 3 and 6 and its `ApplyOptions`; docs/bulk-and-runs.md). Tests: RunApplyTest (a retried item ends `done` with `TargetTranslated` once and reads current; a localization whose creation save threw is published as its origin with the translated slug on the retry; the creation's suffixed slug is still translated; the slug, its warning and the published state an attempt saved before it threw are kept; a slug an editor gave the draft between the attempts is kept; a draft an editor published, with `mirror` and an unpublished origin, stays published; after **Retry failed** the localization the failed attempts created keeps the slug an editor gave it), EntryApplyTest (the same fields applied again after a throw after save, with and without a confirm in between; a removed field removed again; with revisions, a localization the attempt published before it threw is published again without a working copy).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): when a server-side Update removed a translation because the origin field was emptied, `AppliesFields::confirmWrites()` forgot the field's record with `StateWriter::forget()`, history included, while the editor's own removal keeps it; so a form left open before the removal or a restored revision that put the removed output back read manual and untracked, and no later Update removed it. The record now keeps only its history (new `StateWriter::recordRemoved()` and `FieldState::removed()`), which takes in the machine pair and the outputs of the pending entries (not a reviewed manual text's), so such a save reads outdated and Update removes it again; it still reads not applicable while the source is empty and untranslated once it has text again (§A5, §A6 rule 1, §A7, T4.16 step 5, T9.2's built note; docs/statuses.md). Tests: EntryApplyTest (the removal keeps only the history, now also for the retried removal; a removed output saved again reads outdated and is removed again; a delivery the removal overtook reads outdated when saved, a reviewed manual text manual), TermTargetTest, GlobalsTargetTest and NavTargetTest (the removal keeps only the history), StateObjectsTest (`removed()`), StateWriterTest (`recordRemoved()`).
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): an item whose step killed its process (memory_limit, max_execution_time, a queue job's timeout) went back to the same worker id, which the repository hands its own item with a fresh claim and no attempt, so a queue lane (`queue-{lane}`), the run page's apply loop (`browser-{user}-apply`) or a tick lane retried it forever: the run never ended, the write lock was held for about 120 s a cycle and a death after a paid call paid again. `RunProcessor::work()` and `applyNext()` now keep a mark in the cache while they hold the item lock (`tolk:item-step:{run}:{index}`, the item's attempts and the lock's owner, kept `runs.retention_days`) and remove it when the step ends; a step that finds one left behind counts an attempt and fails the item at the third with "No worker finished this item in 3 attempts.", as troubleshooting.md promised. A mark with fewer attempts than the item was already counted by the takeover of its expired claim, one with more is from before a Retry, and a step that outlived its lock leaves a later step's mark alone. Kept in `RunProcessor` rather than the repository, so a step costs two cache writes and no run-file writes. New test helper `dieWorkingElsewhere()` (`OtherProcess` job `die-working`: the step ends the process with `exit()`, so no finally block runs and its locks stay). `RunTranslateTest` +3 (three deaths of `lane-1` fail the item; a takeover by another worker counts a death once; a death from before a Retry is not counted; the last two guards passed before), `RunApplyTest` +1 (three apply deaths of `apply-1`), `RunsControllerTest` +1 (three deaths of the user's apply loop, then `POST apply` fails the item and applies the others); troubleshooting.md and §A12 "Claims".
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): the browser worker ids hold a nonce made once per page load, and the editor's push follower keeps driving its run across CP visits, so opening a pushed run's page without a reload started a second set of lanes with the same worker ids (a loop that `follow()` restarted did the same while its tick was in flight). The server gives a worker its own item back with a new claim, so each driver's tick took the other's claim and its saves failed with `ClaimLost`: the item's usage was lost (`TargetTranslated` got no models), and its waits and attempts too, so a provider that kept timing out was called every second and the item never failed. `ticker.js` now sends a lane's tick only once the tick before of its worker id in the page has its answer, whichever loop sent it (kept per run and per api), and a lane stopped while it waited sends nothing; the run page lends the run from the push while it shows it (`pushes.lend()`: the push's follower stops, a status that names the run meanwhile does not start it, and it goes on from the page's last state when the page closes), so a page drives a run once. Fixed in the browser, not the server: a worker's own item still comes back with a new claim, so a step that outlived its item lock cannot save over the step after it. `ticker.test.js` +3 (two drivers of one run in one page, a restarted lane behind the stopped loop's tick, a lane stopped while it waited), `push.test.js` +3 (`lend()` and its give-back, no drive while lent, nothing to give back for a run no push follows); §A12 `browser` and T8.13's note on `push.js`. Not checked in the browser: the sandbox holds the owner's uncommitted work.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): the run page asked before leaving only on `beforeunload`, so a visit inside the CP (the sidebar, "All runs") or Back unmounted it without a word and stopped the lanes of a browser-driven run, which then paused with the runs list still saying Running, while the docs said the page warns before you leave it. `createFollower({ dirty })` now holds the page's name in core's dirty state while it drives a running browser run (`follower.driving`: the last state is running on the browser driver and a lane runs or the apply loop has not finished), so the CP asks as for unsaved changes before a visit, Back or closing the tab (unless the user's Confirm Dirty Navigation preference is off), and drops it as soon as the run is cancelled or final, the loops end or the page stops; the page's own `beforeunload` listener asks on the same condition. A run the editor's push of the same page load follows goes on after a visit (`pushes.lend()`), so its page holds no name and keeps "Keep this tab open"; the others say "Keep this page open while the run is in progress. Leaving it pauses the run until you open it again." `follow.test.js` +4 (held while driving until the lanes and the apply loop end; dropped on cancel and on a completed state while the loops still end, held again after Resume; dropped on stop and when the lanes end after the apply loop gave up; nothing held for a pending, queue, CLI or finished run or without a name), `Show.test.js` +2 (the two notices); `docs/bulk-and-runs.md` "Keep this page open", `docs/troubleshooting.md`, T8.9 (the browser driver, and the Built note on leaving the page, which still said the "keep this tab open" notice covered every run), T13.1's list of the docs, and `07-spike-findings.md` "Leaving a page through core's dirty state". Not checked in the browser: the sandbox holds the owner's uncommitted work.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): the run page's follower started its loops only in `follow()`, and a state that `refresh()` read afterwards (after the estimate, or for another page of items or a filter) or that the polling passed on was only shown: two editors open one pending run and one presses Start, and the other's page, once its estimate finished, showed Running at 0% with "Keep this page open" but neither drove nor polled the run, as it did for a run resumed elsewhere after it was cancelled or finished, or a queue run another page took over. `createFollower()` now follows a passed-on state whose run needs other loops than the followed one (`loopsFor()`: the estimate, the lanes and the apply loop, polling, or none), keeping the `latest()` guard, so a state older than the one shown starts nothing; a final state needs none, and the loops end on it with the counts of what they finished, as after Cancel. `follow.test.js` +6 (a pending run started elsewhere driven after the estimate; started, resumed or taken over elsewhere seen on another page of items, for each pair of loops; a run cancelled while driven and resumed elsewhere; a polled queue run taken over; the same loops kept, also after a lane gave up, and left to end on a final state; nothing for a running state older than a completed one), and the page-of-items test now reads a completed run; `docs/bulk-and-runs.md` "Keep this page open" and T8.9's Built note. A pending page whose estimate has finished still notices a start elsewhere only when it reads the run again. Not checked in the browser: the sandbox holds the owner's uncommitted work.
Review fix 2026-10-14 (reproduced by failing tests first): a push or automatic update on a queue that no worker takes up (`QUEUE_CONNECTION=database` without `queue:work`) showed "Translating into …" at 0% with Cancel on every visit, forever: `describePush()` ignored the state's `stalled`, and the take-over, like Resume, needed `run bulk translations`, which the push's creator may not have, so the banner neither said so nor offered a way out. The `take-over` route now takes `RunFollowRequest` (the run's creator with `translate content`, or `run bulk translations`) and `EditorPush::URLS` adds `take_over`; `describePush()` gives `stalled` (the sentence to show, for a running run only) and `takeOver` (a queue run) from the run's own driver, which the keep-open note now reads too, so it follows a run taken over; the banner shows the warning with **Translate in this page instead** (`pushes.takeOver()`, through the follower, which then drives the run) for a queue run, and for an automatic update whose saving process went away the advice to cancel it and start again. Resume still needs `run bulk translations`. `EditorPushTest` +1 (the creator moves a stalled queue push to the page and finishes it there; the push's URLs carry `take_over`), `RunsControllerTest` (the creator may take over but not resume; anyone else may not take over), `push.test.js` +4 (the sentence per driver and none for a moving, unread or final run; the keep-open note after a take-over; `takeOver()` once at a time; a failed take-over); `RoutesTest`'s note, §A14, T8.7, T8.13, `docs/editor.md`, `installation.md` and `troubleshooting.md`. Not checked in the browser: the sandbox holds the owner's uncommitted work.
